# Work packages for phases 2 and 3 (evaluation; compositor, effects and colour)

Planning only. Built on the parity matrix (parity matrix (origin/main 392d449); schema/diagnostics adoption is Bob's branch phase1-contract). Phase 2 = EVAL rows, phase 3 = COMP and FX rows. Machine-readable twin: [WORKPACKAGES-P2-P3.json](WORKPACKAGES-P2-P3.json). Acceptance gates use the render-parity harness (`tools/parity/render_parity.py`, branch `parity-render`), Rust tests whose expectations can be ported to pytest, and `eval`-level comparisons where pixels are too indirect. A NEW scene is a probe the package must author under `tools/parity/scenes/`; the Rust binary renders its reference.

### Parallel tracks

Phase 2 (EVAL) has three tracks (P2-A, P2-B, P2-C) and phase 3 (COMP + FX) three tracks (P3-A, P3-B, P3-C, each a COMP lane paired with the FX lane that shares the fewest files with it). Each track is one agent's work; within a track follow `depends_on`. Sizes: S=0.5, M=2, L=5 days.

### Dependency on Bob's phase1-contract (schema, diagnostics, native checks)

Bob's branch `phase1-contract` vendors the Rust XSD/Schematron exactly (1.1.3 + 1.2 + 1.3), ports the diagnostics and the native checks, and changes `scenerender/document.py` and `schema.py`. Packages that edit those files (P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-04, P2-EVAL-05, P2-EVAL-06, P2-EVAL-07, P2-EVAL-08, P2-EVAL-09, P2-EVAL-10, P2-EVAL-24) should start after phase1-contract lands (or rebase onto it before their first commit); packages that need new schema attributes or elements (`needs_schema_adoption`) cannot start before it. Evaluation diagnostics E01-E18 (P2-EVAL-02) are separate from Bob's validation codes S/V/C/R/A/P; keep one registry each and share only the diagnostic record format.

### Area notes (ordering and shared infrastructure)

**EVAL.** ORDER. (1) Build the measurement spine first, in group C: P2-EVAL-00 (tools/parity/eval_parity.py: runs `scene-render eval FILE -t T --format json --no-assets [--variant --param k=v --layout --row --data]`, canonicalises, diffs against the Python dump, also an expression-table mode and a sidecar-args extension of render_parity.py), P2-EVAL-01 (scenerender/framegraph.py: a Python FrameGraph that walks the SAME RenderContext paths as render_children, so every divergence it shows is real) and P2-EVAL-02 (E01-E18 diagnostics registry, which A/B packages raise through). P2-EVAL-03 (CLI eval/explain/--row) can follow. After that groups A, B, C run independently; inside a group follow the id order. Cross-group edges: P2-EVAL-12 needs P2-EVAL-04/05 (typed param values) and P2-EVAL-21 (anim.eval_loop); P2-EVAL-13/14/15/16/17/18/19 need 00-02 only.
RUST CLI FACTS (verified with /home/admin/src/rs-scene-render/target/release/scene-render, binary exists). `eval FILE -t T|-f N --format json` prints ONE JSON object but ALL output, including E16 warnings and diagnostics, goes to stdout and the warnings come first: parse from the first line that is exactly `{`. Keys: time, frame, size, background, nodes[], transitions[], camera (index or null), elements[], problems[]. Node keys: id (scoped: `inst/inner`, `list[2]/label`, `inc/mark`), kind (`copy` for repeat copies, `repeat`, `include`, element names), parent (index; absent for roots), depth, local/world (affine [a,b,c,d,e,f], x'=a*x+c*y+e, y'=b*x+d*y+f), opacity, world_opacity, timeline_time, local_time (= timeline_time - node start), source_time, asset, text, draw, matte, is_matte, repeat [k,n], size, anchor, content{dest,uv,blur_fill}, clip, props. props: Length serialises as a string ('32', '50%'), Pair as ['50','50%'], numbers/colours as JSON numbers/arrays. Errors: non-zero exit, `error[E03]: ...` blocks with `-->` location and `= at <id>`; diagnostics for E-codes can be compared as a set of codes. Only ACTIVE nodes are listed (transition handles keep both sides of a transition visible). Validation (S/C/R/P rules, e.g. C16 fit needs box, C38 decreasing key times, R21 marker refs, P03 self-instance) fires BEFORE eval: those belong to Bob, so negative eval scenes must avoid them.
FINDINGS THAT CHANGE SCOPE (all measured, not guessed): Python already matches Rust on sequence/instance/group clocks, flex row/column/stack/space-*, 39 of 41 curves (only cubic-bezier default handles and spring differ), noise/wiggle/random golden values, Math.* to within 2.4e-16 relative (so EVAL-40 is deferred), motion paths to 0.006 px, and most fit modes. Real gaps: marker/@start composition, beat.N numbering, sequence marker pinning, @parent cycles (RecursionError), `include @symbol` (always raises 'symbol not found': the symbols section is moved before the lookup in document._expand_includes, no test covers it), self-instance and 300^3 repeats (RecursionError / unbounded expansion), a 30-deep shared prop() chain (Python hangs, exponential; Rust 2 ms), if/else/return/compound assignment/?. syntax, fps/duration vars, vector helpers, Math.random, ease/easeIn/easeOut, loopOut(kind,n) inside the key span, linear extrapolation, non-uniform splines, anchor/skew aliases, normalized time base on open-ended nodes, negative speed, back-out clamp and z rounding, link marker/smoothing semantics, grid layout and content-hugging groups, safe-area preset table, fit/focus for plain fit and fit-blur darkening.
PROBE SCENES. I authored and ran these against the Rust binary (all load and eval cleanly) and kept them committed under docs/parity/probes/eval/ (copy into tools/parity/scenes/eval/ when P2-EVAL-00 lands): docs/parity/probes/eval/*.xml (m1 seq1 seq2 seq3 media neg rep1 rep2 rep3 inst1 inc1+lib kf1 kf2 al1 lk1 chain mp1 lay1 lay2 lay3 lay4 fit1 sa_* par1 par2 prm1 cyc err2 big1 curves41 tr1) plus prototypes of the harness pieces: rs.py (runs eval and strips the diagnostics prefix), pydump.py/pyworld.py (crude Python dumps), exprdiff.py + exprs1-3.txt (the expression table mode, 190 expressions with measured RS/PY verdicts), propdiff.py. Scene prelude used below: P = `<scene version='1.1'><project width='1000' height='500' fps='10' duration='10' seed='1'/>`, A = `<assets><image id='img' src='img.png' width='100' height='50'/><video id='vid' src='clip.mp4' width='100' height='50' fps='10' duration='4'/></assets>`; section order is project, parameters, layouts, safeAreas, assets, symbols, markers, composition; always run eval with --no-assets and keep attributes single-quoted. Eval-level times are seconds; the `times` arrays below are fractions of the 10 s duration.
GATE TOLERANCES. Eval-level: ids/kinds/order/parent/depth/repeat/draw/matte/text exact; times, opacity 1e-9; affines 1e-6 (arc paths 5e-2, motion-path orientation 1e-4); numbers in props 1e-9 (1e-6 for splines/springs). cairo vs wgpu only matters for the few pixel gates (layout, fit, safe-area, reframe) where MATCH is PSNR>=45 as in render_parity.
CROSS-AREA. P2-EVAL-19 supplies the seed primitive (hash_str) but emitter/physics/deform seeds stay with the SIM/FX areas (tests/test_particle_evaluation.py:312-317 pins CRC-32 emitter seeds). vexpr.py is a second vectorised parser used for object3D @instances: new syntax must raise its _Unsupported so it falls back. assets/lottie.py calls expr.builtin_functions: keep its signature.

**COMP.** Ordering. Build P3-COMP-01 first (probe scenes + fixture generator + runner defaults + shared reference helpers). It is the only package every other one leans on. All probe scenes listed below were prototyped and rendered with the existing Rust binary on this machine (Intel Arc, Vulkan) and with the current Python tree; the measured PSNR figures quoted as current_expected_status are real measurements (scratch copies live in /tmp/claude-1000/-home-admin-codebases-py-render/79c66742-8ab0-4941-b43a-ababfc401a00/scratchpad/p/*.scene.xml, helper cmp.py there). A throwaway patch of is_isolated/_passthrough in a scratch copy (scratchpad/exp) took iso_opacity from 26.4 to 99.0 dB, so P3-COMP-03 is small and low-risk. Group A (compositor.py/nodes/core.py/camera.py/gpucomp.py/registry.py/cli.py/gl.py): 03 -> 04 -> 05 -> 20 -> 22 -> 21 -> 23. Group B (blend.py/masks.py/paint.py/assets/generator.py/render.py/output.py): 02, 09 -> 10, 12 -> 13, 11, 14 -> 15 (15 also needs 16). Group C (color.py/raster.py/assets/image_*.py/tools/parity): 01 first, then 06, 07, 16, 17, 18 -> 19, and 08 last because it touches compositor.py (one RenderContext line), output.py and the GPU 'linear' flag. Cross-group edits are therefore expressed as depends_on rather than shared files. Key findings the matrix did not record: (1) add/linear-dodge in Rust use fixed-function One/One for colour but over-alpha for alpha (render.rs pipeline 'additive'); Python also sums alpha (29 dB on blend_add_alpha probe). (2) Python's raster cache is deliberately approximate for moving placements (docs/RUNTIME.md; max diff 152/255 in a sequence render vs fresh render); the parity harness must run with SCENERENDER_RASTER_CACHE=0 or numbers are unrepresentative. (3) Rust generators are evaluated at the asset's own pixel size and then sampled as a texture, so a generator drawn at scale != 1 differs in Python by texture magnification, not only by sampling error. (4) A non-isolated threeD group in Rust silently drops its own 2.5D transform and `collapse` is never read by the renderer (see rust_may_be_wrong); copying that would remove documented Python behaviour, so P3-COMP-05 is a decision package. (5) The Rust schema default colorProfile='embedded' means ICC profiles apply to every image by default; Python's XSD lacks the attribute (schema adoption by Bob first). (6) Only HLG differs among the 17 transfer curves (ramp probe: all others within 1 code value), only xyz-d65 among working spaces, only radial gradients with fr>0 / focal on the end circle, animated stop crossing, mask radius/feather, add family, 3 isolation cases, depth sort, background paint, generator contrast/pixel-rounded patterns, mips and ICC differ in pixels. Mesh, dither, blend-mode formulas, and every other generator/gradient kind already agree (blend_grid 57.7 dB MATCH, mesh 41 dB, dither 53.7 dB). Realistic thresholds: flat-colour/blend/colour scenes MATCH >= 45 dB; masks, projected 2.5D quads, generators with grid sampling, gradients with exact stop evaluation CLOSE >= 40 (cairo 8-bit coverage and its AA versus the shader's analytic AA caps edges near 44 dB: mask_sdf control scene measured 44.3 dB with identical geometry). Gates use harness classification (PSNR >= 45 and <0.5% pixels off by >2 = MATCH) on 8-bit PNGs from `scene-render render -t T` versus `scenerender still -t T --lenient`; all probe scenes use an OPAQUE project background because Rust PNG export of translucent frames and Python's flattening differ in RGB. No other agent may edit the Rust tree; probe fixtures must be generated by tools/parity/make_fixtures.py (copies of common.rs fixtures plus ramp.png, hf.png, and the sr-media still fixtures p3.png/adobe.png/srgb-icc.png/gray.png read from /home/admin/src/rs-scene-render/crates/sr-media/tests/fixtures/still).

**FX.** ORDERING. (1) Day 0, group A: P3-FX-01 (probe generator, test card, per-member gate runner, Rust-fixture helpers), P3-FX-02 (Params node-space API: px = max singular value of the node's frame-space 2x2 incl. render scale, lin, dirv, offset) and P3-FX-05 (edgeMode core). Group C packages need 02 for node-local units and rotated vectors, and 04 (effect target rectangle) for anything anchored to the Rust effect target: pixel grids, noise draws, phase of wave/ripple, uv-based overlays and the whole-frame targets of UNBOUNDED effects (letterbox, vignette, fractal-noise, lens-flare, light-leak, light-sweep, god-rays, radial/zoom blur, twirl, spherize, bulge, lens-distortion, mirror, kaleidoscope, tile, displacement-map). (2) Group B (colour, light, shadow, keying, finishing) can start immediately: per-pixel colour packages need only 01. (3) P3-FX-04 is the riskiest (touches apply_effects/finish_node and the effect cache keys); keep the Params fallback for node=None so unit tests that call rc.apply_effects(ids, buf, ctx) on bare tiles keep working.

SHARED INFRASTRUCTURE. Probe template T1: project 128x128 fps=10 duration=1 background=#303030; asset tc = tools/parity/scenes/assets/fx-testcard.png (96x96 RGBA: 6 saturated patches, 6 skin/pastel/white/black patches, 0-255 grey ramp, hue ramp, anti-aliased disc, half-alpha square, 4 px white bar; generated deterministically with PIL); body <layer id='n' asset='tc' x='16' y='16' effects='e'/>; one <effect id='e' .../> in <effects>; time fraction 0.5 (t=0.5, frame 5). T2: same but <layer id='bg' asset='tc' x='16' y='16'/><adjustment id='n' effects='e'/>, used when Rust's effect target is the whole frame. Use an image test card, not text, so fonts do not enter the comparison. One generated scene per (member, variant); the family is the generator table row group; fx_gate.py prints one row per member with PSNR / %>2 levels and checks floors. I built and ran a prototype of exactly this on 2026-10-04 (rs-scene-render 0.1.2 vs wt-matrix): /tmp/claude-1000/-home-admin-codebases-py-render/79c66742-8ab0-4941-b43a-ababfc401a00/scratchpad/fxp/gen2.py with res2.json, plus mb*.py, ord*.py, edm.py, fin.py, tm.py, tr.py, off.py, fx02.py. Every 'current_expected_status' below is measured there (PSNR in dB, whole 128x128 frame). The rs binary has no 'still'; use 'render FILE -t T -o PNG'. 'scene-render eval --format json' prints nodes[].world/size/opacity/local_time/props, transitions[] and elements[].props (evaluated animated effect parameters, e.g. radius); it prints no pixels and no static effect attributes, so eval_level gates exist only for FX-02, FX-04, FX-06 and animated parameters.

DECISION REGISTER (coordinator): Python has many deliberate supersets of Rust (D9 color-grade, kelvin white balance, exact AgX/ACES2, CMYK selective-color, 8-type lighting, bevel styles, FFT lens blur, optical-flow-free rigid pixel-motion-blur, echo operators, camera shutterAngle). Packages implement the Rust meaning of the shared schema attributes as the default (same document, same pixels) and keep the Python model reachable only through Python-only <param name=...> children or a documented opt-in, because Rust ignores unknown params. Items that need an explicit user decision before implementing: color-grade D9 vs Rust (07), tone-mapper accuracy vs Rust fits (09), look@space for CDL (10), lighting model and light coordinate space (15), echo ordering/normalisation (25), motion-blur edge-of-timeline behaviour and camera shutterAngle (06). See rust_may_be_wrong.

MATRIX CORRECTIONS FOUND WHILE PROBING. FX-28: Rust echo is not 'over only': it accumulates equal/decaying weights normalised to sum 1 (render_fx.rs L1013-1022; the Rust test echoed_layers_keep_their_effect_targets_to_the_trail needs alpha 1/3 at x=94). FX-38: README Limitations bullet 'blend modes inside samples composite against transparency' is stale; the code blends the accumulation (draw_accumulated) and tests/motion_blur.rs requires >=40 dB against the average of sharp frames, which is exactly Python's whole-frame supersampling, so no per-node accumulation rewrite is needed. FX-39: shutterAngle<=0 is verified DIFFERENT (Python renders at t+shutterPhase, 26.5 dB; Rust draws at t). FX-16 (grain/noise/glitch) is PRESENT only when the effect target is the whole frame (adjustment: 70.6/51.1/72.6 dB); on layers they measure 35.9/21.2/14.7 dB because Rust indexes the hash by pixel position inside its effect target (P3-FX-04). FX-12: sepia/grayscale/invert 'amount' are ignored or mixed in the wrong space in Python (sepia amount=0.7: 30 dB). FX-07: glow measures 39.8 dB, not a MATCH (Dual-Kawase vs Gaussian plus alpha combine). FX-21: chromatic-aberration is PRESENT in formula but 17.5 dB on full-frame content with edgeMode (Python ignores it) and 53 dB at layer targets (content box).

OUTSIDE THIS AREA (not packaged): (a) a hidden node (visible='false') used as a matte is empty in Python but works in Rust (tests/effects.rs::transition_doc relies on it): matte-with-invisible-node probe 15.5 dB, luma transition with hidden matte 14 dB; belongs to COMP/transition matte handling. (b) transition morph measures 15.8-24.4 dB (Rust documents luminance-difference displacement; Python uses optical flow): FX-35 row says PRESENT, probably DIVERGENT-by-design. (c) all other 33 transition types measure 59-99 dB at p=0.2/0.5/0.8, confirming FX-31..FX-37 PRESENT. (d) image downscaling baseline: a 0.8-scaled layer already differs 35 dB with no effect (COMP resampling filter), so probes use scale 1 or flat colours. (e) fractional-edge vector shapes differ ~38 dB (cairo vs wgpu AA), so motion-blur probes with moving shapes are CLOSE, not MATCH.

NUMERIC LIMITS used for floors: per-pixel colour ops with identical formulas reach 50-80 dB (8-bit PNG, half-float intermediates); Dual-Kawase vs separable Gaussian costs about 10-15 dB (drop-shadow baseline 53 dB, glow 40 dB) unless Kawase is ported; hash/fbm effects reach 70 dB when target alignment and the D24 draw index agree; optical flow (pixel-motion-blur) can only be CLOSE.

### Rust behaviour that may be wrong (flag to the user, do not copy)

| Row | Observation | Rust evidence | Why questionable |
|---|---|---|---|
| EVAL-42 | beat.N / bar.N are 0-based (beat.0 = offset, bar.1 = one bar after the offset); Python and its tests use 1-based DAW numbering (beat.1 = offset). The XSD text only says 'generated ids beat.N and bar.N' without defining N. | crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::marker_time (match kind { 'beat' => offset + n*beat, 'bar' => offset + n*beat*per_bar }); test comment in crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::markers_place_nodes_and_keys 'bar.1 = 1 + 4 x 0.5 = 3'; Python tests/test_core.py:95-97 | A spec ambiguity rather than a bug, but the choice silently shifts every beat-snapped key by one beat. Users expect 'bar.1' to be the first bar in musical notation. Needs a user decision before P2-EVAL-09 flips Python. |
| EVAL-08 | Repeat opacity multiplies the repeat's own opacity by max(0, 1-k*opacityStep); the XSD text for repeatType says copy i receives 'opacity - i*opacityStep' (subtract). With opacity=0.8, step=0.25: Rust copy 2 has world_opacity 0.4, the documented rule gives 0.3. Python implements the documented rule and pins it in a test. | crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs:1608 `(1.0 - kf * r.opacity_step.get()).max(0.0)` applied to the copy while the repeat node keeps its own opacity (copy.tf reset at ~1590); schema/scene-render-1.1.xsd repeatType documentation 'opacity - i*opacityStep'; Python test tests/test_document_scopes.py::test_repeat_opacity_decrements_after_animation | Contradicts its own schema documentation; result differs whenever the repeat's opacity is not 1. |
| EVAL-27 | The continuity parameter of the TCB tangent has the opposite sign of Kochanek and Bartels 1984: with c=-1 Rust's outgoing tangent equals the incoming chord (an inverted corner), while the paper (and Wikipedia: c=+1 inverted corners, c=-1 box corners) gives the next chord. Tension and bias are standard. Only matters when continuity != 0; Python follows the paper. | crates/sr-eval/src/channel.rs:394-395 `out_w = ((1-te)(1-co)(1+bi)/2, (1-te)(1+co)(1-bi)/2)`, `in_w = ((1-te)(1+co)(1+bi)/2, (1-te)(1-co)(1-bi)/2)` while the comment cites 'Kochanek & Bartels 1984, eq. 9'; python scenerender/anim.py::_tcb_tangents uses (1-t)(1+b)(1+c)/2 for the outgoing difference | Contradicts the cited paper. Probe tcbc (continuity 0.8 at the middle key): Rust 28.625 at t=0.5 vs Python 31.75 even before the non-uniform scaling. |
| EVAL-37 | ease, easeIn, easeOut are cubic-bezier approximations with arbitrary constants (0.33/0.67, not 1/3, 2/3) whereas Python uses the polynomial smooth ease family (u*u*(3-2u), u*u*(2-u), 1-(1-u)^2*(1+u)); differences up to 0.09 on a 0..100 range (9.087 vs 9.001 at u=0.185). | crates/sr-eval/src/expr/vm.rs:1286-1288 `cubic_bezier(0.33, 0.0, 0.67, 1.0, u)`, `(0.33,0,1,1)`, `(0,0,0.67,1)`; schema expressionType doc calls them linear/ease/easeIn/easeOut(t, t0, t1, v0, v1) without defining the curve | Magic numbers (0.33 vs 1/3) and no spec for the shape; the After Effects functions they imitate are smooth polynomial easings. Port anyway for parity but flag. |
| EVAL-17 | <instance> declares clipOut (XSD instanceType) but sr-eval never reads it: the symbol clock is built from clipIn, speed, loop and reverse only, so an instance's clipOut has no effect in Rust. Python honours it. | crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs:1731 `self.symbol_clock(idx, dur, inst.speed, inst.clip_in, inst.r#loop, inst.reverse, remap)` (no clip_out); schema attribute clipOut on instanceType; layers use clip_out in layer_clock (program.rs:1469-1473) | A declared attribute that is silently ignored. Python's behaviour should be kept as the parity target. |
| EVAL-29 | A channel with loop or ping-pong extrapolation evaluated at exactly the last key time returns the FIRST key's value (eval() sends t >= last key to extrapolate, and cycle=1 maps to the span start), a discontinuity at an authored key. | crates/sr-eval/src/channel.rs Channel::eval `if t >= tn { return self.extrapolate(t, self.after, true) }` then extrapolate Loop branch; probe lp keys (1,10)(2,30)(3,35): Rust 10.0 at t=3.0, Python 35.0 | The last key's authored value is replaced at its own time; likely an off-by-one (should be t > tn). Exclude that instant from gates. |
| EVAL-34 | null and undefined are one value (V::Undef), so `null === undefined` is true (JavaScript: false). | crates/sr-eval/src/expr/vm.rs:19-21 `/// undefined / null. Undef`; probe `null === undefined` -> Rust 1 | Contradicts the stated ECMAScript-subset semantics; very low impact. |
| EVAL-37 | parseFloat is an alias of Number, so parseFloat('3.5abc') is NaN (property keeps its base value) while JavaScript returns 3.5. | crates/sr-eval/src/expr/vm.rs:321 `"Number" \| "parseFloat" => (Number, 1, 1)`; probe parseFloat("3.5abc") Rust keeps base 5, Python 3.5 | Not the documented JavaScript behaviour of parseFloat. |
| EVAL-02 | `scene-render eval --format json` is not machine-clean: E16 warnings and every diagnostic are written to stdout before the JSON object (all output uses the same writer), so the JSON cannot be piped to a parser without stripping a prefix. | crates/scene-render/src/main.rs:870-872 `for w in ev.warnings() { out.diagnostic(file, &lines, w)?; }` before the format switch; observed: stdout of `eval seq2.xml --format json` starts with 'warning[E16]: ...' lines and stderr is empty | Warnings belong on stderr; a JSON format should print only JSON. The Python eval command (P2-EVAL-03) should not copy this. |
| EVAL-41 | Two different seed rules coexist: node, copy and emitter seeds use FNV-1a + SplitMix64 (hash_str of the id), while text animators, generators, scramble and effects use CRC-32 of 'project:id:@seed:purpose' (d24 element_seed); README documents only the CRC-32 rule. | crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs:1246 and :1616 (hash_str), crates/sr-eval/src/sim.rs:1178 (hash_str ^ project seed) versus crates/sr-vector/src/d24.rs:72 element_seed and crates/sr-gpu/src/text.rs:473 | Inconsistent derivation of 'the element seed' across features and an undocumented rule for nodes; copying both into Python needs the coordinator to confirm which features use which. |
| EVAL-20 | alignItems='baseline' is accepted by the schema and the Rust model but behaves exactly like 'start' (no baseline computation). | crates/sr-eval/src/layout.rs::cross `AlignItems::Start \| AlignItems::Baseline => (0.0, 1.0)`; Python scenerender/layout.py::_baseline aligns text first baselines | An enumerated option with no implementation; Python's richer behaviour should be kept rather than downgraded. |
| COMP-12 | The Rust schema documents mask @feather as 'blurred by @feather pixels' while the shader, the README and the Rust tests implement standard deviation in the node's LOCAL units (scales with the node transform). | schema/scene-render-1.1.xsd:475 ('blurred by @feather pixels') versus README.md:119 ('Feather is a Gaussian blur of standard deviation feather ... apply in local space'), crates/sr-gpu/src/shaders.wgsl::mask_value (sigma = max(m.feather, 0.4*aa), local units) and tests/composite.rs masks_combine_modes_invert_and_feather ('sigma 0.5 local units (2 pixels)'). Python's copy of the XSD repeats the 'pixels' wording while its CONVENTIONS 5.15 says std dev `feather`. | Schema text and implementation disagree; at scale 1 both agree, at any other scale (the Rust test uses x4) they differ by that factor. P3-COMP-10 implements the shader/README behaviour; the coordinator should confirm which is normative and fix the doc. |
| COMP-02 | In Rust a threeD group that is not otherwise isolated silently loses its own 2.5D transform (zDepth/rotationX/rotationY), and group @collapse is never read by the renderer, although the schema defines both. | crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::isolated (l.2006-2022: no threeD/collapse clause; only isolate, effects, mask, clip, matte, blend); `collapse` appears in crates/sr-model only (grep of sr-gpu and sr-eval finds none). Rendered check: threeD group rotationY=50 non-isolated draws a flat 40x40 (1600 px) while the same group with isolate=true projects (1171 px). schema/scene-render-1.1.xsd:1518 threeD ('place this 2D node in camera space at depth zDepth with rotationX/rotationY') and :2566 collapse ('2.5D children share the parent's camera space instead of being flattened'). | Contradicts its own schema documentation, and whether a group is projected depends on unrelated attributes (isolate, blend). P3-COMP-05 therefore keeps Python's behaviour and asks for a decision rather than copying. |
| COMP-27 | Mesh gradient point x/y are accepted by the schema but ignored by the renderer; the schema calls the element a 'bicubic colour patch grid'. | schema/scene-render-1.1.xsd:610-617 meshPointType declares x and y (xs:double); crates/sr-gpu/src/paint.rs PaintsChild::MeshGradient arm reads only row, col and colour; common.wgsl eval_paint kind 4 evaluates a regular-grid Catmull-Rom surface (positions via catmull_rom_param). | A schema attribute with no effect; Python honours it. Keep Python's behaviour; do not 'fix' towards Rust. |
| COMP-31 | Grid line width and tile size replicate the Python tile-rasteriser's pixel rounding inside an analytic shader (arbitrary constants). | crates/sr-gpu/src/shaders.wgsl fs_generator case 6u: n = clamp(round(s), 2, 256); w = max(1, round(n*0.04))/n; and tests/composite.rs is literally named generator_patterns_and_gradient_geometry_follow_the_python_renderer. | The 0.04 line fraction is quantised to whole pixels of a notional n-pixel tile, so the line width jumps (e.g. scale 16 gives 1/16=0.0625, scale 100 gives 0.04) instead of scaling smoothly; it copies an artefact of Python's cairo tile, not a specified formula. P3-COMP-14 copies it for parity but the coordinator should decide whether the artefact is specified. |
| COMP-38 | The offscreen/matte cache key includes a raw pointer address of the node's element Arc, so keys are not stable across runs and depend on allocator reuse. | crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::content_hash (words include Arc::as_ptr(&n.elem) as u64); crates/sr-gpu/tests/cache_regressions.rs::caption_cache_survives_program_address_reuse documents a regression of this class. | Output correctness then depends on addresses never being reused for a different element while a cache entry lives. Not a rendering divergence; do not port pointer hashing: Python's cache keys the element XML plus signatures (P3-COMP-20). |
| COMP-19 | The mip chain drops the last row/column of odd-sized levels instead of averaging it in. | crates/sr-gpu/src/resources.rs::mips: next level is (pw/2, ph/2) and each texel averages the 2x2 block starting at 2x,2y (sx=min(2x+dx,pw-1)), so for pw=5 column 4 is never sampled; its test mip_chain_averages only checks the sizes (5,3)->(2,1)->(1,1). | Non-power-of-two images lose edge content in minified views (a standard NPOT chain would weight or include it). Effect is small; P3-COMP-16 follows Rust for parity and notes it. |
| FX-28 | echo and posterize-time are hoisted in front of every other effect regardless of document order: the first loop of run_chain processes them, the second loop skips them. An effect listed before echo (e.g. effects='grade echo') is therefore applied to the accumulated trail instead of to each frame. | crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs L996-1057 (first loop: comment 'they apply first, in document order') and L1079 (`if matches!(kind, "posterize-time" \| "echo") { continue; }`); README 'The chain runs in document order' | Contradicts the README sentence that the chain runs in document order, and the code comment says 'in document order' while implementing 'first'. Python keeps document order; do not copy the hoisting without a decision. |
| FX-28 | echo normalises its weights to sum 1 and accumulates additively, so the current frame is only weight/total opaque (a moving layer's leading edge is 1/count opaque); samples is clamped to at least 2 so samples=1 still draws two copies. | crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs L1013-1030 (`weights[k] / total`, `.clamp(2, 16)` also at L846); crates/sr-gpu/tests/effect_costs.rs::echoed_layers_keep_their_effect_targets_to_the_trail (alpha 1/3 at x=94) | An echo trail normally keeps the current frame at full strength; here the visible result loses opacity as copies increase. The matrix describes it as 'over only', which is wrong; behaviour is the documented 'decaying weights' but the normalisation is probably unintended. |
| FX-38 | README Limitations says blend modes inside motion-blur samples composite against transparency, but the code blends the accumulated result with the node's blend mode (draw_accumulated) and a test requires >= 40 dB against the average of sharp frames for multiply/screen/add. | README.md L352 (Limitations bullet 2) vs crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs::draw_accumulated L1445-1490 ('opacity, masks and matte were applied inside each sample; the samples blend as one') and crates/sr-gpu/tests/motion_blur.rs::moving_layers_keep_their_blend_mode L45 | Stale documentation (also repeated in the parity matrix row FX-38); implementation and test agree with Python's whole-frame supersampling. |
| FX-38 | A node that does not exist at the first or last shutter sample (it starts at t=0 or ends inside the shutter) gets no motion blur at all and is drawn sharp; the adaptive box test uses an arbitrary 100x100 box for unsized nodes; camera@shutterAngle is declared in the schema but unused. | crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs L1297-1300 (`let (Some(&a), Some(&b)) = ... else { return false }`; eval at t<0 returns 0 nodes), L1303 (`n.size.unwrap_or([100.0, 100.0])`), L1293 (`let angle = pr.shutter_angle;` project only) vs schema/scene-render-1.1.xsd L2529 (camera shutterAngle) | Disabling blur exactly where a node fades in/out contradicts 'motion blur is the average of the sharp frames' (tests/motion_blur.rs header); 100x100 is an arbitrary stand-in for unsized groups; the camera attribute exists in the shared schema and Python honours it. |
| FX-22 | Schema defaults are used as 'unset' sentinels: size > 1 means set (so size=1 cannot be authored: the Rust test writes size="1.001" to get a 1 px pixelate cell), letterbox uses > 1.01, halftone >= 2, unsharp-mask treats threshold == 0.7 (the schema default shared with glow) as no threshold, tile's amount is max(1). | crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs L1508 (long-shadow `sz > 1.0`), L1747 (letterbox `sz > 1.01`), L1768, L1784, L1809 (halftone `sz >= 2.0`), L1900-1962 (turbulent/wave/ripple/heat-haze/pixelate), L2000 (unsharp `0.7 - 1e-9..=0.7 + 1e-9`), L1958 (tile); crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs L300 (`<effect id="pix" type="pixelate" size="1.001"/>`) | Inconsistent thresholds (1.0, 1.01, 2.0) and values that cannot be authored; one shared schema default (size=1, threshold=0.7) is reinterpreted per effect. The same document cannot express a legitimate 1 px cell or a 0.7 threshold. |
| FX-11 | color-grade ignores @exposure and applies contrast about 0.5 on sRGB-encoded values, while its sibling effects (glow, drop-shadow, vignette) are described by the Rust code and tests as following decision D9, and Python documents the D9 colour-grade as 2^exposure then a 0.18-pivot power contrast in working space. Also the README says the colour kernel runs 'on straight linear values' but color-grade, lift-gamma-gain, cdl, levels, curves, hue/sat, sepia, invert, posterize, threshold, tritone, gradient-map are computed on encoded values (enc()). | crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl L366-372 (enc() contrast), L373-465 (encoded ops) vs README.md 'A per-pixel kernel runs 25 colour operations on straight linear values'; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs L105/L118 (glow and vignette cite D9) | Possible deviation from the shared decision D9 (its text lives in sr-core, not in either repo, so this is not proven) and a README/code contradiction. Needs the user to confirm before copying Rust's colour-grade. |
| FX-41 | look @space is ignored for CDL looks (CDL is applied on sRGB-encoded values) and CDL attributes are ignored when @src is present, while the schema gives look/@space a default of acescct and the Python renderer converts to that space. | crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs L1901-1946 (CDL branch applies grade op 3 with no space conversion; `if let Some(src) = &look.src` takes precedence), schema/scene-render-1.1.xsd L3803 | @space is a typed attribute on every look; honouring it only for LUT looks makes the same look change meaning when a .cube is added. README only says 'a LUT file in the look's space, or ASC CDL'. |
| FX-19 | lights referenced by the lighting effect are all read as point lights (x, y, range or distance, intensity) whatever their type, ambient/directional/spot included, and their position is in the node's local space; light colour is ignored (only the effect's @color tints). | crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs::lights_of L699-722 (no `type` check; `to_uv` maps through the node transform); schema lightType L3156-3200 lists 8 types; README 'up to four point lights from lights' | An ambient light has no position and becomes a point light at (0,0); a directional light's x/y/range are meaningless. Python's per-type treatment is the more faithful reading of the schema. |
| FX-18 | fractal-noise (and lens-flare, light-leak, light-sweep, god-rays, letterbox, radial/zoom blur, warps) are UNBOUNDED: on a layer node the effect target is the whole frame and fractal-noise returns mix(src, opaque grey, amount), so a layer with fractal-noise paints noise over the whole frame outside its own box. | crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs UNBOUNDED L84-102; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl case 70 L640-644 (`mix(s, vec4(vec3(n),1.0), v[1].y)`); measured: 128x128 frame filled with noise from a 96 px layer | A generator applied to a layer should be bounded by the layer or documented as frame-wide; the README only says unbounded effects 'use the whole target' for cost reasons. |
| FX-09 | long-shadow's colour alpha (default 0.6) is used as the far-end opacity of a linear fade, so the shadow is nearly opaque next to the object whatever the colour alpha; fade = 1 - k/(n+1)*(1 - colour.alpha) and colour.alpha itself is not applied to the shadow. | crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs L1507-1515 (`v[1] = [c0, c1, c2, 1 - c[3]]`), crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_line default case L880-890 (`a = max(a, Sz(...).a * (1.0 - k/(n+1) * v[1].w))`, `vec4(v[1].rgb, 1.0) * a`) | README documents `color` only; a 60 % default colour producing a ~100 % opaque near end is an unusual reading of colour alpha. |
| FX-10 | stroke/outline @position='center' draws a band 2*size wide (extends size outside and size inside) although @size is documented as the width and the shape stroke reach is 'half the width centred'; matte-choke dilation leaves rgb = 0 in the grown area (black premultiplied fringe). | crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs L2035-2054 (center: dilate and erode both over radius size*px), crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl L1118-1128 (`ring = clamp(mx - mn)`; dilate branch `unpre(s) * na`); README 'stroke and outline size (width)' | Centre stroke is twice the stated width; Python's centre ring is r/2 each side (width r). The black fringe of a grown matte is a visible defect Python avoids on purpose. |
| FX-R02 | Pixel-anchored effects (film-grain/noise/glitch draw indices (y*width+x)*3+c, pixelate/mosaic/halftone cells, scanline phase, wave/ripple phase, fbm coordinates) are computed in the effect target's own pixel coordinates, whose origin and width come from the box + reach rounding to a multiple of 32 px that was introduced as a texture-pool optimisation. | crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs TARGET_STEP/round_span L147-173 and the rect computation L900-945; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl grain_draws L353-362, pixelate case 12 L776, scanlines case 66 L607; measured: Python pixelate at layer (19,21) vs Rust 32.6 dB, at (16,16) 67.4 dB | Output depends on a performance heuristic (target size rounding) rather than on node or frame coordinates; two scenes differing only by the layer's position (or an equivalent group wrapper) give different noise/grid phase. Parity requires reproducing it (P3-FX-04), but it deserves a documented convention. |
| FX-23 | The lens-distortion shader comment says 'barrel < 0 < pincushion' while the inverse mapping uv = 0.5 + p*(1 + k1*r^2) samples farther out for k1 > 0, which compresses content towards the centre (barrel); k1 = amount*0.2 is also fixed to the frame centre and aspect-normalised by height. | crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl L744-748; crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs L1936-1942 | Sign convention in the comment contradicts the mapping (low confidence: the convention may be defined in sr-core). Python uses Brown-Conrady k1 = amount*0.01; settle the sign with the user before porting. |
| FX-13 | The PQ-to-SDR tone mapper is commented as 'BT.2390-style knee at 1.0' but implements a 0.75 knee with an exponential roll-off, not the BT.2390 EETF; hable is available as an effect tonemapper but not as a colorManagement toneMapping value. | crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl L321-331; crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs L1963-1970 (match has no 'hable') | Comment and code disagree and the curve is an ad-hoc approximation; Python implements the real BT.2390 EETF. |
| FX-07 | The bloom/halation soft knee uses hard-coded factors (`w * w * k * 0.5 * 2.0` with k=0.5, a window of +-0.5 around the threshold) and bloom and halation are the same code with only the default tint different; glow uses a 1e-4 knee. | crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl L216-224; crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs L1430-1443 | The 0.5 and 2.0 factors cancel and the knee width is not exposed or documented; the matrix describes the knee as '0.5' only. |

## Phase 2: evaluation semantics

### EVAL

#### P2-EVAL-00 — Eval-parity harness: rs eval vs Python dump, expression table, sidecar args for render_parity  

Size **M** · track **P2-C** · rows: EVAL-01 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: —

**Goal.** tools/parity/eval_parity.py exists and is the single gate runner for every EVAL package. It (1) runs `scene-render eval FILE -t T --format json --no-assets` (plus per-scene sidecar args from SCENE.args: --variant, --param k=v, --layout, --row N, --data id) and extracts the JSON after the diagnostics prefix, (2) obtains the Python record from scenerender.framegraph (P2-EVAL-01) or, for negative scenes, the Python diagnostic codes (P2-EVAL-02), (3) canonicalises (Length strings -> (value,unit), pairs, colours, affines) and compares the field set with the tolerances in area_notes, (4) prints a per-scene/time/field table and writes results.json + SUMMARY.md with statuses MATCH / DIFFERENT / RS-ERROR / PY-ERROR / BOTH-ERROR-SAME-CODES. A second mode, tools/parity/expr_table.py, takes a text file with one expression per line, generates one single-layer scene each (x animated by the expression, fps 10, seed 1), compares the value of x at t=0,0.3,1.3,2.9 and treats `Rust compile error` as expecting Python diagnostic E01. render_parity.py gains the same sidecar args so pixel gates can use --layout/--variant/--param. The probe scenes listed in area_notes are committed under tools/parity/scenes/eval/ with their expected Rust verdicts in a manifest (scenes.json: scene, times, expected status per package). A pytest wrapper tests/test_parity_harness.py skips unless SR_RS_BIN points at the binary.

**Python files.** tools/parity/eval_parity.py (new); tools/parity/expr_table.py (new); tools/parity/scenes/eval/*.scene.xml + scenes.json (new); tools/parity/render_parity.py (branch parity-render; add sidecar args); tests/test_parity_harness.py (new)

#### P2-EVAL-01 — Python FrameGraph walker (scenerender/framegraph.py) and evaluation purity test  

Size **L** · track **P2-C** · rows: EVAL-01 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-00, P2-EVAL-02

**Goal.** scenerender.framegraph.evaluate_frame(renderer, t, frame=None) returns a FrameGraph mirroring the Rust JSON: nodes in paint order with id (effective scoped id: instance path joined with '/', repeat copies 'rep[k]' and 'rep[k]/child', include namespace 'inc/x', object3D copies 'id[k]'), kind ('repeat', 'copy', 'include' for the generated groups, schema names otherwise), parent/depth, local and world affines (parent chain via the same RenderContext.node_matrix incl. @parent and layout slots), opacity and world_opacity (plain product, no clamping), timeline_time (the clock the parent hands the node, sequence shifts undone), local_time (= timeline_time minus node start), source_time (layer media_time, instance symbol time), asset, resolved text (after templating and repeat substitution), draw (False for hidden matte sources), matte index / is_matte, repeat [k,n], size, anchor, content{dest,uv}, clip, props (values of properties that have animate/expression/link/motionPath, with Length unit preserved), transitions (kind, from/to index, eased progress, velocity), camera index, elements (animated effect/paint-stop state) and background. It is built by walking exactly the same methods render_children uses (child_order incl. animated z, active/enter_node/child_ctx, layout_positions, active_transitions with handle extension, hidden_mattes, enter_instance) and never draws or opens files. Evaluating frames in any order, repeatedly, on one Renderer gives byte-identical JSON.

**Python files.** scenerender/framegraph.py (new); scenerender/compositor.py (read-only use: RenderContext.child_order, active, enter_node, node_matrix, layout_positions, active_transitions, hidden_mattes, world_matrix); scenerender/evaluator.py (read-only use: Evaluator.get/num/enter_instance/child_ctx, run_expression); scenerender/render.py::Renderer.open (read-only); scenerender/nodes/core.py (read-only use: media_time, _repeat_vars)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_evaluated_nodes.py (end-to-end style; no pins expected to break)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs::evaluate_inner (visit, world_of, mattes, transitions, camera); crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs::FrameNode / FrameGraph / FrameTransition; crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs::Frame::local, Frame::size, Frame::arrange, Frame::stacked; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::transitions_and_mattes; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::transforms_compose_through_groups_and_percentages

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/seq-groups.scene.xml` at t=[0.45, 0.575, 0.75, 0.85]: P A <symbols><symbol id='two'><layer id='f' asset='img' start='1' end='2'/></symbol></symbols> <composition><sequence id='s' start='1'><group id='g1' end='2'><layer id='a' asset='img' start='0' end='2'/></group><group id='g2' end='2'><layer id='b' asset='img' start='0.5' end='2'/><sequence id='in' transition='crossfade' transitionDuration='0.5'><layer id='c' asset='img' end='1'/><layer id='d' asset='img' end='1'/></sequence></group><group id='g3' end='2' timeScale='2'><layer id='e' asset='img' start='1' end='3'/></group><instance id='i' symbol='two' end='2'/></sequence></composition></scene>. Rust at t=4.5: s,g2,b,in,d with s local 3.5, g2 local 1.5, b local 1.0, d local 0.5; t=5.75: s,g3,e with e timeline 6.5 local 0.5; t=7.5: s,i (local 0.5); t=8.5: s,i,i/f (i/f timeline 1.5 local 0.5). Scratch file seq1.xml. Expect **MATCH on listed fields (ids exact, times 1e-9)** (today: DIFFERENT today only because no dump exists; activity pattern already agrees).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/seq-transitions-matte.scene.xml` at t=[0.3, 0.55]: P A <composition><sequence id='s' transition='crossfade' transitionDuration='1'><layer id='a' asset='img' end='3'/><layer id='b' asset='img' end='3'/><layer id='c' asset='img' end='3'/><transition type='wipe' from='b' to='c' duration='2' alignment='end' curve='linear'/></sequence><layer id='m' asset='img'/><layer id='u' asset='img' matte='m'/></composition></scene> (tr1.xml). Expect **MATCH** (today: DIFFERENT (Python dump must extend visibility over transition handles; my crude walker lost c at 5.5)).
- Port Rust test `transitions_and_mattes` (crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs): t=3: 1 transition 'crossfade', progress 0.5, a and b present; t=5.5: 'wipe' progress 0.75, m.is_matte and not draw, u.matte == index of m Python: build tr1 scene in tmp_path, fg = evaluate_frame(Renderer.open(p), 3.0); assert fg.transitions[0].kind=='crossfade' and approx(fg.transitions[0].progress,0.5); repeat at 5.5 for wipe/0.75/matte index.

**Risks.** The walker duplicates traversal logic of render_children and can drift; mitigate with the purity test and by calling the same methods, and plan a later refactor where render_children consumes an iterator the walker shares. #### P2-EVAL-04 — Parameters: precedence and typed conversion/checks (time timecode, list, E07/E08/E10)  

Size **M** · track **P2-A** · rows: EVAL-03, EVAL-05 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-02

**Goal.** Parameter values resolve default < variant <set> < data row < --param (later wins) and every resolved value is converted by declared type: string/enum/color/asset as text; number via finite xsd double; boolean true|false|1|0; time as seconds or HH:MM:SS:FF timecode at the project fps; list as JSON array or comma list with numeric cells typed (a, b ,3 -> ['a','b',3]). Checks: number/time min/max, string/enum maxLength (chars) and anchored regex pattern, enum options, color parses, asset id exists, required means a value must come from default/variant/row/CLI (E08). Violations, a --param naming an undeclared parameter (E07 with did-you-mean), an unknown variant or layout (E10) are errors, including for a declared default (today a bad default only warns). Typed values are stored as Document.param_values and returned by Evaluator.parameter/_typed_param (the one-line evaluator change is made by P2-EVAL-12). `load(..., row=)` precedence slot is added here and filled by P2-EVAL-05.

#### P2-EVAL-05 — Data sources: JSON/CSV/TSV parsing, numeric cells, E09, --row [source:]index, data as list  

Size **S** · track **P2-A** · rows: EVAL-04 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-04

**Goal.** <data> sources parse like sr-eval data.rs: JSON array of records/values or {'rows': [...]} (anything else is E09), CSV/TSV with RFC 4180 quoting, trimmed header names, header required, ragged rows are E09 ('row N has K fields; the header has M'), numeric cells (trimmed, finite) become numbers, everything else stays text. A source is exposed as a list parameter (param('rows') and repeat @over='rows'), data src may not be remote, sha256 still checked. load(row=(source_id|None, index)) overlays the record's fields onto DECLARED parameters only, after the variant and before --param; a missing source or index is E09; --row/--data CLI flags (P2-EVAL-03) feed it.

#### P2-EVAL-06 — Binds, variant and layout overrides: order, typed set, E05/E06  

Size **M** · track **P2-A** · rows: EVAL-06 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-04

**Goal.** After parameters resolve, apply binds first (param -> target.property, optional 'a=b;c=d' map with trimmed keys matched against the param's string form), then the variant's overrides, then the selected layout's overrides; later wins (Python today does layout, variant, bind). An override or bind goes through a typed set: an unknown target id is E05, an attribute the target element does not declare or a value its XSD simple type rejects is E06 (today: log warning / raw el.set). Instance <override> children are validated the same way inside their instance scope at load (E05 target not inside the symbol, E06 bad value) instead of being applied silently by evaluator.Scope.

#### P2-EVAL-07 — Repeat expansion semantics and {{placeholder}} templating (E16, E10)  

Size **M** · track **P2-A** · rows: EVAL-07, EVAL-08 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-01, P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-04, P2-EVAL-05

**Goal.** Repeat copies match sr-eval: copy k gets cumulative offsets k*offsetX/Y, k*rotationStep, scaleStep^k, opacity factor max(0,1-k*opacityStep) multiplied into the repeat's own opacity (decision pending, see rust_may_be_wrong), timeStep stagger on the copy's clock; ids are 'rep[k]' for the copy and 'rep[k]/child' for its descendants (nested: 'a[1]/b[2]/g'), kind 'repeat' for the container and 'copy' for copies. Variables: index is the copy position k (0-based), count the number of copies, and for count repeats the item (param(@var)) is the number from + k*step; for @over repeats from/step select the items (skip 'from', every 'step') and count is the number selected. @over naming something that is not a list parameter/data source is E10. Placeholders {{id}}, {{id.field}} resolve at templating in the text/data/tex/value/label/prompt attributes of text, object3D, code, formula, meta and word elements and in span text; {{index}}, {{count}}, {{var}}, {{var.field}} per copy on text asset layers; an unknown name is left unchanged and reported as warning E16 (not for repeat variables).

**Python files.** scenerender/document.py::_expand_repeats, _repeat_text, _substitute_text, _remap_tree (mapping format), _param_list; scenerender/document.py (internal attributes urn:scenerender: index/count/var/item/kind); scenerender/nodes/core.py::_repeat_vars (read only; consumes the internal attributes unchanged)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_repeat_expands_with_steps (ids r#i, dot#2 -> r[i], r[2]/dot); tests/test_core.py::test_repeat_over_data_fills_text_templates (ids L#i); tests/test_document_scopes.py::test_repeat_opacity_decrements_after_animation (pins subtract semantics); tests/test_document_scopes.py::test_nested_repeat_parameter_bindings_keep_outer_items; tests/test_document_scopes.py::test_repeat_parent_links_mattes_and_expressions_stay_in_each_copy; tests/test_document_scopes.py::test_included_repeat_and_sequence_are_prepared_in_the_parent

#### P2-EVAL-08 — Instances, includes (@symbol, own clock, lifetime) and expansion guards E12/E13/E18  

Size **M** · track **P2-A** · rows: EVAL-09, EVAL-10, EVAL-11 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-01, P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-06, P2-EVAL-07

**Goal.** Includes work like sr-eval: <include src symbol?> loads the document, namespaces ids and assets ('inc/logo', 'inc/mark'), applies the include's <override> children inside that namespace (E05/E06 via P2-EVAL-06), and runs the included composition or symbol on instance clock rules: it lasts the symbol's duration (or the library project's duration), speed and loop default, box is the symbol width/height. include @symbol must work (today document._expand_includes moves the symbols section into the host document and only then looks the symbol up, so every include with @symbol raises 'symbol not found'). Missing file, invalid document or missing symbol is E12. Instance scoped ids are 'instance/inner' in the effective-id function used by the walker and doc.resolve_id (prop('i/f.x')). Guards: instance or include recursion (a symbol reaching itself directly or through other instances/includes, or depth > 16) is E13 instead of RecursionError; a composition whose repeats, object3D @instances and nested instances expand past 2^19 nodes is E18, counted with saturating integer arithmetic before any element is created (today 300x300x300 nested repeats are expanded eagerly).

**Python files.** scenerender/document.py::_expand_includes, _namespace_ids, _remap_tree, _expand_repeats (pre-count), _prepare; scenerender/eval_diagnostics.py (use)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_document_scopes.py::test_include_preserves_relative_paths_through_multiple_levels; tests/test_document_scopes.py::test_include_namespace_preserves_computed_references_and_tokens; tests/test_document_scopes.py::test_included_repeat_and_sequence_are_prepared_in_the_parent; tests/test_document_scopes.py::test_include_strict_cycle_and_hash_contracts (SceneError -> E13 code); tests/test_core.py::test_instance_clock_and_overrides

#### P2-EVAL-09 — Timing: marker windows (marker + @start/@end), beat grid numbering, marker-pinned sequence children  

Size **M** · track **P2-A** · rows: EVAL-13, EVAL-14, EVAL-42 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-01

**Goal.** Node windows follow sr-eval: start = marker time (when @startMarker) PLUS @start, end = @end, or marker time plus @end when @endMarker is given (Python today uses the marker alone and ignores @start/@end). Layers of video/image sequences still end with their media unless @end is set. In a sequence a child with @startMarker stays on its marker (not shifted by the cursor) and the cursor continues from marker start + the child's duration; contents of groups/nested sequences move with their container; instances run on their own clock. beatGrid generates beat.N = offset + N*60/bpm and bar.N = offset + N*60/bpm*beatsPerBar for any non-negative integer N, 0-based, from the FIRST grid only; explicit markers win; beat() keeps its formula (t-offset)*bpm/60 (audio-detected beats are the audio area). Evaluation stays dynamic (expressions/animation on start/end keep working) but static documents must give these windows.

**Python files.** scenerender/document.py::_markers, Document.window, natural_duration, _schedule_sequences; scenerender/scheduling.py::authored_window, _place, sequence_schedule, window  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_beat_grid_markers (pins 1-based numbering: beat.1 == offset; must be rewritten to beat.0/beat.1); tests/test_core.py::test_sequence_schedules_children; tests/test_sequence_scheduling.py (all; timing overrides must keep working); docs/TIMING.md (update the marker rule)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::marker_time, markers_of; crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::instantiate (start/end/marker placement); crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::place_sequence, shift, intrinsic; crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs::Program::beat_at; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::markers_place_nodes_and_keys; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::sequences_place_children_end_to_end; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::sequences_move_groups_with_their_contents; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::group_time_offset_and_scale_shift_children

#### P2-EVAL-10 — @parent: scoped resolution, cycle rejection (E11), dropped links  

Size **S** · track **P2-A** · rows: EVAL-44 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-07

**Goal.** A load-time pass resolves every @parent in its lexical scope (instance, repeat copy, include namespace via doc.resolve_id), builds the parent graph including the implicit container parent, and reports E11 for a cycle or a target outside the composition (e.g. a symbol child naming a composition node); the offending @parent is dropped so evaluation cannot recurse. Today a parent cycle crashes with RecursionError in Evaluator._anims.

#### P2-EVAL-11 — Expression language syntax: if/else, blocks, return, compound assignment, ?., strict declarations  

Size **M** · track **P2-B** · rows: EVAL-34 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-00

**Goal.** expr.compile_expr accepts every program sr-eval's parser accepts and evaluates it identically: statements separated by ; or newline; let/const/var NAME = expr (a declaration without initialiser is a syntax error in Rust); bare assignment declares; compound assignment += -= *= /= %= **= on declared names; if (c) stmt [else stmt], blocks {...} with block scope, return expr; optional chaining ?. and the arrow token are lexed (arrow functions stay rejected); // and /* */ comments; out-of-range index is undefined. Still rejected: loops, function definitions, object literals, new, template literals, assignment to members. Python's extra operators (bitwise, typeof, comma declarations) may stay (superset) but must not change results of Rust-valid programs. Nesting limit aligned with Rust (MAX_DEPTH 64, MAX_HEIGHT 1000) and the cost bounds (string/array doubling refused: 'too large', 'too deeply'). vexpr.py must raise _Unsupported for the new statements so object3D instancing falls back to the scalar path.

**Python files.** scenerender/expr.py::_Tokenizer/_tokenize, _Parser (statements, program), compile_expr; scenerender/vexpr.py::_Parser (reject new syntax)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_expr.py (precedence, ternary, strings, short-circuit, declarations, test_security, test_step_limit, test_depth_and_size_limits, test_compile_is_cached); tests/test_vexpr.py

Currently DIFF (measured): `let x = 3; x **= 2; x` (Rust 9), `let x = 3; x -= 1; x /= 2; x %= 5; x` (1), `[1,2]?.length` (2), `undefined?.length ?? 9` (9), `let x = 1; { let x = 2; } x` (1), `if (1) 4` (4), `if (time > 1) 10; else 20;` (20 at t<=1), `if (time > 1) { x = 1 } else { x = 2 } x` (2 at t<=1), `return 7` (7), `let x = 3; return x*2; 99` (6), `let a = 2; a += 3; a *= 2; a` (10), `if (time > 1) { return 10; } return 20;` (20 at t<=1).

**Risks.** expr.py is a closure-tree interpreter without an AST: statement support (blocks, return, scoping) needs a small AST or a control-flow signal in the closures. Keep lru_cache behaviour of compile_expr. Do not break lottie selector expressions that call builtin_functions.

#### P2-EVAL-12 — Expression built-ins and variables: fps, duration, frame, vector helpers, Math.random, ease family, typed param()  

Size **M** · track **P2-B** · rows: EVAL-37 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-04, P2-EVAL-05, P2-EVAL-11, P2-EVAL-21

**Goal.** Variables fps and duration exist; `frame` is floor(t*fps+1e-9) of the node's own time t (Python overrides it with the composition render frame); vector helpers length(a[,b]), normalize, dot, add, sub, mul, div, degreesToRadians, radiansToDegrees exist with the sr-eval semantics (missing components 0, normalize of a zero vector is zeros); Math.random() is the same seeded draw as random() (same site numbering); Number(x)/parseFloat(x) both = JS Number() (see rust_may_be_wrong); Math.min()/max() with no args are +/-Infinity; linear/ease/easeIn/easeOut(t[,t0,t1],v0,v1) use the sr-eval curves (linear u; ease cubic-bezier(0.33,0,0.67,1); easeIn (0.33,0,1,1); easeOut (0,0,0.67,1)); spring, clamp, lerp, smoothstep unchanged. param(name) returns the TYPED value from Document.param_values (number, bool, list, record rows for data sources, dotted item.field) and falls back to repeat vars first. loopIn/loopOut(kind,n) delegate to anim.eval_loop (P2-EVAL-21) and apply only outside the key range. geo/geoVisible are deferred (map assets). Non-finite or non-numeric results of a numeric property leave the property at its previous value (see P2-EVAL-15).

**Python files.** scenerender/expr.py::builtin_functions, _GLOBALS, _MATH, _interp, make_wiggle; scenerender/evaluator.py::run_expression (env vars, loop closure), parameter, _typed_param; scenerender/vexpr.py (mirror or raise _Unsupported for new functions)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_expr.py::test_linear_and_ease (ease(0.5,0,10)==5 still holds; easeIn<5<easeOut still holds); tests/test_expr.py::test_clamp_lerp_smoothstep_spring; tests/test_expression_clocks.py::test_loop_functions_use_animation_time_base_and_vector_component; tests/test_vexpr.py

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/expr/vm.rs::Func::lookup and call (all built-ins); crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs::ExprHost (var, param, loop_value, beat, random); crates/sr-eval/src/curve.rs::cubic_bezier; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::expressions_read_values_other_properties_and_params; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::seeded_expression_functions_are_deterministic; crates/sr-eval/src/expr/vm.rs tests::vectors_and_builtins

**Acceptance gate.**

#### P2-EVAL-13 — Static compile pass: E01 names/arity, E02 unknown properties, E03 prop() cycles, shared-dependency memoisation  

Size **L** · track **P2-B** · rows: EVAL-35, EVAL-36 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-11

**Goal.** Every expression and @condition is analysed once when the Evaluator is built (before any frame): syntax errors, unknown identifiers/functions/Math members, wrong argument counts, prop()/markerTime()/param loops with non-literal arguments, unknown markers and unknown audio bands/loop kinds are E01 with did-you-mean; prop('id.property') arguments resolve at analysis time to (node, property) and an unknown node/property is E01/E02 (animate/expression/link on a property the element does not declare is E02 with 'did you mean'); the dependency graph over prop() and node-sourced links is checked and a cycle is E03 with the cycle spelled out ('a.x -> b.x -> a.x'). Strict mode raises EvalError; lenient mode keeps today's warn-and-skip. Property reads inside one evaluation are memoised per (element, property, context) so a chain where every layer reads the next one twice (30 deep) evaluates in linear time (Python currently never finishes it).

**Python files.** scenerender/expr.py (analysis hook: record identifiers, calls with arity, literal prop()/markerTime() arguments while compiling); scenerender/evaluator.py::Evaluator.__post_init__/compile_all, get, _animate, run_expression, _link_source (memo and reference graph)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_expr.py::test_compile_is_cached; tests/test_expression_clocks.py::test_value_at_time_on_window_property_does_not_reenter_itself (valueAtTime self reference must stay legal); tests/test_expression_clocks.py::test_value_at_time_recomputes_normalized_window

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/expr/vm.rs::compile, Func::lookup (arity table), tests compile_errors, deps_are_collected; crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::compile_pending (E01 sites); crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::Builder::order and ExprResolver::prop (topological order, E03); crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::slot (E02 with suggestions); crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::expression_cycles_and_compile_errors; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::delayed_links_over_shared_dependencies_stay_cheap

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/prop-chain.scene.xml` at t=[0.1]: P A <composition><layer id='top' asset='img'><link property='x' source='s0.x' delay='0.1'/></layer> then for k in 0..29 <layer id='s{k}' asset='img'><expression property='x'>(prop('s{k+1}.x') + prop('s{k+1}.x')) / 2 + 1</expression></layer> and <layer id='s30' asset='img'><expression property='x'>time * 10</expression></layer></composition></scene> (chain.xml, generated by a 6-line script in the harness). Rust t=1.0: s0.x = 40, s1.x = 39, top.x = 39 (the chain 0.1 s ago). Expect **MATCH within 1e-9 and Python wall time < 10 s** (today: PY-ERROR (does not terminate)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/errors-E01-E03.scene.xml` at t=[0]: Documents: a.x expression 'time +* 2' -> {E01}; prop('nope.x') -> {E01}; cyc.xml (prop cycle between a and b) -> {E03}; link cycle (link on a.x source b.x and b.x source a.x) -> {E03}; expression table lines measured with Rust E01: `Math.sin()`, `Math.sin(1,2)`, `clamp(1,2)`, `wiggle(1)`, `param()`, `foo(1)`, `Math.foo(1)`, `markerTime(zz)`, `markerTime('zz')` (no marker), `a.b.c`, `1_000`, `-2 ** 2`, `typeof time`, `new Foo()`. Expect **identical code sets; message of E03 contains both ids** (today: DIFFERENT (Python warns at render time, `prop('a.x')` self-reference hits the recursion limit)).
- Port Rust test `expression_cycles_and_compile_errors` (crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs): a.x=prop('b.x'), b.x=prop('a.x')+1 -> codes ['E03'] with a.x and b.x in the message; 'time +* 2' -> ['E01']; prop('nope.x') -> ['E01'] Python: three scenes loaded with strict=True; pytest.raises(EvalError) and compare [d.code ...] and message substrings.
- Port Rust test `compile_errors` (crates/sr-eval/src/expr/vm.rs): messages: 'tim + 1' contains "did you mean 'time'"; 'wigle(1, 2)' -> "did you mean 'wiggle'"; prop(name) -> 'string literal'; prop('b.y') -> 'no property b.y'; markerTime('nope') -> 'no marker'; clamp(1, 2) -> 'takes 3 arguments'; loopOut('bounce') -> 'unknown loop type'; audioAmplitude('m','bass') -> 'unknown audio band'; Math.TAU -> 'not a constant'; value.map(1) -> 'only built-in functions' Python: expr.analyze(src, resolver) raising ExprError whose text contains each substring (resolver stub knows prop 'a.x' and marker 'drop').
- Port Rust test `delayed_links_over_shared_dependencies_stay_cheap` (crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs): 30-deep shared chain with a delayed link: s0.x=40, top.x=39 at t=1 Python: generate chain.xml in tmp_path, evaluate with a 10 s timeout (pytest-timeout or signal.alarm) and assert 40 and 39.
- Eval-level: negative-scene code sets plus the chain scene below (Python must complete in < 10 s; today it hangs, measured >60 s).

**Risks.** E01 in strict mode makes documents fail that Python silently tolerated (unknown identifiers, wrong arity): list affected corpus files before enabling by default. The memo key must include scope path, repeat vars and sequence clocks (Ctx.__hash__ already does) and be cleared per frame/sample time; do not break valueAtTime resampling.

#### P2-EVAL-14 — Channel compilation: anchor/skew aliases, normalized time base, additive, E04 value errors  

Size **M** · track **P2-B** · rows: EVAL-30 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-02

**Goal.** The pair aliases position, scale, anchor and skew animate (x,y), (scaleX,scaleY), (anchorX,anchorY), (skewX,skewY) (Python only knows position and scale: animate property='anchor' or 'skew' is ignored); timeBase='normalized' maps the node's window to 0..1 using the node's end or, when it has none, the project duration (Python gives 0 for open-ended nodes); local and composition bases unchanged; additive channels add to the layer below (value.add); key times with a marker add the marker time (unknown marker is validation's R21, not here). Eval-level E04: a key value that does not parse as the property's type (opacity 'lots'), keys that mix units or shapes (10% then 10), an invalid @bezier (x1 or x2 outside [0,1], not four numbers) are E04 naming the key; unknown animated properties are E02 (P2-EVAL-13).

#### P2-EVAL-15 — Typed values: schema facet clamping, integer rounding, non-finite results, z restacking  

Size **M** · track **P2-B** · rows: EVAL-31 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-12

**Goal.** Every value produced by animation, link or expression is clamped to its attribute's XSD facet range before use: numbers to minInclusive/maxInclusive (opacity unitDecimal 0..1; exclusive bounds nudged by epsilon), integer types rounded with floor(x+0.5) (z, steps...), positive lengths >= 1e-9, colour channels to [0,1]; NaN passes through; an expression or link result that is non-finite or cannot represent the property's kind leaves the property at its previous value (a numeric property never becomes inf or a string); a scalar assigned to a pair property fills both components; colours resolve style tokens (chains up to 8 deep) and interpolate component-wise; lengths keep their unit through interpolation. The animated integer z drives sibling order using the rounded value (equal z keeps document order). The facet table comes from the Python XSD (new scenerender/facets.py using schema.py read-only, or Bob's accessor if it exists).

**Python files.** scenerender/facets.py (new); scenerender/evaluator.py::get, _resolve_value, kind, parse, color, num; scenerender/compositor.py::child_order (z rounding; group C owns compositor.py: coordinate or expose Evaluator.z_index() and let child_order call it); scenerender/anim.py::parse_value/_mix (read only)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_evaluated_nodes.py::test_visibility_z_and_shape_change_rendered_pixels (z animates 0->2); tests/test_core.py::test_stacking_z_and_group_opacity; tests/test_core.py::test_timebase_marker_additive_and_colors (colour interpolation)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/value.rs::PropKind::clamp, from_v, of_simple_type, range_of, Value::lerp, weighted; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::values_clamp_to_their_schema_range; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::animated_z_restacks_siblings_every_frame; crates/sr-eval/src/value.rs tests::kinds_follow_the_schema, interpolation_and_clamping; crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs::Frame::stacked

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/values-clamp.scene.xml` at t=[0.05]: P A <composition><layer id='l' asset='img'><animate property='opacity' defaultInterpolation='back-out'><key time='0' value='0'/><key time='1' value='1'/></animate></layer></composition></scene>. Rust t=0.5 opacity 1.0 (back-out overshoots 1.0877 unclamped; Python gives 1.0877). Expect **MATCH** (today: DIFFERENT).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/z-restack.scene.xml` at t=[0.05, 0.15, 0.4, 0.6]: P <composition><shape id='a' shape='rect' width='10' height='10' z='0'><animate property='z'><key time='0' value='0' interpolation='hold'/><key time='1' value='10'/></animate></shape><group id='g' z='5'><shape id='g1' shape='rect' width='10' height='10'/></group><shape id='c' shape='rect' width='10' height='10'/><shape id='a2' shape='rect' width='10' height='10'><animate property='z'><key time='0' value='0'/><key time='10' value='3'/></animate></shape><shape id='b2' shape='rect' width='10' height='10' z='1'/></composition></scene>. Rust paint order of top-level ids at t=0.5: a,c,a2,b2,g,g1... (z 0,0,0.15->0,1,5) and at t=1.5: c,a2,b2,g,g1,a; a2 vs b2: t=4 -> a2 (z=1.2 rounds to 1, level with b2, document order), t=6 -> b2 then a2 (1.8 rounds to 2). Expect **MATCH on node order** (today: DIFFERENT (Python sorts by unrounded float z)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/errors-nonfinite.scene.xml` at t=[0]: layer x expressions `1/0`, `0/0`, `'a' + 1`, `param('zzz')`: Rust keeps x at its base value 5 for all four (non-finite or non-numeric results are ignored); Python returns inf, nan, 'a1', None. Expect **MATCH (x == 5)** (today: DIFFERENT).
- Port Rust test `interpolation_and_clamping` (crates/sr-eval/src/value.rs): Pair '0,50%'->'100,100%' lerp 0.5 = (50px, 75%); colour #ff0000 -> (0,0,1,0) lerp 0.5 = [0.5,0,0.5,0.5]; strings step; unitDecimal clamp(1.2)=1.0; positiveInteger clamp(2.6)=3.0; var(--brand) token -> [1,0.5,0,1] Python: tests/test_facets.py: facets.kind_of('unitDecimal').clamp(1.2)==1.0, kind_of('xs:positiveInteger').clamp(2.6)==3.0; anim._mix on parsed pairs and colours for the lerp values.
- Port Rust test `values_clamp_to_their_schema_range` (crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs): back-out opacity 0->1 at t=0.5 is <= 1.0 Python: ev.num(l,'opacity',Ctx(0.5,0.5)) <= 1.0
- Port Rust test `animated_z_restacks_siblings_every_frame` (crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs): paint_order at 0.5 == [a,c,g,g1]; at 1.5 == [c,g,g1,a]; animated z 0->3 over 10 s: at 4 [a,b] (rounded 1 equals b's 1), at 6 [b,a] Python: framegraph node ids in order for the two scenes.

#### P2-EVAL-16 — Links: marker progress, trailing smoothing, per-component vectors, E14  

Size **M** · track **P2-B** · rows: EVAL-33 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-13

**Goal.** link maps its source through scale, offset, then min/max clamp, per component for vector sources, with delay and smoothing as in sr-eval: sources are nodeId.property, param:name, audio:track[:low|mid|high] and marker:id, anything else is E14; marker:id yields the normalised progress through the marker's @duration (0 before the marker, 1 after marker+duration or when duration is 0, linear in between) instead of seconds since the marker; param:name feeds the typed parameter (numbers, lists); delay shifts the sample time back by @delay; smoothing averages n = clamp(round(smoothing*fps),1,240) samples at t-delay - smoothing*i/n for i in 0..n-1 (trailing window of exactly @smoothing seconds, Python uses 9 samples including the far end); a link on a property resamples the SOURCE property at those times (frame cache not required but shared dependencies must stay linear: see P2-EVAL-13).

**Python files.** scenerender/evaluator.py::_link, _link_source, parameter; scenerender/eval_diagnostics.py (use)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_link_expression_and_scale_alias; tests/test_expression_clocks.py::test_link_delay_and_smoothing_resample_source_frames (hard-codes np.linspace(...,9) samples: rewrite to the Rust sample set)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs::link_value; crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::compile_pending / resolve_links (link sources, E14); crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::links_scale_offset_clamp_delay_and_markers; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::remaining_error_codes (E14)

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/links.scene.xml` at t=[0.4, 0.6, 0.9]: P <parameters><param id='p' type='number' default='4'/></parameters> A <markers><marker id='m' time='5' duration='2'/></markers><composition><layer id='src' asset='img'><animate property='x'><key time='0' value='0'/><key time='10' value='100'/></animate></layer><layer id='dst' asset='img'><link property='x' source='src.x' scale='2' offset='1' max='150' delay='1'/><link property='rotation' source='param:p' scale='10'/><link property='opacity' source='marker:m'/><link property='y' source='src.x' smoothing='1'/></layer></composition></scene> (lk1.xml). Rust t=4: x 61, rotation 40, opacity 0.0, y 35.5 (10 samples, 40-4.5*... mean of src.x(4-i/10)); t=6: x 101, opacity 0.5, y 55.5; t=9: x 150 (clamped), opacity 1.0, y 85.5. Python: opacity -1, 1, 4 (seconds since marker) and y 35.0/55.0/85.0. Expect **MATCH** (today: DIFFERENT (opacity, y)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/errors-E14.scene.xml` at t=[0]: layer l with <link property='x' source='audio:music:bass'/> (bass is not a band) -> {E14}; source='nodeonly' (no dot/prefix) -> {E14}. Expect **code set {E14}** (today: DIFFERENT).
- Port Rust test `links_scale_offset_clamp_delay_and_markers` (crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs): t=4: dst.x=61 (src.x at t-1=30, *2+1), rotation 40, opacity 0; t=6: opacity 0.5; t=9: x 150 clamped to max Python: links scene; framegraph props at 4, 6, 9.
- Eval-level: eval_parity on links scene fields props.x/y/rotation/opacity.

**Risks.** Existing Python test pins the 9-sample smoothing; migrate it. marker@duration exists in both schemas. Per-component clamp changes vector links only.

#### P2-EVAL-17 — Motion paths: default end, key-parameter easing, analytic orientation, E15  

Size **S** · track **P2-B** · rows: EVAL-32 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-02, P2-EVAL-20

**Goal.** motionPath progress runs over [start,end] where end defaults to the node's own end, else the PROJECT duration (Python uses start+1); u is clamped and is 1 when end<=start; the easing is the full curve resolution of sr-eval (any curveType incl. cubic-bezier defaults), an animated 'progress' replaces it; auto-orient uses the analytic tangent angle plus orientOffset (including at the path ends, no finite difference, no None); constantSpeed=false samples per segment; SVG path data incl. arcs, S/T reflections and relative commands parse like path.rs, a path that does not parse is E15. Arc flattening differences below 0.05 px are accepted.

**Python files.** scenerender/evaluator.py::_motion_path, motion_path_angle; scenerender/geometry.py::PathSampler, parse_svg_path, _arc_to_beziers (group B owns geometry.py for this package only)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_svg_path_and_trim_and_sampler

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/path.rs::MotionPath::parse, sample, flatten; crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs::Frame::local (motion branch); crates/sr-eval/src/path.rs tests lines_and_relative_commands, curves_and_arcs, subpaths_flatten_separately, errors; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::motion_paths_and_camera_switching

#### P2-EVAL-18 — Layer media: negative speed, clock edge cases and fit/crop/flip placement  

Size **M** · track **P2-B** · rows: EVAL-17, EVAL-19 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-01

**Goal.** Local time maps to source time with rate = |speed|/timeStretch and reverse flipped by a negative speed (Python currently returns negative source times); clipIn offset, loop = extra plays then hold the end, reverse within the length, freezeAt holds one source frame for the whole window and frozen layers do not end with their media, timeRemap keys give source seconds, a clip ends when its media runs out. The same mapping applies to instances (symbol time). Python keeps honouring instance clipOut (sr-eval ignores it). Fit placement of media in the box equals layout.rs::place: contain, cover (focusX/focusY share the cut-away part), fill, scale-down (never enlarge), none, contain-blur (contain over a cover fill), with cropLeft/Top/Right/Bottom and flipX/flipY applied to the cropped region (flip swaps uv corners). fit requires boxWidth and boxHeight (validation C16); with fit='none' the box does not change the natural size.

**Python files.** scenerender/nodes/core.py::media_time, fit_matrix, render_layer, render_instance, instance_time; scenerender/evaluator.py::instance_time, enter_instance; scenerender/compositor.py::_media_box (group C file: only if the fit='none' + box case needs a change, coordinate)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_clip_ends_when_its_media_runs_out; tests/test_core.py::test_instance_clock_and_overrides; tests/test_reference_source_projection.py (instances with speed, fit)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::MediaClock::map; crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::layer_clock and symbol_clock; crates/sr-eval/src/layout.rs::place (FitSpec, Content); crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::layer_source_time_speed_reverse_loop_freeze_and_remap; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::a_negative_speed_plays_backwards; crates/sr-eval/src/layout.rs tests::fit_modes; crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::fit_cover_contain_and_blur_fill

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/media-negative-speed.scene.xml` at t=[0.025, 0.075, 0.1, 0.14, 0.16]: P A <symbols><symbol id='two' duration='2'><layer id='f' asset='img' start='1.5' end='2'/></symbol></symbols><composition><layer id='back' asset='vid' speed='-1'/><layer id='fast' asset='vid' speed='-2' clipIn='1'/><layer id='twice' asset='vid' speed='-1' reverse='true'/><instance id='i' symbol='two' speed='-1'/></composition></scene> (neg.xml).
