#### P2-EVAL-19 — Node seeds and copy ids: hash_str/mix64 primitive, object3D copy ids 'id[k]', per-copy seeds  

Size **S** · track **P2-B** · rows: EVAL-41, EVAL-12 · needs schema adoption: **yes** · depends on: P2-EVAL-01, P2-EVAL-07

**Goal.** scenerender.noise gains mix64 (SplitMix64 finaliser), hash(words) and hash_str(s) = mix64(FNV-1a-64 of the UTF-8 bytes) identical to crates/sr-eval/src/rng.rs, and Evaluator.node_seed(el, scope) returns @seed when authored, else hash_str(effective scoped id); repeat copies and object3D copies derive theirs from the copy id ('r[1]', 'p[2]'). object3D with @instances=N exposes N copies with effective ids 'id' (copy 0), 'id[1]'... each evaluated with its own index/count (already true in three/scene.py) and addressable by prop('p[1].x') and by the FrameGraph (kind object3D, repeat [k,N]). Expression `seed` stays the @seed of the expression else the project seed (already equal). Existing CRC-32 element_seed users (text animators, wiggly selectors, scramble, effects) keep Evaluator.seed_for; emitters/physics/deform seeds are switched by the SIM/FX packages, not here.

**Python files.** scenerender/noise.py (mix64, hash, hash_str); scenerender/evaluator.py::seed_for, seed_for_value (add node_seed); scenerender/three/scene.py::_instances, build_object (copy ids); scenerender/schema (object3D @seed attribute, via Bob's schema adoption)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_particle_evaluation.py:312-317 (CRC-32 emitter seed pins; SIM-owned, do not change here)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/rng.rs::hash_str, hash, mix64; crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::instantiate (node seed) and ::repeat (copy.seed); crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::instantiate (copies for object3D @instances); crates/sr-vector/src/d24.rs::element_seed (the CRC-32 rule); crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::object3d_instances_evaluate_once_per_index; crates/sr-eval/src/rng.rs tests::streams_are_addressable_and_stable

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/object3d-instances.scene.xml` at t=[0]: P <composition><object3D id='p' primitive='plane' instances='4' y='5'><expression property='x'>170 + index * 100 + count</expression></object3D></composition></scene>. Rust: 4 object3D nodes p, p[1], p[2], p[3] with repeat [k,4] and props.x = 174, 274, 374, 474. Expect **MATCH** (today: DIFFERENT (Python draws one node with N GPU instances; ids/props not exposed per copy)).
- Port Rust test `streams_are_addressable_and_stable (golden part)` (crates/sr-eval/src/rng.rs): hash(&[1,2,3]) == 0xd56d0264932a233f (verified: my Python port of mix64/hash reproduces it); Stream(1,2,3).at(4,5) == 0.1493229690615926 Python: tests/test_noise_hash.py: noise.hash([1,2,3]) == 0xd56d0264932a233f; and reference values computed from the definition: hash_str('') == 0xf52a15e9a9b5e89b, hash_str('a') == 0x02c0bdbf481420f8, hash_str('r[1]/l') == 0xfac49df890dbeb83, hash_str('p[1]') == 0x2729f997f6f66fed.
- Port Rust test `object3d_instances_evaluate_once_per_index` (crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs): 4 copies p, p[1], p[2], p[3]; x = 174 + 100k; repeat == [k,4] Python: evaluate_frame nodes filtered by kind object3D.

#### P2-EVAL-20 — Curves: cubic-bezier default handles, spring segments, steps at u=0, Catmull-Rom/TCB non-uniform tangents  

Size **M** · track **P2-C** · rows: EVAL-25, EVAL-26, EVAL-27 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-00

**Goal.** Per-segment curves equal sr-eval (39 of 41 already match numerically, measured at 10 times each): cubic-bezier without @bezier uses AE handles x = clamp(influence,0,1), y = influence*speed with defaults influence 1/3 and speed 1 for BOTH the key's easeOut and the next key's easeIn (Python defaults speed to 0, which makes an absent handle ease instead of staying linear); @bezier must have x1,x2 in [0,1] (E04 via P2-EVAL-14); spring runs in seconds with the underdamped, critically damped and OVERDAMPED solutions (Python reuses the critical formula for zeta>1) and its residual 1-spring(duration) is spread linearly (progress = spring(tau) + (1-spring(d))*tau/d) so the value lands exactly on the next key; steps with stepPosition start gives 1/n at u=0; catmull-rom/tcb use Hermite tangents scaled by 2*dt_next/(dt_prev+dt_next) / 2*dt_prev/(dt_prev+dt_next) for non-uniform key spacing, end keys use the single available difference, TCB parameters are read from the key (tension, continuity, bias) with the continuity sign see rust_may_be_wrong.

**Python files.** scenerender/curves.py::cubic_bezier, spring, steps; scenerender/anim.py::_ease_for, _tcb_tangents, _hermite, sample  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_curves_endpoints_and_shape; tests/test_core.py::test_linear_hold_steps_and_extrapolation; tests/test_core.py::test_catmull_rom_passes_through_keys (uniform spacing: unchanged: 5.625)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/curve.rs::resolve, cubic_bezier, spring, spring_segment, hermite, penner, parse_bezier; crates/sr-eval/src/channel.rs::tangents, segment; crates/sr-eval/src/curve.rs tests every_curve_starts_at_zero_and_ends_at_one, known_values, springs, bezier_strings; crates/sr-eval/src/channel.rs tests::curves_markers_and_splines

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/curves-41.scene.xml` at t=[0, 0.025, 0.05, 0.125, 0.25, 0.375, 0.45, 0.495, 0.5, 0.75]: P(fps 100, duration 2) with one layer per curveType value (the first 41 enumerations of curveType: step hold steps linear ease-in ease-out ease-in-out cubic-bezier catmull-rom tcb spring and the 30 Penner names): <layer id='c{k}' asset='img'><animate property='x' defaultInterpolation='{curve}'><key time='0' value='0'/><key time='1' value='100'/></animate></layer> (curves41.xml). Rust vs Python at t=0,0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9,0.99,1.0,1.5: all curves equal except cubic-bezier (Rust 5.0 at 0.05 and 10.0 at 0.1 i.e. linear; Python 0.725, 2.8) and spring (Rust 10.4297 at 0.05, 34.0083 at 0.1, 102.2817 at 0.25; Python 10.4405, 34.03, 102.336). Expect **MATCH on all 41** (today: 2 DIFFERENT (cubic-bezier, spring)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/splines-handles.scene.xml` at t=[0.05, 0.09, 0.13, 0.15]: P A <composition> layers on x: 'ae2' key0 value 0 interpolation='cubic-bezier' easeOut='0.8,0' + key 2s value 100 (no easeIn); 'cr' defaultInterpolation catmull-rom keys (0,0)(1,50)(4,20)(5,90); 'tcb' keys (0,0)(1,50)(4,20)(5,90) interpolation='tcb' with tension 0.5 bias 0.3 on the first two and tension -0.3 bias -0.5 on the third (kf2.xml). #### P2-EVAL-21 — Extrapolation modes and loopIn/loopOut over the first/last n key intervals  

Size **M** · track **P2-C** · rows: EVAL-29 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-20

**Goal.** anim.sample extrapolation equals Channel::extrapolate: hold; linear continues with the end velocity of the ACTUAL curve (finite difference 1e-3 clamped to the segment: a hold ends still, steps continue along the chord, smooth ends keep their velocity) instead of the chord between the last two keys; loop, ping-pong and offset repeat the key span (offset adds cycles*(last-first)). anim.eval_loop(keys, t, out, kind, count) implements loopOut/loopIn(kind in cycle|pingpong|offset|continue, n key intervals, 0 = all) and applies ONLY outside the loop range (inside it the normal value is returned: Python's loopOut('cycle', 1) wrongly resamples the last n keys everywhere and returns the first of them for earlier times); the expression closure in evaluator.py delegates to it (done in P2-EVAL-12) and uses the last animate channel of the property.

**Python files.** scenerender/anim.py::sample, new eval_loop  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_linear_hold_steps_and_extrapolation; tests/test_expression_clocks.py::test_loop_functions_use_animation_time_base_and_vector_component

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/channel.rs::extrapolate, slope_value, eval_loop, eval; crates/sr-eval/src/channel.rs tests linear_hold_and_extrapolation, loops_over_odd_counts_and_tiny_spans, linear_extrapolation_past_a_jump

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/extrapolation-loops.scene.xml` at t=[0, 0.05, 0.09, 0.13, 0.2, 0.25, 0.35, 0.45, 0.55, 0.7]: P A <composition> on x: 'lin' extrapolateBefore/After='linear' defaultInterpolation='ease-in-out' keys (1,10)(2,30)(3,35): Rust 9.9622 at 0 (velocity of the eased segment), Python -10; 'lin2' linear with keys (1,10,hold)(2,30)(3,35,steps=4)(4,55): Rust 10.0 before; 'lp' loop/loop keys (1,10)(2,30)(3,35); 'pp' ping-pong; 'of' offset; plus expressions on keys (0,0)(1,10)(2,30): lo1 loopOut('cycle',1): Rust 0 at 0, 5 at 0.5, 9 at 0.9 (normal value inside), Python 10; lo2 loopOut('pingpong'); lo3 loopOut('offset'); lo4 loopOut('continue'); li1 keys (1,0)(2,10)(3,30) loopIn('cycle',1): Rust 20 at 2.5, 30 at 3.0; Python 10; li2 loopIn('offset') (kf2.xml). Expect **MATCH except lp at exactly t=3.0 (see rust_may_be_wrong, gate excludes that instant)** (today: DIFFERENT on lin, lin2, lo1, li1).
- Port Rust test `linear_hold_and_extrapolation` (crates/sr-eval/src/channel.rs): keys (0,0)(1,10)(2,10 hold)(3,30): hold ext: c(0.5)=5, c(2.9)=10, c(3.0)=30, c(-1)=0; linear/loop: l(-1) = -10, l(3.5)=l(0.5); pingpong p(3.5)=p(2.5); offset o(3.5)=o(0.5)+30; eval_loop(4.5,out,cycle,1)=c(2.5) Python: anim.sample with before/after modes on those keys; anim.eval_loop for the last assert.
- Port Rust test `linear_extrapolation_past_a_jump` (crates/sr-eval/src/channel.rs): hold segment ends still (c(3)=20 and loopOut continue = 20); steps=4 segment continues along its chord (c(3)=30); a smooth end keeps its velocity (-1 -> 0 for hold start, 3 -> 30) Python: three key lists exactly as in the Rust test.
- Port Rust test `loops_over_odd_counts_and_tiny_spans` (crates/sr-eval/src/channel.rs): loopOut('cycle', all) at 2.5 on keys 0,1,2 equals the usize::MAX count variant; loopIn at -0.5 = c(1.5); spans of 1e-13 stay finite for every kind Python: eval_loop with count=0 and a huge count; tiny span keys: all four kinds return finite values.
- Eval-level: eval_parity props.x on extrapolation scene at times -0.0,0.5,0.9,1.3,1.5,2.0,2.5,3.0,3.5,4.5,5.5,7.0 (measured: pp, of, lo2, lo3, lo4, li2 already match).

**Risks.** loop/ping-pong at exactly the last key time differs by design of Rust (see rust_may_be_wrong): do not copy blindly. expression loopOut in evaluator.py is group B's file, hence the dependency from P2-EVAL-12.

#### P2-EVAL-22 — Group layout: grid columns as wide as the widest child, content-hugging groups, stretch  

Size **S** · track **P2-C** · rows: EVAL-20 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-01

**Goal.** Group (and sequence) layout equals layout.rs::arrange: row/column place children by their scaled bounding boxes with gap and padding; justify start/center/end/space-between/space-around/space-evenly on the main axis (space-between with one child = start); alignItems start/center/end/stretch on the cross axis; a child's own x/y offset it from its slot; stack overlays all children (justify applies on x, alignItems on y); GRID: column widths = widest child of the column, row heights = tallest of the row, justify distributes the columns on x, y offset only for center/end, cell cross alignment per row; a group WITHOUT width/height hugs its content (available space = content size, so justify/align offsets vanish) instead of using the parent box (Python centres inside the parent); stretch scales the child about its box origin by avail/size (factor on the cross axis, Python resizes shapes instead). Baseline: Python's true first-baseline alignment for text is kept (sr-eval treats baseline as start).

**Python files.** scenerender/layout.py::flex_layout, _cross, _baseline; scenerender/compositor.py::layout_positions, local_matrix, node_size (stretch)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_layout_clocks.py (3 tests: clocks and expression-driven layout mode must stay); tests/test_paint_layout_conformance.py::test_group_stretch_fills_allocated_cross_axis; tests/test_paint_layout_conformance.py::test_stretch_reflows_group_children

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/layout.rs::arrange, justify, cross; crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs::Frame::arrange; crates/sr-eval/src/layout.rs tests::flex_rows_columns_and_grids, alignment; crates/sr-eval/tests/semantics.rs::flex_layout_alignment_and_fit_boxes

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/layout-grid-flex.scene.xml` at t=[0]: P <composition> six groups of rect shapes (lay2.xml): g1 layout='grid' gridColumns='2' gap='10' (no size) with a 20x10, b 30x20, c 10x5; g2 x=0 y=100 w200 h100 row gap10 pad5 justify='space-between' alignItems='stretch' with d 20x10, e 30x45; g3 x=300 y=100 100x100 column gap10 pad5 justify='end' alignItems='end' f 20x10 g 30x20; g4 x=500 y=100 100x100 stack pad5 justify='center' alignItems='center' h 20x10 i 30x20; g5 x=600 y=100 200x100 row justify='space-evenly' j 20x10 k 30x20 l 30x20; g6 x=0 y=300 200x100 grid gridColumns='2' gap='10' pad='5' justify='center' alignItems='center' m 20x10 n 30x20 o 10x5. Rust origins: g1: a (0,0) b (30,0) c (0,30); g2: d (5,105) abcd d=9, e (165,105) d=2; g3: f (375,155) g (365,175); g4: h (540,145) i (535,140); g5: j (630,100) k (680,100) l (740,100); g6: m (70,337.5) n (100,332.5) o (70,362.5). Python today: g1 b (505,0) c (0,255); g6 m (40,320) n (135,315) o (45,372.5); the other groups already match. Expect **MATCH; pixel gate with render_parity at t=0 expects MATCH (shapes only)** (today: DIFFERENT on g1 and g6 (grid), g2 stretch representation).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/layout-hug.scene.xml` at t=[0]: P <composition><group id='g' layout='row' gap='10' justify='center' alignItems='center'><shape id='a' shape='rect' width='20' height='10'/><shape id='b' shape='rect' width='30' height='20'/></group></composition></scene> (lay4.xml). Rust: g hugs content: a origin (0,5), b (30,0). Python centres in the 1000x500 parent: a (470,245), b (500,240). Expect **MATCH** (today: DIFFERENT).
- Port Rust test `flex_rows_columns_and_grids` (crates/sr-eval/src/layout.rs): row start/center on 200x100 container gap10 pad5 children 20x10 and 30x50: slots (5,45),(35,25); row space-between stretch children 20x10 and 30x45: second x = 5+190-30, first stretch [1,9]; column end/end 100x100: second slot (65,75); grid cols=2 gap10 no container sizes 20x10,30x20,10x5: slots (0,0),(30,0),(0,30) Python: call flex_layout through a minimal rc built from a scene (tests/test_layout_clocks.py style) or test the new pure function layout.arrange(spec, container, gap, pad, sizes) that the package extracts; numbers verbatim.

- Eval-level: eval_parity world origin (and abcd for stretch) of every child.

**Risks.** Content-hugging changes the position of every layout group without a size in existing documents (spec-faithful but visible). Rust treats alignItems='baseline' as start (layout.rs::cross): keep Python's real baseline. Stretch as a transform scale changes stroke widths of stretched shapes: check pixel gate.

#### P2-EVAL-24 — Layout selection and reframe: reflow size, crop/fit/fit-blur placement, fit-blur background  

Size **S** · track **P2-C** · rows: EVAL-23 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P2-EVAL-06, P2-EVAL-23

**Goal.** Selecting a layout (CLI --layout) changes frame size and safe area: reframe='reflow' (default) re-flows the scene at the layout size (FrameGraph size = layout size); crop, fit and fit-blur keep the composition at the PROJECT size (FrameGraph size stays 1000x500) and delivery places it: crop scales to cover and shares the cut-away part by focusX/focusY, fit scales to fit and CENTRES (focus ignored, Python applies focus to fit too), fit-blur draws the fitted frame over a cover copy placed with the focus, the copy being the frame averaged in 8x8 blocks then a separable 13-tap Gaussian of sigma 3 blocks (24 px) with no darkening (Python uses gaussian(max(4, 0.02*width)) and multiplies by 0.85). Unknown layout is E10; layout overrides apply last (P2-EVAL-06); the layout's safeArea wins.

**Python files.** scenerender/compositor.py::reframe_matrix, _fit_blur_backdrop, render_frame; scenerender/document.py::_project (read; override order is P2-EVAL-06)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-eval/src/program.rs::template (layout / Reframe branch) and struct Reframe; crates/sr-gpu/src/output.rs::Placement (Fit, Crop, FitBlur; place); crates/sr-gpu/src/output_blur.wgsl (8x8 downsample, sigma 3 texels); crates/sr-deliver/src/pipeline.rs::Video::placement (focus only for crop and fit-blur); crates/sr-deliver/tests/segments.rs::fit_blur_fills_the_margins_with_the_picture

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/eval/layout-reframe.scene.xml (sidecar args: --layout sq / rf / fit1 / crop1)` at t=[0]: P <layouts><layout id='sq' width='500' height='500' reframe='fit-blur' focusX='0.25'/><layout id='rf' width='400' height='800'><override target='a' property='x' value='50%'/></layout></layouts> + two more layouts reframe='fit' focusX='0.1' and reframe='crop' focusX='0.1'; A <composition><layer id='a' asset='img' x='10%' y='50%'/> plus a 1000x500 gradient/checker background shape</composition></scene> (lay3.xml extended). Pixel gate via render_parity with the sidecar. Expect **eval MATCH on size/world; pixels MATCH (PSNR>=45) for crop and fit, CLOSE (>=30) for fit-blur until the blur is ported, then MATCH** (today: fit with focus != 0.5 DIFFERENT; fit-blur CLOSE/DIFFERENT (0.85 darkening)).
- Port Rust test `fit_blur_fills_the_margins_with_the_picture` (crates/sr-deliver/tests/segments.rs): a fit-blur frame in a different aspect fills the margins with a blurred copy of the picture (not black) Python: tests/test_reframe.py: render a gradient scene with the fit-blur layout and assert the margin pixels are neither black nor equal to the fitted edge colour; plus matrix tests: reframe_matrix('crop') scale = max(kx,ky) with focus, ('fit') = min(kx,ky) centred, ignoring focus.
- Eval-level: eval --layout L size and node world for sq and rf layouts: Rust sq (fit-blur 500x500) size [1000,500], a origin (100,250); rf (reflow 400x800 with override x=50%) size [400,800], a origin (200,400).

**Risks.** Rust's fit-blur uses GPU texture reduction (8x8 block average, 13 taps): cairo/numpy needs an explicit box-average then gaussian(sigma=24 px at full size scaled by output scale). Delivery placement may belong to the output area: coordinate before editing compositor.render_frame.

#### P3-COMP-02 — Blend: add/linear-dodge unclamped with over-alpha, input clamping for all other modes  

Size **S** · track **P3-B** · rows: COMP-03, COMP-04 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-01

**Goal.** add and linear-dodge compute colour as the unclamped premultiplied sum dst+src and alpha as src_a + dst_a*(1-src_a) (Rust: fixed-function One/One for colour, premul alpha for alpha; HDR values above 1 survive). plus-lighter stays min(dst+src,1) on all four channels. Every other W3C/AE mode takes straight cb and cs clamped to [0,1] before B(cb,cs); the exempt set is exactly add(2), linear-dodge(17), multiply(4), difference(7) (and plus-lighter/stencils/silhouettes/alpha-add/behind, which never reach blend_fn). After the change screen of cb=2,cs=0.5 equals the cb=1 result, and 'red + red(add) then multiply by 50% grey' gives 1.0 not 0.5.

**Python files.** scenerender/blend.py: _add (split into _add_hdr for add/linear-dodge vs plus-lighter), _separable (np.clip of cb/cs before fn except exempt set), _divide eps (1e-6 vs 1e-30), blend_func registration; scenerender/gpucomp.py::_MODES (only normal/behind run on GPU: no change, verify supports() stays false for add)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_blend.py::test_add_family_clamps (must be split: plus-lighter keeps [1.0,0.5,0.2,1]/[0.4,0.4,0.4,0.8]; add/linear-dodge become [1.3,0.5,0.2,1] and alpha 0.4+0.4*0.6=0.64); tests/test_blend.py::test_results_stay_valid_premultiplied (premultiplied colour may now exceed alpha for add); tests/test_blend.py::test_separable_edge_cases, test_w3c_alpha_model (iterate BLEND_FUNCS; add new HDR-clamp cases)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/shaders.wgsl::blend_fn (clamp block, exempt modes 2,17,4,7) and ::composite (plus-lighter case 3u, default alpha as+ab(1-as)); crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::fixed_function_blend (modes 2|17), 'additive' pipeline: colour One/One, alpha premul; crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::all_blend_modes_match_the_reference_formulas, divide_takes_clamped_inputs, partial_alpha_uses_the_general_compositing_formula, non_separable_modes_use_w3c_luminance

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/comp/blend_hdr.scene.xml (NEW)` at t=[0.25]: 64x32 bg #000000 duration2. Left half: opaque red shape 32x32 #FF0000, same-size #FF0000 blend=add (cb becomes 2.0), then #BCBCBC blend=multiply (lin ~0.5): Rust R=1.0 -> 255. Right half: #FFFFFF shape, #FFFFFF blend=add (cb=2), #808080 blend=subtract: Rust clamps cb to 1 -> 229 (guards the clamp; today it passes by accident because add clamps). Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (19.4 dB; left pixel (10,10): Rust 255,0,0 vs Python 188,0,0)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/comp/blend_add_alpha.scene.xml (NEW)` at t=[0.25]: 64x32 bg #336699. Left: #808080 opacity .5 shape then identical shape blend=add. Right: same pair with blend=plus-lighter. Rust left pixel (130,137,148) (alpha .75 lets bg through); plus-lighter side (128,128,128) already matches. Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (29.1 dB; Python left pixel 128,128,128 because alpha sums to 1)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/comp/blend_grid.scene.xml (NEW, generated by gen_comp_probes.py)` at t=[0.25]: bg #404040 opaque; backdrop shape #4080C0; for each of 33 non-stencil modes three 8x8 cells in rows: opaque #C86432, #C86432 at opacity .5, #C8643280 fill alpha; project width 33*8, height 24. Regression guard for all other modes. Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB (stays MATCH)** (today: MATCH (57.7 dB; max 2 levels in hard-light/linear-light/pin-light)).
- Port Rust test `all_blend_modes_match_the_reference_formulas` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs): 35 modes vs CPU reference within 3e-3*max(1,|w|) for opaque cb=(0.0513,0.2159,0.5271)/cs=(0.5776,0.1274,0.0319); stencil-alpha keeps cb inside layer, 0 outside Python: parametrize over blend.BLENDS; build 1x1 premultiplied dst/src arrays, call blend.BLENDS[mode](dst, src) (stencils via composite() with whole_tile), compare to rs_reference.reference(mode,b,s); same tolerance.
- Port Rust test `divide_takes_clamped_inputs` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs): divide gives 1 where cs=0 and cb>0; layer 'half' over grey: (1-as)cb + as*cb/1 Python: direct arrays through blend.BLENDS['divide'] with HDR cb=2: expect min(1/cs,1) semantics of clamped cb.
- Port Rust test `partial_alpha_uses_the_general_compositing_formula` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs): half-alpha white multiply/screen over opaque #4080C0: co=(1-as)cb+as*B(cb,cs), as=128/255 Python: direct premultiplied arrays; tolerance 3e-3.

#### P3-COMP-03 — Isolation predicate: opacity multiplies down through pass-through groups  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: COMP-01 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-01

**Goal.** A group (or sequence) is isolated only for isolate=true, effects, any mask child, clip, matte, blend != normal (plus instance/include as today). Opacity<1 is not a trigger: _passthrough passes fold_opacity*group_opacity (evaluated at the group's node context, animated opacity included, <=1e-4 skips the subtree) to render_children, so overlapping children show through. threeD is removed from the predicate ONLY if P3-COMP-05 decides to copy Rust (see there); this package changes opacity only. Overlap of two 50% white children inside group opacity .5 gives 0.75 linear (225 sRGB) instead of 0.5.

**Python files.** scenerender/nodes/core.py::is_isolated (drop the opacity<1 clause); scenerender/compositor.py::_passthrough (multiply fold_opacity by the group's opacity; early-out when <=1e-4), ::_isolation_redundant.plain (drop opacity/threeD), ::render_children (run cache requires fold_opacity==1.0: keep), ::apply_adjustment (already multiplies fold_opacity); docs/RUNTIME.md and camera.py module docstring (group opacity wording)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_stacking_z_and_group_opacity (overlap pixel (70,70) is pinned to (255,128,128) for the isolated reading; becomes (255,191,191) with linearLight=false); tests/test_particle_ancestors.py lines ~79,112,178,189,199 (groups with opacity .6-.8 around particles/walls: review whether expectations assumed a flattened group); tests/test_reference_source_projection.py::test_source_appearance_is_evaluated_before_parent_compositing and the 'group' parametrization at l.35 (matte keeps those groups isolated; confirm unchanged); tests/test_raster_cache.py (runs inside translucent groups no longer cached as a run: confirm still green)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::isolated (l.2006-2022) and module doc 'Isolation'; crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::emit (op = n.world_opacity / iso_op; fully transparent isolated returns early); README.md l.115 'Compositing is isolated only where the result depends on it'; crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::isolated_groups_composite_as_one_layer, isolated_content_fading_in_from_zero_is_not_cached_empty, masks_apply_to_groups_without_a_box, clipped_instance_and_moved_group_reuse_the_offscreen

Overlap pixel (30,10): Rust 225, isolated Python 188. Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB (scratch experiment produced 99.0)** (today: DIFFERENT (26.4 dB; 11% of pixels off by >2)).

Children must project through the frame camera at world opacity. Expect **CLOSE >= 34 dB (scratch experiment 36.4; residual is 1-px edge AA of projected quads, not geometry)** (today: DIFFERENT (24.0 dB)).

#### P3-COMP-04 — Depth sort of consecutive threeD siblings at every parent  

Size **S** · track **P3-A** · rows: COMP-37 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-03

**Goal.** At the composition root, in every group (isolated or not) and in every instance, each maximal run of consecutive siblings with threeD=true is drawn in descending zDepth order (stable; larger zDepth = farther = first); 2D nodes and object3D/camera/light keep their slots and break runs; the sort follows animated zDepth each frame. The existing collapsed-group depth_sort hook (camera-space depth, includes object3D) stays for collapse=true groups only.

**Python files.** scenerender/compositor.py::render_children (apply run sort after child_order and before the collapsed-group hook), ::child_order; scenerender/camera.py::depth_sort (keep for collapse=true; add run_sort helper next to it reading zDepth via rc.ev.num(c,'zDepth',node ctx,0))  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_camera3d.py::test_depth_sort_hook_orders_farthest_first (collapse hook stays; unchanged); tests/test_3d_render.py and tests/fixtures/scene3d_layers.xml (check for documents relying on document order of threeD layers)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::depth_sorted (l.2922; called at l.2170, 2214, 3199); crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::emit; crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::layers_in_2_5d_project_and_sort_by_depth, animated_z_restacks_with_warm_caches; crates/sr-eval/src/eval.rs FrameNode.three_d = [zDepth, rotationX, rotationY] only for threeD=true nodes

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/comp/iso_depth_sort.scene.xml (NEW)` at t=[0.25]: 64x64 bg #000 duration2, no camera. Root: red 30x30 threeD zDepth -50 at (10,10) declared BEFORE white 30x30 threeD zDepth 100 at (20,20): Rust draws white first so red covers it. Group g with green threeD zDepth -80 (2,40,20x20) before blue threeD zDepth 200 (10,44,20x16): same rule inside a plain group. Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (17.7 dB; document order stacks white over red)).

#### P3-COMP-05 — threeD groups and collapse: decision, flagged divergence and camera routing for pass-through groups  

Size **S** · track **P3-A** · rows: COMP-02 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-03

**Goal.** Explicit, documented behaviour for threeD on groups. Verified Rust behaviour (rendered on this machine): a threeD group that is NOT isolated ignores its own zDepth/rotationX/rotationY (children draw flat, group transform dropped); when it is isolated (isolate=true, blend, effects, mask...) the whole offscreen is projected as one plane; `collapse` is parsed but never read by the renderer. Python today projects the group as one plane (flatten) and implements collapse=true (children share the group's camera space). This package (a) keeps threeD as an isolation trigger for groups ONLY so documented Python/schema behaviour survives, unless the coordinator decides to copy Rust, in which case it is a two-line removal verified by the scratch experiment (non-isolated threeD group then matches Rust at PSNR >= 99); (b) guarantees that threeD CHILDREN of pass-through groups project through the frame camera with the group's 2D world matrix and folded opacity (after P3-COMP-03 this measured 36.4 dB); (c) records the divergence in registry notes, docs/RUNTIME.md and PARITY-MATRIX.

**Python files.** scenerender/nodes/core.py::is_isolated (threeD clause kept for groups, documented); scenerender/compositor.py::_passthrough, ::_render_node_once (camera hook uses world matrix incl. pass-through parents); scenerender/camera.py: _collapsed_ancestors, is_threed, camera_hook, module docstring l.60-68; scenerender/registry.py FEATURES entries for threeD/collapse notes (via nodes/core.py)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_camera3d.py::test_collapse_composes_group_3d_transform (unchanged if option (a)); tests/test_reference_source_projection.py (threeD group with opacity .2 and matte, l.83); tests/test_particle_projection.py, tests/test_particle_projected_emitters.py (collapse placements)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::isolated, ::emit, ::draw_cmd (three = n.three_d + n.anchor only when the node is drawn itself); crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::layers_in_2_5d_project_and_sort_by_depth, positive_2_5d_rotations_turn_the_right_and_top_edges_away; README.md module doc '2.5D' in render.rs l.1-30

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/comp/group_3d_flags.scene.xml (NEW)` at t=[0.25]: 128x64 bg #000. g: group x10 y10 40x40 threeD rotationY50 isolate=true holding an orange 40x40 rect (Rust projects as a plane: 32 px wide); h: group x70 y10 40x40 threeD rotationY50 opacity .5 holding a 40x40 green rect (Rust: flat, 1600 px; non-isolated threeD ignored); a third group with collapse=true and a threeD child (Rust ignores collapse). Expect **g: MATCH >= 45 (already ~39 dB); h and collapse group: documented DIVERGENT-BY-DECISION (keep Python), listed with status DIFFERENT and the reason; flip to MATCH only if the coordinator chooses to copy Rust** (today: DIFFERENT (15.9 dB overall: isolated plane 39.1 dB after P3-COMP-03; non-isolated group 22.2 dB)).
- Port Rust test `positive_2_5d_rotations_turn_the_right_and_top_edges_away` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs): rotationY=40 makes right edge shorter than left by >2 px; rotationX=40 makes the top edge narrower than the bottom by >2 px Python: white shape 32x32 threeD in a 64x64 frame, frame_rgba(0) column/row scans as in the Rust test.

#### P3-COMP-06 — HLG transfer: scene-light decode normalised by the 75% reference level  

Size **S** · track **P3-C** · rows: COMP-18 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-01

**Goal.** decode('hlg') = inverse OETF / hlg_reference() (75% signal -> 1.0, no OOTF gamma 1.2, no 1000/203 factor) and encode('hlg') its inverse, so image/video/working-space HLG matches Rust (ramp: Python 26.5 dB -> >=45). encode_output()'s HDR delivery path (inverse OOTF at display peak) is unchanged because it serves deliverables, not compositing. PQ and the 15 other curves stay as they are (already within 1 code value).

#### P3-COMP-08 — linearLight=false in the working space's own encoding and primaries  

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: COMP-21 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-07, P3-COMP-03, P3-COMP-11, P3-COMP-22

**Goal.** With linearLight=false the compositor stores values encoded with the working space's default transfer (sRGB when the space is linear: srgb/display-p3 -> sRGB, rec709/rec2020 -> BT.1886 gamma 2.4, dci-p3 -> gamma 2.6, acescct -> ACEScct, linear spaces -> sRGB) in that space's primaries, instead of always sRGB-encoded Rec.709. Literals, gradients, images, mattes (luma weighting on stored values), blends and the final display conversion all use the same stored encoding. Default sRGB working space is bit-identical to today.

**Python files.** scenerender/color.py::working_primaries_for (drop the early return 'srgb' when not linear), add stored_transfer(doc), ::working_primaries; scenerender/raster.py::bgra_to_working/_bgra_to_working non-linear branch, ::rgba8_to_working, ::working_to_rgb8 (decode stored transfer, matrix back to sRGB, encode sRGB), ::color_to_working; scenerender/assets/image_pixels.py::input_pixels (replaces color.encode(rgb,'srgb') at l.58 with the stored transfer); scenerender/compositor.py:112-124 RenderContext.__post_init__ (remove `if self.linear else 'srgb'`) and ::_frame_target (disable the GL tile path when the stored transfer is not sRGB) - the only compositor.py edit in group C; apply after P3-COMP-03/22 are merged; scenerender/output.py rgb16/float branches and scenerender/gpucomp.py to_rgb8/yuv shaders (`linear` uniform): route non-sRGB stored encoding through raster.working_to_rgb8 (coordinate after P3-COMP-11); scenerender/kernels.py to_rgb8 (guard)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py (template uses linearLight=false everywhere with srgb working space: must stay bit-identical); tests/test_image_inputs.py::test_gamma_compositing_still_converts_linear_input; tests/test_color_mgmt.py::test_working_space_api; tests/test_gpucomp.py::test_frames_match_cpu_compositing

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/color.rs::Working::store, ::to_linear, ::to_display_srgb, ::from_literal, ::default_transfer; crates/sr-gpu/src/common.wgsl to_lin/from_lin (note: shader assumes sRGB for the stored encoding when linear_light=0; Rust CPU Working uses the working space's transfer: check the GPU path for the non-sRGB case before porting); crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::working_spaces_and_non_linear_compositing (second half)

#### P3-COMP-09 — Mask shapes as analytic local-space SDF coverage: rect ignores radius, rounded radius clamp, float coverage  

Size **M** · track **P3-B** · rows: COMP-10 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-01

**Goal.** Mask rect, ellipse and rounded-rect coverage is computed per pixel in the node's local space as float coverage clamp(0.5 - d/aa) (aa = max of the pixel footprint lengths in local units, i.e. max(|M^-1 col0|,|M^-1 col1|)), not by cairo into 8-bit A8; mask x/y/width/height default to the node box; type=rect ignores radius (Python currently promotes it to rounded-rect); rounded-rect radius is clamped to half the smaller side; expansion offsets the distance (rect + expansion grows round corners). Polygon/star/path kinds keep the existing cairo path until P3-COMP-10 but gain float local-space AA if cheap. A numba kernel (kernels.py) or vectorised numpy keeps 1080p masks cheap.

**Python files.** scenerender/masks.py::_mask_attrs (drop rect->rounded-rect promotion at l.18), ::mask_coverage (new analytic branch for rect/ellipse/rounded-rect using rc/M inverse), ::apply_masks; scenerender/geometry.py::shape_commands (unchanged for polygon/star/path); scenerender/kernels.py (optional mask_sdf kernel)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_mask_feather_sigma_and_add (feather 3 at scale 1, atol .012 must hold), ::test_mask_star_inner_radius_is_a_fraction; tests/test_effects_full.py (mask/matte tests), tests/test_particle_mattes.py

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/shaders.wgsl::sd_box, ::mask_distance (kind 0 rect r=0; kind 2 min(radius,min(half)); kind 1 ellipse (k-1)*min(r)), ::mask_value non-feather branch, ::mask_coverage (combine modes: first mode add/lighten/difference starts at 0); crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::masks_of (l.1441; box defaults, kind mapping); crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::masks_combine_modes_invert_and_feather, masks_add_as_a_plus_m_minus_am, specialized_ellipse_masks_match_general_masks_exactly

#### P3-COMP-10 — Mask feather as a Gaussian in the node's local space (anisotropic under node scale/rotation)  

Size **L** · track **P3-B** · rows: COMP-12 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-09

**Goal.** Feather is a Gaussian of standard deviation `feather` in LOCAL units, so under node scale/rotation the blur is scaled/anisotropic with the node (Python today blurs the rasterised 8-bit frame-space coverage with sigma=feather*frame scale only). Rect: exact product of edge integrals Phi((q+h)/s)-Phi((q-h)/s) per axis (erosion keeps a rect); ellipse and rounded-rect: 32-step row integral of Phi over +-4 sigma with half-width w(y) (rounded_half_width); polygon/star/path: Phi(-(d-expansion)/sigma) with signed distance to the flattened outline (winding or even-odd), sigma = max(feather, 0.4*aa) so a tiny feather still antialiases. Expansion applies before blur. Rust-tested numbers: layer at scale 4, rect mask, feather .5 => pixel centre 0.125 local units inside the edge covers 0.5987 +-0.02.

**Python files.** scenerender/masks.py::mask_coverage (feather branch: replace effects.gaussian on cov at l.66-68; add phi/erf vectorised via math.erf or numpy approximation A&S 7.1.26), new helpers rounded_half_width/row_integral; scenerender/geometry.py (flatten path/polygon to polylines for the distance branch; reuse shape_commands + a flatten helper); scenerender/kernels.py (optional numba kernel for the 32-step integral and edge distance)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_core.py::test_mask_feather_sigma_and_add (scale 1; erf profile, atol .012); tests/test_transitions.py (feather occurs in wipes: unrelated, confirm unaffected)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/shaders.wgsl::mask_value (feather branch), ::phi, ::rounded_half_width, ::mask_distance (polygon winding/evenodd); crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::masks_combine_modes_invert_and_feather (feather 0.5 local), specialized_ellipse_masks_match_general_masks_exactly; README.md l.119 (feather in local space, masks stay sharp under any transform)

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/comp/mask_feather_local.scene.xml (NEW)` at t=[0.25]: 128x64 bg #202020. (1) white 4x4 shape at x=4, scaleX=scaleY=4, mask rect 0,0,4,4 feather .5: Rust first-pixel coverage 0.604 (linear), Python 0.893; (2) orange 40x40 at (30,32) anchor 20,20 scaleX 1.6 scaleY .6 rotation 20, ellipse mask 4,4,32,32 feather 4 (anisotropic blur); (3) cyan 30x40 at (90,10) rounded-rect mask radius 10 feather 2 expansion -2; (4) a path mask 'M0 0 L20 0 L0 20 Z' feather 3 on a scaled shape. Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB; MATCH if both quantise equally** (today: DIFFERENT/CLOSE (combined c_mask2 control 34.6 dB, 16% pixels off by >2; r_feather strip: first pixel .893 vs .604)).

#### P3-COMP-11 — Project background as a gradient or pattern paint (background="url(#id)")  

Size **M** · track **P3-B** · rows: COMP-09 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-01

**Goal.** background="url(#id)" referencing linear/radial/conic/mesh gradients or an image pattern is rasterised over the document frame and composited BENEATH the finished layers last (behind, subtract, stencils act on layers only, as with a solid bg); alpha outputs (frame_rgba, rgba8/rgba16/floata kinds, alpha codecs) still omit the background; an unknown paint id or missing pattern asset gives transparent. #### P3-COMP-12 — Exact gradient stop evaluation: spaces, midpoints and animated stop ordering  

Size **M** · track **P3-B** · rows: COMP-28 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-01

**Goal.** Linear/radial gradient stops in linear, srgb, oklab, oklch (shorter hue) spaces and with midpoints evaluate exactly per device pixel (as the conic painter already does) instead of 12 cairo sub-stops; offsets are used in document order and a decreasing offset is raised to the previous one (no sorting, no [0,1] clamp; animated offsets can cross at runtime); the srgb/no-midpoint case keeps the cairo fast path. Result: gradient interpolation errors fall from a few 8-bit levels to <=1 and crossing animated stops match Rust.

**Python files.** scenerender/paint.py::_stops (remove sort/clamp; add monotonic raise), ::_add_stops (_SUBDIV path replaced), new device-space evaluator generalising _conic/_mix_color_array, ::_linear, ::_spread; scenerender/nodes/particle_paints.py (consumes paint.set_source; verify)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_paint_layout_conformance.py::test_conic_midpoint_changes_colour_distribution, test_gradient_dither_is_effective_and_deterministic, test_radial_focal_animation_does_not_require_base_attribute (patterns equal fx/fy fallbacks); tests/test_particle_paints.py

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/common.wgsl::eval_stops (midpoint u^(ln0.5/ln m), clamp .001..0.999), ::to_space, ::from_space, ::mix_space (oklch shorter hue), ::spread_t; crates/sr-gpu/src/paint.rs::gradient (stops_of: non-decreasing raise l.~130), ::gradient_stops; crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::gradients_interpolate_in_the_requested_space; Schematron C37 (stop offsets must be non-decreasing) in schema/scene-render-1.1.sch: validation is Bob's; do not re-implement

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/comp/gradient_stops.scene.xml (NEW)` at t=[0, 0.5, 1]: 128x64 bg #000 duration 2. linearGradient l3 (dither=false, default linear space): stop0 #FF0000 offset animated 0.3->0.9 over t 0..1 (animate property=offset, keys time 0 value .3, time 1 value .9), stop1 #00FF00 @.6, stop2 #0000FF @1, painted on a 64x32 rect; at t=1 stop0 (0.9) exceeds stop1 (0.6): Rust raises stop1 to 0.9 (red then blue), Python sorts (green early). Plus oklch conic, oklab linear with midpoint .3 and srgb strips. (Document with decreasing static offsets is INVALID in Rust: C37.) Expect **MATCH >= 45 at every time** (today: DIFFERENT at t=1.0 (3.0 dB for the strip: Python (0,255,0) where Rust (255,0,0)); t=0 strip 39.2 dB (12-sub-stop approximation)).

#### P3-COMP-13 — Radial gradient: Rust ray formulation for focal point, focal radius and aspect  

Size **S** · track **P3-B** · rows: COMP-25 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-12

**Goal.** Radial gradients evaluate t = (len - fr)/(s - fr) per pixel, len = distance from the focal point to the pixel (after the aspect stretch about the centre) and s = distance from the focal point to the end circle along that ray (sqrt clamp when the focal point lies outside the circle), instead of cairo's two-circle cone. With fr=0 and a focal point inside the circle the cairo path is kept (identical). The conic-style device-space surface from P3-COMP-12 carries the evaluation.

**Python files.** scenerender/paint.py::_radial (l.259: cairo.RadialGradient(fx,fy,fr,cx,cy,r)) -> device-space evaluator when fr>0 or focal outside/at the circle  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_paint_layout_conformance.py::test_radial_focal_animation_does_not_require_base_attribute (fx/fy defaults to cx/cy)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/common.wgsl::eval_paint kind 2 (l.185-205); crates/sr-gpu/src/paint.rs::gradient RadialGradient arm (p0=(cx,cy,r,aspect), p1=(fx,fy,fr)); crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::generator_patterns_and_gradient_geometry_follow_the_python_renderer (radial aspect part)

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/comp/gradient_radial.scene.xml (NEW)` at t=[0.25]: 128x64 bg #000 duration2. Rect 64x64 radial r1: fx .3 fy .4 fr .15 r .5 white->black dither=false; rect 64x32 radial r2: fx .8 fy .5 (focal ON the end circle) r .3 aspect 1.5 srgb red->blue; plus aspect-only and centred-focus controls (already identical). Expect **r1 MATCH >= 45; r2 (focal on/outside circle) CLOSE >= 40** (today: DIFFERENT: r1 35.5 dB (off-centre focal + fr), r2 18.1 dB (max 255: cone semantics)).
- Port Rust test `generator_patterns_and_gradient_geometry_follow_the_python_renderer (radial/linear rotation part)` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs): radialGradient aspect 2 in a 32x16 box: pixel 8 px right of centre brighter than 4 px below by >0.1; linear rotation 90 about the box centre: top (48,16)=0.266, bottom (48,31)=0.734 +-0.03 Python: shapes with fill=url(#rg)/url(#lg) as in the Rust test, frame_linear reads.
- Port Rust test `object_units_map_the_box_to_the_unit_square, rotation_turns_about_the_box_centre_in_pixels` (crates/sr-gpu/src/paint.rs (#[cfg(test)])): object_xform maps (10,20)->(0,0),(110,70)->(1,1); 90 degree rotation of a 200x100 box maps (100,100)->(0.75,0.5) in object units and (150,50) in user units Python: compare paint._units_matrix at the same inputs (matrix arithmetic, 1e-12).

#### P3-COMP-14 — Generators: contrast on every kind, analytic grid/checkerboard/stripes in asset units  

Size **M** · track **P3-B** · rows: COMP-31 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-01

**Goal.** contrast applies to solid/gradient/checkerboard/grid/stripes as t=clamp((t-0.5)*contrast+0.5,0,1) with t=0 paint, t=1 paint2; checkerboard, grid and stripes are evaluated analytically in the centred/rotated/scrolled pattern space (floor((rotate(p-centre,-angle)-evolution*period)/scale)) instead of an n-pixel cairo tile rounded to whole pixels; gradient kind and noise kinds mix paint/paint2 premultiplied in display sRGB as today. Grid line width follows Rust: n=clamp(round(scale),2,256), w=max(1,round(0.04n))/n of the cell (flagged, see rust_may_be_wrong).

#### P3-COMP-15 — Generators evaluated at the asset's own resolution, then drawn like an image layer  

Size **M** · track **P3-B** · rows: COMP-33 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-14, P3-COMP-16

**Goal.** Every generator kind (including film-grain at full density and light-rays) is evaluated per pixel on the asset's w x h grid (capped at the max texture size 16384 by default; Rust uses the device limit) into a float premultiplied texture, then placed with the same mip/bilinear path as image layers (P3-COMP-16), instead of sampling smooth fields on a coarser grid at output resolution. A generator scaled up x3 is therefore magnified from its asset-size texture exactly like Rust. Numba kernels (kernels.fbm) are used for speed; cached per (asset, params, frame).

**Python files.** scenerender/assets/generator.py::render_generator (noise branch l.290-300), ::_max_spacing (remove), ::_visible_region (kept only for crop), ::_draw_raster (switch to assets.image_pixels.draw_pixels + FloatMips); scenerender/assets/image_pixels.py (consumed API only: FloatMips, draw_pixels)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_assets.py::test_generator_resolution_follows_scale (asserts the coarse grid rw<=17: must be rewritten to assert asset-size evaluation); tests/test_assets.py::test_generator_kinds_draw / deterministic_seeded_animated; tests/test_raster_cache.py

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::generator_texture (w,h = asset size capped at max_texture); crates/sr-gpu/src/shaders.wgsl::fs_generator (is_noise_kind mixing in display sRGB); crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::generators_render_deterministically

#### P3-COMP-16 — Trilinear mip-chain sampling for minified images  

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: COMP-19 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-01

**Goal.** Minified image layers (and image sequences/video frames sharing draw_pixels) sample a continuous trilinear mip chain: lod = log2(1/s) with s = max of the two Jacobian column lengths (GPU derivative convention), the two neighbouring levels are drawn bilinearly and blended by the fractional lod; levels are the Rust box chain (floor(w/2) x floor(h/2), 2x2 box on premultiplied working samples, edge clamped). Magnified images stay bilinear on level 0. Full minification to 1x1 still averages (test_downsampling_filters_linear_premultiplied_samples).

**Python files.** scenerender/assets/image_pixels.py::FloatMips (levels via 2x2 box instead of PIL BOX resize on odd sizes), ::FloatMips.pick (replace by lod_levels returning (lo, hi, frac)), ::draw_pixels, ::_draw_cairo (two passes + mix in float; exponent trick retained), ::_draw_translated; scenerender/assets/video.py (consumer of draw_pixels only; check)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_image_inputs.py::test_downsampling_filters_linear_premultiplied_samples (128x128 stripes to 1x1 -> [.5,.5,.5,.5], and stale-file case); tests/test_raster_cache.py (placement resample exactness); tests/test_assets.py (image tests), tests/test_image_inputs.py::test_input_primaries_are_converted_to_working_space

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/resources.rs::mips (box chain; test mip_chain_averages), ::decode_dynamic; crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs sampler (l.802-804: mag/min/mipmap all Linear) and shaders.wgsl fs textureSample(src, samp, uv) (implicit lod); crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::background_and_image_placement, opacity_rotation_and_anchor, fit_cover_contain_and_blur_fill

Expect **each minified patch CLOSE >= 40 (MATCH >= 45 expected for scale .7 and .3)** (today: DIFFERENT/CLOSE per patch: s=.3 33.8 dB (max 34), s=.7 28.0 dB (max 81), anisotropic 26.5 dB (max 73)).

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: COMP-20 · needs schema adoption: **yes** · depends on: P3-COMP-16, P3-COMP-22

**Goal.** imageAsset/imageSequence @colorProfile (embedded|declared, schema default embedded) is honoured: with embedded, a PNG/JPEG/TIFF ICC profile (matrix/TRC; mapped to a named space+transfer when it equals srgb/display-p3/rec2020/dci-p3 within 3e-3 colorants and 2e-3 curves, else decoded via its own matrix and tone curves into the working space), or CICP code points (PNG cICP, HEIF nclx, ICC cicp tag: primaries 1,9,11,12; transfer 1/6/14/15->bt1886, 4->gamma22, 8->linear, 13->srgb, 16->pq, 18->hlg) take precedence over declared colorSpace/transfer; CMYK/LUT/unusable profiles produce a note ('embedded colour profile not used (why); colorSpace applies') surfaced through P3-COMP-22. declared always uses colorSpace/transfer; a linear-format file (EXR/HDR) with transfer=auto is linear.

**Python files.** scenerender/assets/image_io.py::decode (return the profile bytes/CICP with the pixels); scenerender/assets/image_pixels.py::input_pixels, ::load_pixels (cache key gains colorProfile), new coding() mirroring resources.rs::coding; scenerender/color.py (new icc.parse for rXYZ/gXYZ/bXYZ + curv/para + cicp tag; icc_d50_to_d65 Bradford; named_profile matcher); scenerender/assets/video.py::color_params (read colorProfile attribute); schema/scene-render-1.1.xsd attribute is Bob's adoption (this package consumes it)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_image_inputs.py (all decode tests: declared behaviour unchanged for untagged files); tests/test_assets_full.py, tests/fixtures/media/make_media_assets.py; tests/test_color_mgmt.py

#### P3-COMP-20 — Raster-cache exactness: warm-cache frames equal fresh frames; cache invalidation regression tests  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: COMP-38 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-03, P3-COMP-22

**Goal.** A frame rendered after other frames by the same renderer is pixel-identical to the same frame rendered fresh (Rust guarantee). Today moving static content is resampled from a 15%-oversized raster (docs/RUNTIME.md; sequence-vs-fresh max difference 152/255 on a moving 10x10 shape at t=1.0 while SCENERENDER_RASTER_CACHE=0 gives 0). The cache therefore reuses an entry exactly only when the placement is the same or an integer-pixel translation (linear part unchanged); any other move redraws; the approximate resampling path stays available as SCENERENDER_RASTER_CACHE=approx for speed (documented as not conformant). Cache signatures keep covering animated paints, mattes, effects' referenced nodes, lights/camera exposure for 3D-bearing groups so nothing stale is ever served. The parity harness and conformance tests run with the exact default.

**Python files.** scenerender/compositor.py::_render_cached, ::_cached_draw, ::_composite_cached, ::_warp_raster, ::_moved, ::_content_static, ::_raster_signature, ::_rc_usable, RASTER_CACHE env parsing (l.1596); docs/RUNTIME.md raster cache paragraph (l.73-82)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_raster_cache.py::test_moving_static_content_is_resampled_closely (pins approximate resampling: becomes the 'approx' mode test; add exact-mode counterpart), test_unmoved_static_content_is_reused_exactly, test_document_edits_are_never_served_stale, test_changed_image_files_are_never_served_stale, test_animated_content_is_not_cached; tests/test_render_speed.py (speed expectations: re-measure; approximate mode may be required there)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs::content_hash, ::subtree_hash, ::used_hash (what a cached subtree depends on); crates/sr-gpu/tests/caches.rs::isolated_groups_follow_the_paints_of_their_content, mattes_follow_the_paints_they_are_filled_with, effects_follow_the_nodes_they_read, an_evolving_generator_does_not_fill_the_texture_pool; crates/sr-gpu/tests/cache_regressions.rs::matte_cache_follows_animated_paints, isolated_groups_follow_animated_paints, isolated_groups_follow_camera_exposure, isolated_groups_follow_animated_materials, isolated_groups_follow_animated_lights, isolated_groups_ignore_unrelated_light_animation; crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::clipped_instance_and_moved_group_reuse_the_offscreen

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/comp/cache_sequence.scene.xml (NEW)` at t=[0, 0.5, 0.775]: 64x32 bg #00000000? NO: bg #000000 opaque. Isolated group with a shape filled by a gradient whose first stop colour animates #FF0000->#0000FF over 0..1 s; a hidden shape using the same gradient as matte for a white layer (matteMode luma); a 10x10 green shape whose x moves 0->50 over 2 s. Compared via pytest (not Rust): Renderer rendering [0.0, 1.0, 1.55] in one session must equal fresh renders of the same times (max diff 0). Expect **equal to fresh render, exactly (and MATCH versus Rust at single frames)** (today: FAILS exactness today: frames after the first differ by up to 152/255 around the moving shape (x 24-35, y 0-10) with the default cache; 0 with SCENERENDER_RASTER_CACHE=0).
- Port Rust test `isolated_groups_follow_the_paints_of_their_content (assert_follows)` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/caches.rs): for isolate/blend=screen/clip/matte/masked groups filled with an animated gradient, frames [0,1] from one renderer: frame 1 == a fresh frame 1 and frame 0 != frame 1 Python: helper assert_follows(path): r=Renderer.open; a=[r.frame_rgba(t) for t in (0,1)]; fresh=Renderer.open(path).frame_rgba(1); assert array_equal(a[1],fresh) and not array_equal(a[0],a[1]); parametrize the five attribute variants.
- Port Rust test `mattes_follow_the_paints_they_are_filled_with / effects_follow_the_nodes_they_read` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/caches.rs): luma and alpha matte whose source shape uses an animated gradient follow the animation; effects reading an animated node follow it Python: same assert_follows helper on matte='m' scenes and on an effect reading a node reference.
- Port Rust test `matte_cache_follows_animated_paints / isolated_groups_follow_animated_paints` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/cache_regressions.rs): pixel (10,10) after frames [0,1] equals the fresh frame at 1 exactly Python: np.array_equal on one pixel and on the whole frame.

#### P3-COMP-21 — Root prefix snapshot: restore the unchanged leading draws instead of redrawing  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: COMP-39 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-COMP-20, P3-COMP-22

**Goal.** At the composition root, the longest run of leading root children that are provably unchanged between the previous and the current frame (same size, node signatures and placement; static over [t_prev, t] by _content_static) is stored as a snapshot of the frame buffer after that prefix; the next frame restores it with one copy and draws only the remaining children. Output is bit-identical to a fresh render; disabled while a contrast probe/adjustment layer or temporal effect reads the backdrop; counters prefix_restored / prefix_stored appear in the render report.

**Python files.** scenerender/compositor.py::render_frame, ::render_children (root only: prefix hashing/restore), new _prefix state on RenderContext; scenerender/gpucomp.py (copy of a GPU frame tile for the snapshot)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_raster_cache.py (no interaction expected); tests/test_gpucomp.py::test_lent_tiles_are_never_written_through (snapshot must copy, not lend)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render.rs: Plan prefix fields (l.275), root prefix reuse l.3221-3250, execute restore l.3685-3700, ::snapshot l.3833; RenderStats::prefix_restored; crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs::unchanged_frames_restore_the_root_prefix

**Acceptance gate.**

- Port Rust test `unchanged_frames_restore_the_root_prefix` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/composite.rs): bg #202020; two static layers (white opacity .2 scale 16x8; red screen) then a mover (x 0->60 over 2 s); rendering [0,0.1,0.2] with one renderer: prefix_restored == 2, draws == 2, and the frame equals a fresh render at 0.2 exactly Python: use shapes; the report from P3-COMP-22 exposes report.prefix_restored and report.draws; assert 2 and 2 and np.array_equal(frame_rgba) against a fresh Renderer.
