### FX

#### P3-FX-01 — Probe-scene generator, test card, per-member gate runner and Rust-fixture helpers  

Size **M** · track **P3-B** · rows:  · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: —

**Goal.** tools/parity/gen_fx_probes.py deterministically writes tools/parity/scenes/assets/fx-testcard.png and one scene per (effect member, variant) under tools/parity/scenes/fx/<family>-<member>[-variant].scene.xml from one table (type, attrs, family, T1 or T2 template, extra elements such as hidden source layers / paints / lights / LUT files, time fractions, expected floor). tools/parity/fx_gate.py runs the existing render_parity.py machinery (branch parity-render) over a family and fails when a member is below its floor, printing one row per member (rs vs py PSNR, %>2 levels, status). tests/fxref.py provides numpy helpers used by the ported Rust tests: phi() (standard normal CDF as in effects.rs), lin8(), srgb transfer, the fixture PNGs of crates/sr-gpu/tests/common.rs (red 4x4 (255,0,0); half 4x4 white alpha 128; quad 2x2 red/green/blue/white; wide 8x4 left red right blue; gray 4x4 128; src 4x4 (200,100,50); white 4x4), and WGSL-loop reference implementations. tests/test_fx_parity.py wraps the gate and is skipped unless SR_BIN (rs binary) is set. The committed baseline table docs/parity/FX-BASELINE.md reproduces the measurements quoted in these packages.

**Python files.** tools/parity/gen_fx_probes.py (new; tools/parity exists only on branch parity-render); tools/parity/fx_gate.py (new); tools/parity/scenes/assets/fx-testcard.png (new, generated); tests/fxref.py (new); tests/test_fx_parity.py (new); docs/parity/FX-BASELINE.md (new)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_every_effect_contract and ::test_registry_exactly_matches_schema stay as the contract tests

**Rust reference.** tools/perf_effects.py::EFFECTS and EXTRA (default attribute set for every effect type; seed for the member table); crates/sr-gpu/tests/common.rs::ASSETS, fixtures(), doc_with; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::every_effect_type_draws (80 KINDS and the per-kind extra attributes); crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::transition_doc

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/baseline-passthrough.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with <effect id='e' type='exposure' exposure='0'/>: proves decode, placement and background composite of the test card are identical in both engines Expect **MATCH >= 60 dB** (today: MATCH (99.0 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/baseline-blur.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with <effect id='e' type='blur' radius='3'/>: the one family already at parity, a regression anchor for the harness Expect **MATCH >= 55 dB** (today: MATCH (63.8 dB)).
- Port Rust test `every_effect_type_draws` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): each of the 80 effect types renders on a moving layer with a gray background layer, no unsupported/error messages (except lighting light-resolution messages), at least one pass and only finite pixels Python: parametrize over the generator table (80 members, attributes from tools/perf_effects.py EXTRA plus lut=id.cube, shader=pass.glsl, curve='0,0 0.5,0.6 1,1', paint=url(#grad), source=bg, lights=pl); Renderer.open(path, strict=True).frame_linear(1.0); assert np.isfinite(px).all() and no warn_once output (caplog)
- Eval-level: Not applicable to pixels. scene-render eval FILE -t T --format json prints nodes[], transitions[] and elements[].props only; later packages cite which fields they use.

**Risks.** The Rust target covers the whole frame for UNBOUNDED effects, so on a layer node letterbox, fractal-noise and lens-flare paint the whole frame (use T2 for those). Image scaling and fractional-edge shapes add a 35-38 dB baseline unrelated to effects; keep probes at scale 1 with the test card. Each probe is 2 process launches (~1.6 s); 25 families x 2 variants x 1-3 times is ~10 minutes at --jobs 4.

#### P3-FX-02 — Node-local effect units: Params node-space API and scale  

Size **M** · track **P3-B** · rows: FX-02 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01

**Goal.** Effect distances use the node's frame-space scale like Rust: Params.d(name, default) returns value * px with px = max singular value of the 2x2 linear part of the node's frame-space matrix (which already includes rc.scale); Params also exposes lin (2x2), dirv(length, angle_deg) and offset(nameX, nameY, default) that rotate a node-local vector into frame space with the same matrix, and fx_space is stashed by compositor.apply_effects so handlers that already call Params(rc, e, ctx) need no signature change (node=None falls back to identity linear part and rc.scale). A 2x scaled layer with blur radius 4 blurs 8 px; a rotated layer's drop-shadow offset, directional-blur direction, long-shadow direction and rgb-split offset rotate with it (the per-handler rotation edits are made in P3-FX-11, P3-FX-17 and P3-FX-21; angles of bevel, emboss, lighting, wave-warp, halftone, kaleidoscope, mirror are NOT rotated in Rust). Adjustment layers use the adjustment's own world matrix. The effect-result cache key (_reuse_effect) includes (px, lin).

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/__init__.py (Params.__init__/d/new px, lin, dirv, offset); scenerender/compositor.py (apply_effects, _reuse_effect key; world_matrix(node, ctx))  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_blur_scaling; tests/test_effects.py::test_colour_and_radius_animation; tests/test_effects.py::test_lighting_types_respond_to_colour_intensity_and_scale; tests/test_kernels.py (fused display chain through apply_effects); tests/test_shaders.py (custom-shader padding stays in render-scale units, not node scale)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs::Cx (px, scale, lin) L1073-1115 and effect_body closures dirv/offset_uv/big L1409-1419; crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs::cx_for L621-669 and node_effects px/pad L809-840; crates/sr-vector/src/geom.rs::max_scale L152-160; crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs TURNING list L43-80; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::blur_radius_is_the_standard_deviation; crates/sr-gpu/tests/motion_blur.rs::turning_layers_keep_the_direction_of_their_effects

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/units-blur-scaled.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with layer x=40 y=30 scaleX=0.75 scaleY=0.75 and <effect type='blur' radius='4'/> (sigma 3 px) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: CLOSE (31.2 dB; Python blurs sigma 4)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/units-shadow-rotated.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with layer x=40 y=30 scale 0.75 rotation=30 and <effect type='drop-shadow' radius='6' offsetX='10' offsetY='0' color='#000000FF'/> Expect **CLOSE >= 42 dB (Kawase vs Gaussian bound)** (today: CLOSE (32.5 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/units-dirblur-rotated.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with layer x=40 y=30 scale 0.75 rotation=40 and <effect type='directional-blur' radius='8' angle='0'/> Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (21.8 dB)).
- Port Rust test `blur_radius_is_the_standard_deviation` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): white layer x=32 y=-4 scaleX=scaleY=10 with blur radius=0.3 has alpha at x in {27,29,31,33,35,37} equal to Phi((x+0.5-32)/3) within 0.05; drop-shadow radius=0.6 (2 sigma, sigma 3) matches the same Phi at x in {27,29,31} within 0.05 and its colour is black when absent (rgb < 1e-3) Python: write white.png (4x4), project 64x32 background #00000000; px = Renderer.open(p).frame_linear(0.0); alpha = px[16, x, 3]; compare with tests/fxref.phi; use the same two effects (blur radius 0.3, drop-shadow radius 0.6 offset 0 0 color #000000)
- Port Rust test `turning_layers_keep_the_direction_of_their_effects` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/motion_blur.rs): a layer spinning 360 deg/s with drop-shadow offsetX=10, long-shadow size=14 angle=0, directional-blur radius=6 angle=0 renders at >= 50 dB against the average of the 12 sharp frames at the shutter's sample times (bevel, emboss, scanlines do not turn) Python: use node-level motionBlur='on' with project motionBlur=false (the project-level path is the average by construction); compare _render_node_blurred output with a numpy mean of 12 render_node_at frames; assert psnr >= 50 for the five effects
- Eval-level: scene-render eval FILE -t T --format json: nodes[i].world = [a,b,c,d,e,f]; max_scale = sqrt((s1+s2)/2 + sqrt(((s1-s2)/2)^2 + (a*c+b*d)^2)) with s1=a^2+b^2, s2=c^2+d^2. Compare with the singular value of rc.world_matrix(node, ctx)[:2,:2]/rc.scale on a scene with animated scale/rotation and a scaled parent group at t in {0, 0.5, 0.9}, tolerance 1e-6. elements[].props gives the evaluated animated effect parameter (e.g. radius 4.0 at t=0.5 for keys 0->0, 1->8) to compare with Params.n.

**Risks.** px uses the largest singular value, so a non-uniform scale still blurs isotropically at the larger scale. Unit tests call apply_effects(ids, buf, ctx) with node=None: keep the identity fallback. Custom-shader padding uses the render scale, not the node's (render_fx.rs L826-835). Vector rotation edits live in group B/C packages, so land this API first.

#### P3-FX-03 — Masks and mattes cut the effected image; chain contract  

Size **M** · track **P3-B** · rows: FX-01 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01

**Goal.** finish_node order becomes deform, depth of field, effects, masks, matte (Rust: bare node + effects first, then opacity, blend, masks, matte). A glow/shadow/blur halo is cut by the node's mask and matte instead of spilling past a pre-cut input. The raster-cache signature, the mask-aware crop logic and _effect_signature follow the new order (effects see the unmasked raster; the cached effect result does not depend on mask parameters). Adjustment layers keep apply_adjustment's own mask handling.

**Python files.** scenerender/compositor.py (finish_node, apply_effects, _render_cached / raster cache keys ~L937-1166, _effect_signature); scenerender/masks.py (apply_masks / apply_matte are only reordered, not changed)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_raster_cache.py (cache keys); tests/test_core.py (mask tests, one with effects=); tests/test_particle_ancestors.py and tests/test_reference_source_projection.py (masks combined with effects=); no unit test pins mask-before-effects; add the new ones

**Rust reference.** README 'A node with effects renders once without its own compositing' and Limitations bullet 'Masks and mattes apply after a node's effects'; crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs module doc L1-17, node_effects composite() call L975-977, composite() L592-640 (mask_off/mask_count, bare blend)

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/order-glow-ellipse-mask.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with layer child <mask type='ellipse' x='0' y='0' width='96' height='96'/> and <effect type='glow' radius='8' threshold='0.3'/> Expect **CLOSE >= 36 dB (glow baseline 39.8)** (today: DIFFERENT (25.6 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/order-shadow-rect-mask.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with child <mask type='rect' x='0' y='0' width='96' height='40'/> and <effect type='drop-shadow' radius='8' offsetX='10' offsetY='12' color='#FF0000FF'/> (shadow below the mask is cut) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (17.1 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/order-blur-feathered-mask.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with child <mask type='rect' x='0' y='0' width='96' height='40' feather='4'/> and blur radius=4 Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: CLOSE (31.6 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/order-shadow-matte.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: regression guard: visible ellipse matte shape declared before the layer, layer matte='m' with drop-shadow radius 8 offset 10,12 red Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: MATCH (56.5 dB)).
- Eval-level: Not applicable (no Rust test covers the order; the gate is the probes plus an analytic pytest).

**Risks.** Pytest to add: white 40x40 layer with glow radius 6 threshold 0.5 and a rect mask covering its left half; assert alpha outside the mask rectangle is < 0.01 and inside near the cut it is the glow-of-whole-layer value (not glow-of-cut-layer). Coordinate with the COMP area: apply_masks/apply_matte signatures and the finding that a visible='false' node used as matte is empty in Python. The cached raster for masked nodes may have been cropped to the mask; effects now need the full box + reach, so the crop must be applied after effects.

#### P3-FX-04 — Effect target rectangle: reach, stroke, frame clipping, 32 px rounding, content box  

Size **L** · track **P3-B** · rows: FX-R02, FX-43 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02

**Goal.** Before running a node's effect chain Python normalises the input tile to the rectangle Rust would use as the effect target and crops the result to it: for sized leaf layers/shapes without shape modifiers/deforms/soft bodies, rect = [floor(min corner - reach_px - 1), ceil(max corner + reach_px + 1)] over the node's transformed box corners, clamped to the frame ([0, W] x [0, H]), then each axis rounded up to a multiple of 32 px growing towards the frame's far edge first then the near edge (round_span); other nodes and effects with infinite reach (UNBOUNDED list: lens-flare, god-rays, light-leak, radial-blur, zoom-blur, twirl, spherize, bulge, lens-distortion, mirror, kaleidoscope, tile, displacement-map, letterbox, vignette, light-sweep, fractal-noise) use the whole frame. reach_px = sum of per-effect reach (port of render_fx.rs::reach: blur/glow/bloom/halation/unsharp/inner-* 4*radius; lens-blur/tilt-shift 2*radius; drop-shadow 2*radius+max offset; directional 2*radius; long-shadow size or 60; stroke/outline max(size,1); matte-choke |amount|; wave/ripple/turbulent/heat-haze/glitch/vhs/chromatic-aberration 4*|amount|+8; rgb-split max offset; pixel-motion-blur 64) times the node's px scale, plus stroke_reach (half width centred, full width outside, x sqrt2 for rect / miter limit for paths, plus 1) for shapes. Pixel positions inside the target (hash draw index (y*width+x), pixelate/mosaic/halftone cells, scanlines phase, wave/ripple phase, uv-based overlays) then agree with Rust, content outside the frame is not rendered (blur at a frame edge sees transparency), chromatic-aberration/rgb-split use the content box (union of the node's and its sized descendants' boxes grown by 2 px, within 64 px of the frame) as the effect input, and the source of displacement-map/difference-key must name a node (an asset id gives a warn_once, runtime only; do not add a validation rule, Bob owns those). Hot paths (raster cache, effect cache) keep working: cache key gains the rect.

**Python files.** scenerender/compositor.py (apply_effects, finish_node, new effect_reach/stroke_reach/target_rect helpers, _reuse_effect key); scenerender/effects/__init__.py (Buf.pad is kept for handlers; source_buf for the node-only source rule; Params.target / Params.content_box); scenerender/raster.py (Buf.crop_to / expand_to if missing)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py (unit tests with node=None must be unchanged: no rect normalisation without a node); tests/test_raster_cache.py; tests/test_effects.py::test_source_node_inputs_and_offsets, ::test_recursive_source_is_guarded, ::test_source_opacity_and_transparent_plate

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs::node_effects L809-975 (bounded rect, shader margin), round_span/round_rect L147-173, reach L236-266, stroke_reach L175-196, drawn_bounds L198-232, content_box L671-695, UNBOUNDED L84-102, cx_for L621-669; README 'Effects run on targets sized to what they cover' and Limitations bullet 3; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::blur_samples_outside_its_input_are_transparent; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effect_costs.rs::shape_effect_targets_include_the_stroke

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/target-pixelate-offset.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with layer x=19 y=21 and <effect type='pixelate' size='8'/> (cells are anchored to the Rust target origin) Expect **MATCH >= 60 dB** (today: CLOSE (32.6 dB; 67.4 dB when the layer happens to sit at 16,16)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/target-mosaic-offset.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with layer x=19 y=21 and mosaic size=8 Expect **MATCH >= 60 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (24.9 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/target-film-grain-layer.scene.xml` at t=[0.2, 0.5, 0.9]: T1 with <effect type='film-grain' amount='0.5' size='1' seed='3'/> Expect **MATCH >= 60 dB (70.6 dB when the target is the frame, i.e. adjustment)** (today: CLOSE (35.9 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/target-noise-layer.scene.xml` at t=[0.2, 0.5, 0.9]: T1 with <effect type='noise' amount='0.2' seed='3'/> Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB (51.1 on adjustment)** (today: DIFFERENT (21.2 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/target-glitch-layer.scene.xml` at t=[0.2, 0.5, 0.9]: T1 with <effect type='glitch' amount='6' size='4' seed='3'/> Expect **MATCH >= 60 dB (72.6 on adjustment)** (today: DIFFERENT (14.7 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/target-clipped-blur.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with layer x=40 y=13 (box passes the frame's right edge) and blur radius=6: Rust blurs the frame-clipped bare render, so the edge fades Expect **MATCH >= 55 dB** (today: CLOSE (38.4 dB)).
- Port Rust test `blur_samples_outside_its_input_are_transparent` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): white layer scaleX=16 scaleY=8 on a 64x32 transparent frame plus an adjustment with blur radius=3: alpha at (32,16) is 1 +-0.01 and at the frame edge (0,16) equals Phi(0.5/3) +-0.03 Python: white.png 4x4; frame_linear(0)[16,0,3] vs fxref.phi(0.5/3); the adjustment must see transparency outside the frame
- Port Rust test `shape_effect_targets_include_the_stroke` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effect_costs.rs): a stroked blurred shape (ellipse, rect, 5-point star; fill #FF0000 stroke #FFFFFF strokeWidth=10, blur radius 2) drawn alone and inside a group carrying the same effect is identical (>= 60 dB)  Python: render both documents with Renderer.frame_linear(0) and compute psnr over the frame; assert >= 60
- Eval-level: eval nodes[].world and nodes[].size give the box corners; Rust does not print its rect, so cross-check Python's target_rect against an independent re-implementation of round_span/round_rect in the test on the eval output of a moving layer (rect origin and size at t in {0,0.5,0.9}); tolerance 0 px.

**Risks.** Highest-risk package: it changes what every handler sees as its tile for node effects. Keep node=None unchanged. Rust clamps the target to the frame (and to 64 px beyond it for custom shaders, which keep their own margin: FX-27 is PRESENT, do not alter). Some results change for layers extending past the frame (edges fade as in Rust). Effect-cache key must include rect and (px, lin). The 32 px rounding is a performance heuristic that leaks into pixel-anchored effects (see rust_may_be_wrong), but parity requires reproducing it. FX-43's pooling, fusion and --stats are not reproduced (performance only).

#### P3-FX-05 — edgeMode core: mirror/clamp sampling and the edgeMode param  

Size **S** · track **P3-B** · rows: FX-25 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-04

**Goal.** Params.edge_mode() reads <param name='edgeMode' value='1|2'/> (values other than 1 and 2 mean 0, transparent) and effects.sample() gains a 'mirror' mode matching Rust's warp_uv: uv clamp to [0,1] for mode 1 and 1 - |1 - (uv - 2*floor(uv/2))| (period-2 reflection) for mode 2, applied before bilinear filtering (so opaque frame edges stay opaque and mirrored content is reflected, not clamped); mode 0 keeps transparent sampling. Modes extend the input boundary only; they never add off-target scene content or fill transparent texels inside the input. Wiring into the 16 listed effects (chromatic-aberration, rgb-split, displacement-map, turbulent-displace, wave-warp, ripple, twirl, spherize, bulge, lens-distortion, heat-haze, mirror, kaleidoscope, tile, pixelate, vhs; mosaic is in fx.rs's list but its shader case returns before edge handling) is done in P3-FX-18, -19, -21, -22, -23 where the handlers live.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/__init__.py (sample(mode=...), Params.edge_mode)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py (sample() users: grep sample( in scenerender/effects/*.py); no test mentions edgeMode (grep over *.py, *.xsd, *.md: zero hits)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl St/Wt/Wz/warp_uv L79-110; crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs edge_mode read in the warp arm L1884-1888 (param(e,a,'edgeMode',0.0), 1.0 or 2.0); README 'Distortion edges can be extended explicitly'

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/edgemode-core-checker.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T2-style full-frame content: <layer id='bg' asset='tc' x='0' y='0' scaleX='1.33333' scaleY='1.33333'/><adjustment id='n' effects='e'/> with <effect type='twirl' angle='60' radius='60'><param name='edgeMode' value='2'/></effect> Expect **MATCH >= 60 dB** (today: MATCH (67.3 dB; twirl does not reach the edge here, so this only guards the core)).
- Port Rust test `warp_edge_modes_preserve_full_frame_colour` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): white layer scaleX=16 scaleY=8 on 64x32 transparent frame + adjustment with rgb-split offsetX=128 offsetY=64, wave-warp amount=16 size=32, lens-distortion amount=4, each with edgeMode 1 and 2: corners (0,0),(63,0),(0,31),(63,31),(16,0) stay [1,1,1,1] within 0.01 Python: unit-test sample(px, x, y, 'mirror') and Params.edge_mode here (reflection of -0.5 -> 0.5, 1.5 -> 0.5 in uv); the end-to-end version is enabled as the handlers are wired in P3-FX-21 and P3-FX-22 (xfail until then)
- Eval-level: Not applicable (pixels only).

**Risks.** Pixel-centre convention: Rust reflects uv before bilinear sampling, Python's sample() works on pixel indices; reflect x -> w-1-... consistently (mirror about the outer pixel edge, period 2*w) and unit-test against the Rust formula on a ramp. edgeMode is a <param> child: no schema change, nothing for Bob.

#### P3-FX-06 — Motion blur: shutter edge cases, adjustment layers, per-node adaptive test  

Size **M** · track **P3-B** · rows: FX-38, FX-39 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01

**Goal.** Keep Python's whole-frame supersampling (it is the definition of motion blur used by Rust's own tests, not an approximation) and fix the measured differences: (a) shutterAngle <= 0 disables blur and draws at t (Python currently samples at t+shutterPhase); (b) adjustment layers are drawn at the frame time t, not once per shutter sample (Rust: 'adjusts where it is at the frame time'; emit one warn_once 'motion blur is not applied to adjustment layers' when its own motion exceeds 0.5 px), so a moving adjustment mask stays sharp; (c) a node that does not exist at the first or last shutter sample (it starts or ends inside the shutter) is drawn sharp like Rust (decision: flagged in rust_may_be_wrong; Python currently dims it by dropping the missing samples); (d) adaptive test per node: node boxes are compared at the first and last sample (max corner displacement >= 0.5 px, unsized nodes use the Rust 100x100 stand-in or, better, the children's union) instead of the frame-level image comparison, with adaptiveMotionBlur=false sampling everything; groups whose own transform is still do not blur themselves; (e) camera@shutterAngle override stays as a documented Python superset (Rust ignores it). Sample times t_k = t + (shutterPhase/360 + shutterAngle/360*(k+0.5)/n)/fps, equal weights, n = motionBlurSamples (<= 256), already identical.

**Python files.** scenerender/render.py (frame_buf, _static_shutter, _accumulate, shutter sample times ~L77-125); scenerender/compositor.py (render_node, motion_blur_mode L794-846, _render_node_blurred, render_node_at, mb_reusable, apply_adjustment); scenerender/camera.py (shutter_angle hook, unchanged)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_shutter_samples.py; tests/test_raster_cache.py (mb reuse); tests/test_temporal_clocks.py

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs::motion_blur L1273-1427 (sample times L1293-1300, adaptive/moved test L1301-1327, adjustment unsupported message L1328-1337), draw_accumulated L1445-1490, rigid_reference L1494; crates/sr-gpu/tests/motion_blur.rs (4 tests); crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::motion_blur_smears_moving_layers_only_with_a_provider, ::motion_blurred_shapes_sample_only_the_box_they_sweep; schema scene-render-1.1.xsd L3921-3930 (shutterAngle default 180 max 720, shutterPhase default -90, motionBlurSamples default 16 max 256, adaptiveMotionBlur default true)

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/mb-shutter-zero.scene.xml` at t=[0.25]: project 160x96 fps=10 duration=2 bg #303030 motionBlur='true' shutterAngle='0' shutterPhase='-90' motionBlurSamples='16'; <shape id='a' shape='rect' x='8' y='20' width='24' height='24' fill='#FFCC00'> animate x 0->80 over 1 s Expect **CLOSE >= 36 dB (flat shape with fractional AA edges; ~38.7 is the moving-shape baseline)** (today: DIFFERENT (26.5 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/mb-first-frame.scene.xml` at t=[0]: same shape, shutterAngle=180 shutterPhase=-90 samples=16, rendered at t=0 (first samples are at negative time) Expect **CLOSE >= 36 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (26.8 dB; Rust draws the node sharp, full (255,204,0); Python (190,153,32))).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/mb-moving-adjustment.scene.xml` at t=[0.25]: project motionBlur=true angle 180 phase -90 samples 16; <layer id='bg' asset='tc' x='0' y='0'/><adjustment id='adj' effects='inv'><mask type='rect' x='0' y='0' width='40' height='96'/><animate property='x'><key time='0' value='0'/><key time='1' value='80'/></animate></adjustment> with <effect id='inv' type='invert'/> Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (24.8 dB; Rust blurred and sharp frames are identical: 99 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/mb-multiply-backdrop.scene.xml` at t=[0.25]: regression guard: backdrop <layer id='bg' asset='tc' x='30' y='0'/> and a moving rect blend='multiply' (blend kept under motion blur) Expect **CLOSE >= 36 dB** (today: CLOSE (49.6 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/mb-group-rotation.scene.xml` at t=[0.25]: regression guard: <group id='g'> rotating 0->90 deg over 1 s containing <shape rect x=40 y=10 width=20 height=40> Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: MATCH (59.5 dB)).
- Port Rust test `moving_adjustment_layers_still_adjust` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/motion_blur.rs): project 128x64 fps 10, shutterAngle 180, phase 0, 12 samples: red layer scaleX=32 scaleY=16 + invert adjustment with <mask rect 0,0,40,64> sliding x 0->96 over 1 s; at t=0.5 (mask spans x 48..88) pixel (20,32) red, (68,32) (0,1,1), (110,32) red within 2e-2, blurred render within >= 50 dB of the render without motion blur Python: two Renderers (motionBlur true/false) on the same document, frame_linear(0.5); assert the three pixels and psnr(blurred, sharp) >= 50; assert the unsupported/warn message mentions 'adj' and 'motion blur'
- Port Rust test `moving_layers_keep_their_blend_mode` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/motion_blur.rs): gray 4x4 layer (scale 6) moving x 0->96 across a red/blue backdrop with blend multiply/screen/add/normal, with and without blur radius=1, equals the average of 12 sharp frames at t+0.5*(k+0.5)/12/10 within >= 40 dB Python: node-level path: project motionBlur=false, node motionBlur='on', shutterPhase=0, shutterAngle=180, samples=12; reference = numpy mean of 12 project-sharp frames at the listed times; assert psnr >= 40 for the 8 combinations
- Port Rust test `moving_groups_keep_their_blend_mode` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/motion_blur.rs): a multiply group containing gray and a difference-blend white layer, moving x 0->96, >= 40 dB against the average of sharp frames Python: same harness as above with the group document
- Port Rust test `motion_blurred_shapes_sample_only_the_box_they_sweep` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): a white rect with a 4 px outside red stroke moving 200 px in 4 s blurred vs the same outline as a path: worst per-pixel difference < 0.02 and the outside stroke at (12,7) stays red (r > 0.5, g < 0.2) Python: two documents (shape='rect' and shape='path' path='M0 0 H10 V8 H0 Z'), motionBlur on, samples 8, phase 0; compare frame_linear(0.1) and the pixel
- Eval-level: scene-render eval FILE -t T --format json at the Rust sample times t_k = t + (phase/360 + angle/360*(k+0.5)/n)/fps for k=0 and k=n-1: nodes[i].world at both times gives the max corner displacement used by Rust's adaptive test (>= 0.5 px, size from nodes[i].size, default 100x100 when unsized); compare with Python's per-node decision on a scene with a still layer, a sub-pixel mover and a fast mover. At t<0 eval returns 0 nodes (verified), which is why Rust draws a node starting at t=0 sharp.

**Risks.** Cairo vs wgpu anti-aliasing of fractional shape edges caps moving-shape probes at about 38 dB (use CLOSE floors, or image layers at scale 1). Per-sample adjustment handling needs the adjustment evaluated at the frame time inside sample renders (use mb_center) while the backdrop below is per-sample. Decision (c) copies a Rust quirk that disables blur for nodes entering/leaving inside the shutter; flag it to the user before implementing. camera@shutterAngle is Python-only; keep.

#### P3-FX-07 — Colour grading: color-grade, lift-gamma-gain, cdl, curves, white-balance, hue-saturation  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-11 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01

**Goal.** Rust formulas become the default (decision on color-grade flagged): color-grade = encoded sRGB e = (e - 0.5)*contrast + 0.5 + brightness, then linear-space saturation mix about the linear Rec.709 luma, @exposure ignored (the Python D9 model stays behind <param name='model' value='d9'/>); lift-gamma-gain = encoded e*gain + lift*(1-e) then pow(max(e,0), 1/max(gamma,1e-3)) (gain BEFORE gamma; one or two numbers replicate the first to all three channels; the fused kernels.display_chain op changes accordingly); cdl = encoded pow(max(e*slope+offset,0), power) with NO upper clamp, then Rec.709 saturation on the encoded values; curves = Catmull-Rom through the sorted points with reflected phantom end points sampled at 256 entries (value clamped to [0,1], outside the first/last x the end value), master curve for rgb, channel red/green/blue per channel, luma = e + (curve(luma(e)) - luma(e)), alpha channel is treated as the master curve (and reports 'curves on the alpha channel apply to the master curve'); white-balance = linear gains (1+0.3t, 1-0.3g, 1-0.3t) with t = temperature/100, g = tint/100 (kelvin Bradford model stays behind <param name='model' value='kelvin'/>); hue-saturation = HSV on encoded values: hue += hue/360 (wrap), s = clamp(s*saturation, 0, 1), v += brightness. levels and exposure are already equal (guard).

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/color.py (color_grade L63, lift_gamma_gain L76, _lgg_op L50, cdl L87, curves L162, white_balance L189, hue_saturation L227, _hue_rotate L209); scenerender/kernels.py (display_chain / lift-gamma-gain op order); scenerender/effects/color_science.py (bradford stays for the kelvin model)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_color_grade_is_d9 (rewrite to the Rust formula; keep a d9-model variant); tests/test_effects.py::test_colour_semantics (color-grade saturation=0, lift-gamma-gain, cdl, hue-saturation cases); tests/test_effects.py::test_display_referred_operations (curves, lift-gamma-gain gamma=2, cdl slope=2); tests/test_effects.py::test_alpha_channel_operations_remultiply (curves alpha); tests/test_effects_full.py::test_bradford_reference_and_kelvin_neutrality; tests/test_kernels.py (fused lift-gamma-gain)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs arms color-grade L1533, lift-gamma-gain L1542, cdl L1549, curves L1571 (curve_table L1164), levels L1598, white-balance L1614, hue-saturation L1622; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl grade() cases 1-6 and 8 L366-415 (rgb2hsv/hsv2rgb L113-134); crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::colour_operations_match_reference_formulas L225

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/grade-color-grade.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with <effect type='color-grade' saturation='0.5' contrast='1.3' brightness='0.05'/> Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (26.0 dB; defaults alone are 99 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/grade-lift-gamma-gain.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with lift='0.1,0.0,0.0' gamma='1.2,1,1' gain='1.1,1,0.9' Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: CLOSE (46.7 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/grade-cdl.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with cdl slope='1.2,1,0.9' offset='0.02,0,0' power='1.1,1,1' saturation='0.8'; second variant slope='2,2,2' (values above 1 survive) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: CLOSE (34.0 dB; slope=2 variant not measured)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/grade-curves.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with curve='0,0 0.5,0.6 1,1'; variants channel='red' and channel='luma' Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: CLOSE (46.2 dB for rgb; variants not measured)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/grade-white-balance.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with temperature='30' tint='10' Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (10.3 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/grade-hue-saturation.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with hue='40' saturation='1.3' brightness='0.05' Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (28.4 dB)).
- Port Rust test `colour_operations_match_reference_formulas` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): color-grade saturation=0 on src (200,100,50) gives grey l = 0.2126*lin(200)+0.7152*lin(100)+0.0722*lin(50) (linear luma) within 1e-2; cdl slope=2 on gray 128 gives srgb_to_linear(min(2*128/255, 10)) = srgb_to_linear(1.0039) within 2e-2 (no upper clamp); exposure=1 on gray gives 2*lin(128) (guard) Python: fixtures gray.png/src.png from tests/fxref.py; project 64x32; layers scale 2 at x=0/8/40; frame_linear(0)[4, x, :3]; same expected numbers; Python's frame_linear is premultiplied linear, alpha is 1 here
- Eval-level: Not applicable (per-pixel colour; eval prints no pixels).

**Risks.** Colour ops are deterministic: expect 50+ dB once formulas agree (8-bit PNG quantisation and half-float intermediates). curves: Rust samples the 256-entry table with a bilinear texture lookup at x*(255/256)+0.5/256, reproduce that quantisation. Premultiplied handling: Rust unpremultiplies (alpha<=0 returns untouched). HSV hue wraps with fract(). Decision flagged for color-grade (D9) and kelvin white balance.

#### P3-FX-08 — Colour styling: tint, tritone, gradient-map/overlay, grayscale, sepia, invert, posterize, threshold, color-overlay, selective-color  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-12 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01

**Goal.** Rust semantics by default: tint = mix(c, luma(c)*tint_rgb, amount) on linear colour, default tint (1,0.8,0.6), colour alpha ignored; tritone = encoded luma l; l<0.5 ? mix(black, mid, 2l) : mix(mid, white, 2(l-0.5)), mid = @color default (1,0.5,0.2), then mix(c, t, amount) (keyColor not read); gradient-map = encoded luma through a 256-entry premultiplied table of the paint's stops (linear interpolation of stored RGBA, sampled at l*(255/256)+0.5/256), unpremultiplied, mix(c, g, amount), pixel alpha kept; gradient-overlay = t = clamp(dot(uv-0.5, d)/(|dx|+|dy|)+0.5, 0, 1) in target uv, d = (cos angle, sin angle), mix(c, g, amount*g.a); grayscale = mix(c, luma(c), amount); sepia = mix(c, dec(min(M*enc(c), 1)), amount) with the standard sepia matrix (mix in linear); invert = mix in ENCODED space with channels rgb/red/green/blue/luma (luma: e + (1 - 2*luma(e))); posterize = floor(enc*(levels-1)+0.5)/(levels-1) (ties round up, levels default 8, min 2); threshold = enc(linear luma) >= threshold (luma of the linear colour, not of encoded channels); color-overlay = mix(c, colour, amount*colour.a) default colour (1,0.5,0.2,1); selective-color = hue window on encoded HSV: d = |fract(h - hue/360 + 0.5) - 0.5|, w = 1 - smoothstep(tol/2, tol/2+0.05, d), s *= mix(1, saturation, w), v += brightness*w (tolerance default 0.2); Python's CMYK family model is used only when <param name='family'> (or per-family cyan/magenta/yellow/black params) is present. Python extras on gradient-map (midpoint, interpolationSpace, source scanline) stay as extras only where Rust's gradient table cannot express them (verify render_fx.rs gradient_value L320-336 first).

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/color.py (tint L297, tritone L304, gradient_map L314, color_overlay L323, gradient_overlay L331, grayscale L263, invert L269, sepia L276, posterize L283, threshold L290, selective_color L345); scenerender/effects/__init__.py is NOT edited here (group A); gradient_colors() is only called  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_colour_semantics (tint, color-overlay, grayscale); tests/test_effects.py::test_display_referred_operations (posterize levels=3 expects 0: Rust gives 0.5 at the exact tie); tests/test_effects.py::test_threshold_is_binary_and_preserves_matte; tests/test_effects.py::test_gradient_map_paint_stop_space_and_midpoint; tests/test_effects_full.py::test_gradient_overlay_angle_and_alpha, ::test_selective_cmyk_families; tests/test_effects.py::test_translucent_style_colours_stay_premultiplied

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs arms tint/tritone/color-overlay/fill L1635, gradient-map/overlay L1653 (gradient_table L1202), grayscale/sepia L1668, invert L1672, posterize L1683, threshold L1687, selective-color L1691; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl grade() cases 10-20 L417-465; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::colour_operations_match_reference_formulas L225

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/style-tint.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with tint color='#3080FFFF' amount='0.7' (plus a no-attribute default variant) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (17.8 dB; default 25.2)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/style-tritone.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with tritone color='#FF8000FF' amount='0.8' (plus default) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (16.5 dB; default 13.0)).

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/style-posterize-threshold.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: posterize levels='4' (guard) and threshold threshold='0.5' (+ default 0.7) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: posterize MATCH (83.0), threshold DIFFERENT (15.6 dB; default 23.6)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/style-color-overlay.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with color-overlay color='#FF0000FF' amount='0.5' (+ default) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (12.1 dB; default 13.4)).

- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** HSV and smoothstep windows are deterministic; gradient-map/overlay depend on the 256-entry table quantisation (<= 1/255 steps). amount mixing space differs per op (grayscale/sepia/tint mix linear values, invert mixes encoded values): copy exactly. Python tests that pin midpoint/interpolationSpace behaviour need a decision (those attributes are not representable in Rust's table; keep them only through the existing paint stops path).

#### P3-FX-09 — Tone-mapping algorithms: tonemap effect and colorManagement toneMapping  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-13 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01

**Goal.** Decision-gated parity profile 'rust': the tonemap effect and the finishing stage use Rust's formulas on working-space linear values: aces = Narkowicz fit clamp((c*(2.51c+0.03))/(c*(2.43c+0.59)+0.14), 0, 1); agx = inset matrix, log2 shaper (log2(x)+12.47393)/16.5 clamped, 6th-order polynomial (15.5x^6 - 40.14x^5 + 31.96x^4 - 6.868x^3 + 0.4298x^2 + 0.1191x - 0.00232), outset matrix, clamp, decode sRGB (matrices given column by column; white must stay neutral); filmic = Hejl-Burgess-Dawson on max(0, c-0.004): (x*(6.2x+0.5))/(x*(6.2x+1.7)+0.06), result display-encoded then decoded; reinhard = c/(1+c); hable = Uncharted 2 on 2*c normalised by h(11.2); pq-to-sdr = luminance knee ks=0.75: o = l for l<=ks else ks + (1-ks)*(1-exp(-(l-ks)/(1-ks))), colour scaled by o/l; aces2 falls back to the ACES fit; the effect also applies c*2^exposure first (@exposure default 0, which Python ignores today); finishing maps aces|aces2 -> aces, agx, filmic, reinhard and runs in the working primaries (no conversion to Rec.709 first). Python's exact AgX/ACES 2.0/BT.2390 stay as an explicit opt-in (<param name='accuracy' value='exact'/> on the effect, SCENERENDER_TONEMAP=exact for finishing) because they are more accurate than Rust; the default choice is for the user.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/color.py (tonemap L234); scenerender/color.py (tone_map_to L751-786, tone_map, aces2, agx wiring); scenerender/effects/color_science.py, scenerender/effects/agx_data.py (kept for exact mode); scenerender/color_lut.py (the 65^3 bake must bake the same curve)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_color.py::test_tone_mappers; tests/test_color_mgmt.py::test_agx_matches_published_algorithm, ::test_aces2_output_transform, ::test_builtin_pipeline_aces2_and_agx; tests/test_effects_full.py::test_tonemappers_monotonic_hdr_and_alpha, ::test_aces_and_agx_reference_processors, ::test_bt2390_preserves_low_light_and_maps_peak, ::test_agx_published_blender_4_reference_values; tests/test_perf_color.py::test_lut_matches_exact_every_tone_mapper, ::test_explainer_plan_aces2_with_acescct_look

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl::tonemap L291-333 and grade case 9 L(c * exp2(exposure)); crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs tonemap arm L1627-1634; crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs finishing tone mapping match L1963-1970; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::agx_keeps_white_and_grey_neutral L794, ::colour_operations_match_reference_formulas (tonemap reinhard); README Limitations 'aces2 uses the ACES fit'

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/tonemap-aces.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: six scenes T1 with <effect type='tonemap' tonemapper='X' exposure='0.5'/>, X in aces, agx, filmic, reinhard, hable, pq-to-sdr; plus tonemap-hdr: effects='x e' with exposure=3 then tonemapper aces Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB each (decision 'rust' profile)** (today: DIFFERENT (aces 17.1, agx 25.0, filmic 16.0, reinhard 30.2, hable 19.7, pq-to-sdr 18.0, hdr-exp3 15.3; reinhard without exposure is MATCH 72.9)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/finish-tonemap.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: project 128x128 <colorManagement toneMapping='X' exposure='1'/> placed after <project> and before <assets>; T1 body without effect; X in reinhard, aces, agx, filmic, aces2 Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB (finishing is baked into a 65^3 LUT in Python)** (today: DIFFERENT (aces 16.4, agx 25.1, filmic 23.8, aces2 16.5); reinhard CLOSE 43.1; exposure only MATCH 69.0).
- Port Rust test `agx_keeps_white_and_grey_neutral` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): white and #808080 rectangles through toneMapping='agx' stay neutral (max-min channel spread < 0.01) for workingSpace linear-srgb and acescg Python: two documents with <colorManagement workingSpace=... toneMapping='agx'/> and project workingColorSpace; compute the spread of frame pixels at (16,16) and (48,16) after the output encode; also run under the exact profile
- Port Rust test `colour_operations_match_reference_formulas` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): white layer with exposure=1 then tonemap tonemapper='reinhard' gives 2/3 within 1e-2 Python: layer effects='exp tmo'; frame_linear(0)[4,60,:3] == 2/3
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** Python is the more accurate implementation (exact Blender AgX, OCIO ACES 2.0); Rust's fits are cheap approximations and aces2 is knowingly the ACES fit. Do not silently lose accuracy: implement both and make the default an explicit user decision. Rust applies the curve in the working primaries; Python converts to Rec.709 first. LUT-baked finishing (<=2 code values) caps finishing probes at CLOSE.

#### P3-FX-10 — Colour finishing order and look semantics  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-41, FX-42 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-09

**Goal.** Finishing order becomes looks (in order, each blended by mix), then exposure in stops, then toneMapping, then the output encode. A CDL look (slope/offset/power/saturation, comma lists as for effects, one or two numbers replicate) is applied on sRGB-encoded values with pow(max(e*slope+offset,0),power) and Rec.709 saturation, and is ignored when the look has @src (the LUT wins); a LUT look applies in the look's @space (default acescct) through the working-to-space matrix and that space's transfer. With an ocioConfig the order is: file/CDL looks (built-in), exposure, then the OCIO transform (named OCIO looks inside it, then display/view) which replaces toneMapping; the 65^3 baked plan in color_lut.py follows the same order. The in-process PyOpenColorIO mechanism vs Rust's ociobakelut is by design and stays. look@space for CDL is flagged (Rust ignores it).

**Python files.** scenerender/color.py (process_rgb L1077-1103, _plan L942, _look_entry L911, _process_ocio L1054, _lut_basis); scenerender/color_lut.py (bake plan order)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_color.py::test_exposure_and_looks, ::test_cdl; tests/test_color_mgmt.py::test_ocio_pipeline_aces_view_and_look, ::test_look_with_spi1d_in_document; tests/test_perf_color.py::test_lut_matches_exact_every_tone_mapper, ::test_lut_ocio_pipeline, ::test_explainer_plan_aces2_with_acescct_look

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs::finishing L1851-1982 (looks loop L1901-1946, exposure L1947, ocio lut_pass_between L1951-1957, tone map L1962-1971); crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::colour_finishing_applies_looks_exposure_and_tone_mapping L534, ::ocio_configs_match_opencolorio L545 (fixture crates/sr-gpu/tests/fixtures/ocio/expected.json from tools/fixtures/make_ocio_expected.py); schema scene-render-1.1.xsd lookType L3800-3809 and colorManagementType L3810-3836

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/finish-look-cdl.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: <colorManagement looks='lk'><look id='lk' slope='1.2,1,0.8' offset='0.02,0,0' power='1.1' saturation='0.8'/></colorManagement> (before <assets>) over the T1 body, no effects Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (15.5 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/finish-order.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: same look with exposure='1' toneMapping='reinhard' (non-identity look so order matters; the Rust test uses an identity look and cannot detect it) Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (16.2 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/finish-look-mix.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: look slope='1.5,1,1' mix='0.5' Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (11.5 dB)).

- Port Rust test `colour_finishing_applies_looks_exposure_and_tone_mapping` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): gray 128 layer, colorManagement looks='lk' exposure=1 toneMapping='reinhard' with an identity CDL look: pixel (8,8) = g/(1+g) with g = 2*lin(128) within 1e-2 Python: Renderer.open(doc).frame_linear(0) after finishing (use the same hook path as tests/test_color.py::test_exposure_and_looks); extend with a non-identity look to pin the order look->exposure->tonemap analytically: reinhard(2^e * cdl(x))

- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** Default 3D-LUT bake (65^3 tetrahedral, <=2 code values) caps finishing probes at CLOSE. The Rust test uses an identity look and is blind to ordering; the new probe is the real gate. FX-42's mechanism (in-process OCIO vs ociobakelut, ocio:// configs, fallback messages) is not changed here and belongs to the interop area.

#### P3-FX-11 — Drop/inner shadow, inner glow, long shadow, bevel: Rust attribute meaning and rotated vectors  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-08, FX-09, FX-20, FX-02 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02

**Goal.** inner-glow default colour (1,1,0.8,1) (currently white); drop-shadow and inner-shadow offsets (offsetX/Y default 8,8) and long-shadow direction use Params.offset/dirv so they rotate and scale with the node; long-shadow reads @size (length, default 60 when size <= 1, in node units, at most 128 samples), @angle, default colour (0,0,0,0.6) and the Rust fade: alpha = max over k=1..n of source alpha at pixel - k*step times (1 - k/(n+1)*(1 - colour.alpha)), colour*alpha drawn behind the content (no intensity, no <param fade>, no raster supercover; Python's exact-raster/fade model stays behind <param name='model' value='raster'/>); bevel (the part of FX-20 living in shadow.py) = alpha-gradient shading with step max(size,2)*px (size default 1 -> 2), angle default -45 deg (0 means -45), n = normalize(-2*(dAlpha/dx, dAlpha/dy), 1), l = normalize(cos a, sin a, 0.7), add = (dot(n,l) - l.z)*intensity*0.5 added to rgb times alpha, alpha unchanged, no position/style/soften/colour/compositeOriginal (Python's styles stay behind <param name='style'>).

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/shadow.py (drop_shadow L22, inner_shadow L33, inner_glow L44, bevel L70, long_shadow L113, _extrusion_offsets L101)

#### P3-FX-12 — Alpha morphology: stroke, outline, matte-choke  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-10 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02

**Goal.** stroke/outline read @size (width in node units, min 1 px after scaling; default 1) and @position outside/inside/center, default colour opaque white; they are different passes: stroke draws the ring together with the content (outside: ring behind the content; inside/center: ring over it), outline draws the ring ALONE; the ring is max(alpha over the disc) - alpha outside, alpha - min inside, max - min centred (both extrema over the same radius). The disc is Rust's: radius <= 4 px samples rings (rings = clamp(ceil(r/1.5),1,12), ring radius r*ring/rings, 8+4*ring points, bilinear alpha with transparent outside); wider radii use the exact disc with a one-texel soft rim: weight w = clamp(r+1-dist, 0, 1), dilate = max(a*w), erode = min(a + 1 - w), transparent beyond the frame. matte-choke: amount in node px (positive erodes, negative dilates, default 1), same disc, partial alpha preserved (erode scales premultiplied colour by min/alpha, dilate unpremultiplied colour * new alpha, so the grown area is colourless), softness blurs the result with sigma = 2*softness*px when softness*px*4 >= 1 (default 0.1). Python's pre-blur/threshold softness, iterations and colour-carrying spread stay behind <param name='model' value='python'/>.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/shadow.py (stroke L61 registers 'stroke' and 'outline', _ring L52 -> new morph_disc helper in this file); scenerender/effects/keying.py (matte_choke L66)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_style_position_and_original_none (stroke/outline positions); tests/test_effects.py::test_keying_and_choke; tests/test_effects_full.py::test_matte_choke_iterations_and_gray_softness; tests/test_effects.py::test_translucent_style_colours_stay_premultiplied (stroke, outline)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs stroke/outline/matte-choke arm L2035-2054, morph_field L1276, distance_field L1306, RING_RADIUS L1269; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_morph L1074-1128, morph_wide L1036, morph_weigh L1013; crates/sr-gpu/tests/morphology.rs (reference() L20-43 is the exact disc definition); crates/sr-gpu/tests/effect_costs.rs::wide_strokes_cover_every_pixel_within_their_width L137, ::matte_choke_erodes_by_its_amount L177, ::posterized_layers_keep_their_effect_targets_to_the_layer L31

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/morph-stroke.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with stroke size='3' color='#FF0000FF' position='outside'; variants position='inside' and 'center', and a no-attribute default Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (22.3 dB; default 12.4)).

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/morph-matte-choke.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with matte-choke amount='3' softness='0'; variants amount='-3', amount='3' softness='0.5', no attributes Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: CLOSE (56.1 dB at amount 3; default 37.5)).
- Port Rust test `wide_morphology_follows_every_level_of_alpha` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/morphology.rs): a 60x50 plate (#FFFFFF40) with an anti-aliased ellipse (#FFFFFFFF or #FFFFFFCC) inside a group, stroke size 6/13 outside, inside, matte-choke amount +-6/13: result matches the exact disc reference (mean abs diff < 3e-3, at most 32 pixels off by > 0.2) Python: copy reference() (weight w=clamp(r+1-dist,0,1); dilate best=max(best,a*w); erode best=min(best,a+1-w); outside the frame alpha 0) into tests/fxref.py as numpy; render the scene with and without the effect (group effects=...), compare ring/alpha as in the Rust test
- Port Rust test `wide_chokes_keep_soft_edges_soft` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/morphology.rs): a blur radius=5 square then matte-choke amount +-7 softness=0 equals the exact disc reference of the blurred alpha (mean < 4e-3, 0 pixels off) Python: effects='soft choke'; compare alpha channel of frame_linear to reference(alpha_of_soft, 128, 7, erode)
- Port Rust test `matte_choke_erodes_by_its_amount` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effect_costs.rs): white 40x40 square at (28,28) with matte-choke amount=12 softness=0 shrinks to 40..56: alpha > 0.95 at (42,48),(48,42),(53,53); < 0.05 at (37,48),(48,37),(59,48),(30,30) Python: direct pixel asserts on frame_linear alpha
- Port Rust test `posterized_layers_keep_their_effect_targets_to_the_layer` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effect_costs.rs): white 4x4 layer scale 8 moving 120 px/s with posterize-time frequency=30 then stroke size=0.5 (4 px at scale 8) color #FF0000 at t=0.51: (116,66) white, (98,66) red (tol 5e-2), alpha (140,66) < 0.02 Python: needs P3-FX-02 (scale) and P3-FX-25 (posterize); pixel asserts on frame_linear(0.51)
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** Python's morphology() helper blends 'a + (v-a)*weight' for the rim, Rust uses a*w (dilate) and a+1-w (erode): use the Rust definition; the ring-sampling branch for r <= 4 is an approximation of the disc and must be reproduced, not 'improved', to match. center position draws a band 2*size wide in Rust (see rust_may_be_wrong). Matte-choke dilation leaves rgb=0 in grown texels in Rust (black fringe over opaque backgrounds is premultiplied-black alpha); Python's colour carry stays only under the python model.

#### P3-FX-13 — Glow, bloom, halation (soft knee, tint, Dual-Kawase helper)  

Size **S** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-07 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02

**Goal.** Bright pass per Rust: l = luma(linear straight colour in working space); glow uses a hard threshold (f = (l - threshold)/l), bloom and halation a soft knee 0.5: w = clamp((l - th + 0.5)/1.0, 0, 1), f = max(w*w*0.5, l - th)/l, colour kept = premultiplied rgb * clamp(f,0,1000) * tint, alpha * clamp(f,0,1); tint = @color (white), halation default (1,0.3,0.12); the pass is blurred with sigma = max(radius*px, 0.5) and ADDED over the content: rgb = s.rgb + p.rgb*intensity, alpha = max(s.a, min(1, s.a + p.a*intensity)); threshold default 0.7, intensity 1, radius 4. Python's multi-radius film halation stays behind <param name='model' value='film'/>. Optionally add scenerender/effects/kawase.py with Rust's Dual-Kawase pyramid (levels = clamp(ceil(log2(sigma/1.45/1.5)),1,10), offset = clamp(sigma/1.45/2^levels, 0.2, 3), 4-tap down and 8-tap up filters with transparent beyond the input) used by glow/bloom/halation here and by group B shadows and group C unsharp-mask, to lift MATCH from ~40 to 50+ dB; the Gaussian stays the fallback and is already within CLOSE. gl_bloom.py (optional GPU path) must implement the same knee.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/light.py (glow L33, _bloom L40, bloom L89, halation L94); scenerender/effects/gl_bloom.py; scenerender/effects/kawase.py (new, optional)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_glow_is_a_thresholded_bloom; tests/test_gl_bloom.py; tests/test_perf_halation.py (pins the film model); tests/test_effects_full.py::test_halation_works_on_opaque_photographs

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs glow/bloom/halation arm L1430-1443, blur() Dual-Kawase L1325-1350, combine() L1352; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_pre case 0 L216-224, fs_down/fs_up L191-210, fs_combine case 1 L244; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::glow_adds_a_bloom_over_the_content L106, ::glow_and_drop_shadow L37

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/glow-bloom.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with bloom radius='4' threshold='0.5' intensity='1' (+ default) Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB** (today: DIFFERENT/CLOSE (30.8 dB; default 33.0)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/glow-halation.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with halation radius='4' threshold='0.5' intensity='1' (+ default) Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB** (today: DIFFERENT/CLOSE (29.9 dB; default 33.5)).
- Port Rust test `glow_adds_a_bloom_over_the_content` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): src (200,100,50) layer scale 4 at (16,8) with glow radius=0.5 threshold=0 intensity=1 on a transparent frame: centre pixel (24,16) = 2*lin(200), 2*lin(100), 2*lin(50), alpha 1 within 2e-2; alpha at (14,16) > 0.1 Python: fixtures src.png; frame_linear(0)[16,24,:3] compared with 2*srgb_to_linear([200,100,50]/255); alpha[16,14] > 0.1
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** Soft-knee constants are arbitrary (see rust_may_be_wrong); copy them. Kawase vs Gaussian: sigma = 1.45*offset*2^levels, measured on an impulse by the Rust authors; a numpy port is ~40 lines. Alpha combine differs trivially today (Python src.a + halo.a*(1-src.a), Rust min(1, s.a + p.a*k)).

#### P3-FX-14 — Light overlay generators: lens-flare, light-leak, light-sweep, god-rays  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-17 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02, P3-FX-04

**Goal.** The four effects follow Rust's formulas on the whole-frame target (UNBOUNDED): lens-flare source = @centerX/@centerY (node-local px) else uv (0.25, 0.25), size = max(@size, 0.1) (default 1), colour default (1,0.9,0.7): core l = 0.02*size/(|p|+0.02*size) with p = (uv - src)*(aspect,1), 4 ghosts at src + (0.5 - src)*0.6k with radius 0.02*k*size and weight 0.25/k, horizontal streak exp(-|p.y|*200/max(size,0.1))*exp(-|p.x|*3)*0.5, added: rgb + colour*(l+streak)*intensity, alpha = max(s.a, min(1, luma(add))); light-leak: t = time*speed, n = fbm(uv*1.5 + (0.15t, 0.05t), D24 seed, 5 octaves), edge = (1 - clamp(|uv - (0.9, 0.1)|*1.1, 0, 1))^2, w = clamp(n*edge*2, 0, 1)*intensity, rgb += colour*w*s.a (default colour (1,0.55,0.2)); light-sweep: pos = fract(t*speed*0.5)*1.6 - 0.3, width = size>1 ? size*px/frame_width : 0.08, angle+30 deg, w = exp(-((dot(uv,dir) - pos)/max(width,1e-3))^2)*intensity*0.8, rgb += colour*w*s.a (default white); god-rays: bright pass (threshold, knee 0.2, tint 1) scattered towards the centre over n = clamp(samples,4,128) taps (s = 1 - clamp(amount*0.5,0,1)*k/n), weights 0.97^k normalised, result * intensity added with rgb + rays.rgb and alpha = max(s.a, min(1, s.a+rays.a)). Python's occlusion-aware god-rays, ghost/blade flare and radius-driven leak stay behind <param name='model' value='python'/>.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/light.py (lens_flare L302, light_leak L324, light_sweep L342, god_rays L353, _add_light L292)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py (test_every_effect_contract covers lens-flare, light-leak, light-sweep, god-rays); tests/test_effects_full.py::test_optical_artifacts_scale_color_time, ::test_god_rays_blocker_occludes

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs light-leak L1756, light-sweep L1762, lens-flare L1774, god-rays L1492; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_color cases 67-69 L612-639, fs_line case 3 L863-880, d24_fbm L145-160; crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs UNBOUNDED L84-102

**Acceptance gate.**

- Port Rust test `every_effect_type_draws` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): the four effects draw with finite pixels and at least one pass (the only Rust test that touches them) Python: covered by P3-FX-01's port; formulas are pinned with numpy reference functions of the WGSL cases in tests/fxref.py evaluated on a 128x128 grid and compared to Python output at <= 1e-3 on a black input
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** fbm/D24 hash must be bit-compatible: Python effects.fields.fractal(kind='basic') must reproduce d24_fbm (octave weights 0.5^i, seed + 101 i + 7919 phase, smoothstep cross-fade, 5 octaves here, 6 for noise effects); verify against the Rust output before trusting. uv is relative to the Rust target (the frame for these effects) so this depends on P3-FX-04. Light overlays on a layer node paint outside the layer (alpha raised outside it in lens-flare); that is Rust behaviour and is mirrored.

#### P3-FX-15 — Lighting effect: luminance relief, ambient + directional + 4 point lights, node-local light coordinates  

Size **M** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-19 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02, P3-FX-04

**Goal.** Default model 'rust': normals from the luminance gradient, h = (L(x+1) - L(x), L(y+1) - L(y)) * max(@relief,0.2) * 20 with L the working luma of the straight colour, n = normalize(-2h, 1); light = 0.25 (ambient) + max(dot(n, normalize(cos a, sin a, 0.8)),0)*0.75 (a = angle, 0 means -45 deg) + for up to four referenced lights (any light type is read as a point light with @x, @y in the NODE's local units mapped through the node transform, @range or @distance in node units divided by frame height, default range 0.5, @intensity): dv = ((lightUV - uv)*(aspect,1), 0.15), fall = clamp(1 - |dv.xy|/range,0,1), light += max(dot(n, normalize(dv)),0)*intensity*fall^2; result rgb = s.rgb*mix(1, light, I)*mix(1, colour, I) with I = clamp(@intensity,0,1), alpha unchanged, colour = @color (white). Python's alpha-relief model with 8 light types, area integration, ray-marched shadows, kelvin and cone stays behind <param name='model' value='full'/> (it is the more correct treatment of non-point lights; flagged).

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/light.py (lighting L199-298, _lighting_fused L168, _light_power L158, _shadow L131); scenerender/kernels.py (fused lighting kernel if present)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_lighting_ambient_and_point_frame_coordinates (frame coordinates: becomes node-local); tests/test_effects.py::test_lighting_types_respond_to_colour_intensity_and_scale; tests/test_effects_full.py::test_every_light_type_kelvin_intensity, ::test_lighting_cast_shadow_and_spot_cone; tests/test_kernels.py, tests/test_light_temperature.py

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs lighting arm L2021-2034; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_conv op 3 L937-1010; crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs::lights_of L699-722; crates/sr-gpu/tests/quality.rs::point_lights_fall_off_over_their_range_in_document_units L220

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/lighting-point.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with <lights><light id='pl' type='point' x='60' y='50'/></lights> (after the composition) and <effect type='lighting' lights='pl' intensity='0.8' angle='-45'/>: the light position is node-local, i.e. frame (76,66) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (18.8 dB; default without lights 22.8)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/lighting-relief.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: same with relief='3' and no lights (ambient + directional only) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: not measured (expected DIFFERENT)).
- Port Rust test `point_lights_fall_off_over_their_range_in_document_units` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/quality.rs): a point light's falloff range is in document units at every quality tier (range scaled by node scale / frame height) Python: render lighting with a point light range=30 at two --scale values (1 and 2); frame_linear brightness at corresponding points must agree within 1e-3 (Python test exists for this: tests/test_effects.py::test_lighting_types_respond_to_colour_intensity_and_scale)
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** Light x/y/range are node-local units in Rust but frame-space in Python today: this is a behavioural break that existing documents may rely on, flag it. Lights of type ambient/directional/spot are treated as point lights by Rust (ambient has no position): decide whether to copy. 4-light cap. luminance-gradient relief is texture-dependent, so a stray 1-ulp difference in luma coefficients is visible at 8 bits only near edges.

#### P3-FX-16 — Keyers: chroma-key, luma-key, difference-key, spill-suppress  

Size **S** · track **P3-A** · rows: FX-26 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01

**Goal.** chroma-key: c = encoded sRGB of the linear straight colour, k = encoded keyColor (default #00FF00), chroma plane Cb = dot(.,(-0.1146,-0.3854,0.5)), Cr = dot(.,(0.5,-0.4542,-0.0458)), d = |(Cb,Cr) - (Cbk,Crk)|/0.7 (luma ignored), alpha *= smoothstep(tolerance, tolerance+max(softness,1e-4), d) (defaults 0.2 and 0.1), then despill of the dominant channel of keyColor: o[ch] = mix(o[ch], min(o[ch], mean(other two)), spill) (default 0.5) on linear straight colour, output premultiplied by the new alpha; luma-key: l = encoded(luma of linear colour), a = smoothstep(threshold - softness/2, threshold + softness/2 + 1e-4, l), inverted when @amount < 0, threshold default 0.7, softness 0.1, output s*a; difference-key: d = |enc(straight src) - enc(straight plate)| (unnormalised length), a = smoothstep(tol, tol+max(soft,1e-4), d), plate alpha ignored, output s*a, @source required (a node, see P3-FX-04); spill-suppress: dominant channel of keyColor limited towards the mean of the other two by @spill (default 0.5), mixed, on linear straight colour.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/keying.py (chroma_key L23, luma_key L35, difference_key L44, spill_suppress L58, _despill L13)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_keying_and_choke, ::test_source_node_inputs_and_offsets, ::test_source_opacity_and_transparent_plate, ::test_colour_semantics (spill-suppress spill=1 expects (.2,.3,.3): Rust gives (.2,.25,.3)); tests/test_effects_full.py (difference-key plate cases); tests/test_reference_pullback.py, tests/test_reference_source_projection.py (difference-key sources)
