#### P3-FX-17 — Blur family: radial-blur, zoom-blur, lens-blur, tilt-shift (and rotated directional-blur)  

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: FX-05, FX-06, FX-02 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02

**Goal.** radial-blur spin = @angle degrees when non-zero else amount*10 (default amount 1 gives 10 deg), samples a = spin*((k+0.5)/n - 0.5) rotating pixel-space offsets about the centre (centerX/Y node-local px, default the node's box centre), n = clamp(samples,1,256); zoom-blur samples scale s = 1 - amount*0.1*(k+0.5)/n about the centre (amount 1 = 10 %); lens-blur = Rust's gather: radius px, blades = @levels (default 8, < 3 means a round aperture), @angle blade rotation, rings = clamp(ceil(sqrt(samples/3)),1,6), ring r has 6r taps at radius r/rings * radius scaled by cos(seg/2)/cos(local angle) for polygons, tap weight 1 + max(luma(straight tap) - threshold, 0)*2*amount (threshold default 0.7, amount default 1), pass-through when radius < 0.5 px; tilt-shift = the same gather with radius scaled by smoothstep(band, band+0.25, |distance from the focus line|) in uv, line through (0.5, centerY) at @angle, band = size <= 1 ? 0.1 : size*px/height*0.5. directional-blur direction (FX-02) uses Params.dirv(radius*2, angle). Python's FFT disc/polygon (<param name='blades'>), Gaussian-stack tilt-shift and padded-tile geometry stay behind <param name='model' value='fft'/>.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/blur.py (directional_blur L43, radial_blur L50, zoom_blur L70, lens_blur L90, tilt_shift L143, _count L21)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects_full.py::test_radial_arc_and_zoom_trajectory (zoom amount=60 and amount=0 identity; Rust amount=60 gives scale 1-6k, i.e. out of range); tests/test_effects_full.py::test_lens_polygon_and_highlight_energy, ::test_tilt_shift_uses_intermediate_radius; tests/test_effects.py::test_lens_blur_does_not_translate_image, ::test_blur_conserves_alpha_and_grows (blur kinds)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs radial-blur L1471, zoom-blur L1482, directional L1466, lens-blur/tilt-shift L1516-1532; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_line cases 0-2 L833-862, fs_bokeh L893-935; README 'radial blur angle (spin, else amount x 10 deg); zoom blur amount x 10 % zoom; lens blur ...; tilt-shift centerY and size'

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/blur-radial.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T2 with radial-blur (no attributes: 10 deg default) and a second scene with angle='12' samples='16' (guard) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: default DIFFERENT (19.1 dB); angle='12' MATCH (68.5 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/blur-zoom.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T2 with zoom-blur amount='1' samples='16' (+ amount='0.4') Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (18.3 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/blur-lens.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with lens-blur radius='6' levels='6' angle='10' samples='16' threshold='0.6' amount='1' (+ default: levels 8, radius 4) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: CLOSE (35.3 dB; default 35.9)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/blur-tilt-shift.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with tilt-shift radius='6' size='20' centerY='48' (+ angle='20' variant) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (18.6 dB; default 18.8)).
- Port Rust test `fs_line cases 1-2 (no Rust test; formula reference)` (crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl): zoom: output(p) = mean_k S(c + (p-c)*s_k), s_k = 1 - 0.1*amount*(k+0.5)/n; radial: mean_k S(c + R(a_k)(p-c)), a_k = spin*((k+0.5)/n - 0.5) Python: impulse test: a 1-px dot at distance 20 px from the centre, zoom amount=1 samples=64 spreads the dot over radius 20 .. 22.2 px (+-0.6); radial default spreads it over an arc of 20*10deg = 3.5 px; write numpy reference loops in tests/fxref.py and compare to the handler output on a random 64x64 tile at <= 1e-4
- Port Rust test `fs_bokeh (formula reference)` (crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl): taps: centre plus rings*(rings+1)*3 taps, weights 1 + max(l - thr,0)*gain, polygon radius scale cos(seg/2)/cos(local) Python: numpy reference of the tap loop; compare on an impulse and on the test card at <= 1e-4
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** Rust's 37-tap default lens blur is a coarse gather; reproduce it (do not improve) or the probe stays at ~35 dB. Zoom amounts are 10x more aggressive than Python's percent. Tilt-shift band is in uv (frame-height) units and uses the node-local centerY. unbounded targets: radial/zoom/tilt use the whole frame so a T1 comparison also depends on P3-FX-04.

#### P3-FX-18 — Screen and block effects: letterbox, scanlines, pixelate, mosaic  

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: FX-15, FX-22, FX-25 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02, P3-FX-04, P3-FX-05

**Goal.** letterbox: size = target aspect ratio (default 2.39 when size <= 1.01); the target is the whole frame, bars are horizontal when the ratio is wider than the frame, vertical (pillarbox) otherwise, colour default black, premultiplied; scanlines: period = max(size,1)*4*px, rows with fract(y/period) < 0.5 are darkened by amount*0.5 (clamped), amount default 1, phase counted from the target's top; pixelate: cell = size > 1 ? size : 16 (node units * px), uv = (floor(uv*d/s)+0.5)*s/d, sampled with the edge mode; mosaic: cell the same, mean of 4x4 bilinear samples per cell (no edge mode). Cells are aligned to the Rust target origin (P3-FX-04). Python's blank-line wave, bar-height letterbox and exact-mean mosaic stay behind <param name='model'>.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/stylize.py (letterbox L319, scanlines L158, pixelate L195, mosaic L205)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_block_effects_have_constant_cells (size=4 cells, mosaic conserves energy: keep for the new model); tests/test_effects.py::test_every_effect_contract; no test for letterbox/scanlines (grep)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs letterbox L1745, scanlines L1751, pixelate/mosaic L1961-1968; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl cases 65-66 L600-611, warp cases 12-13 L776-790; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::keyers_and_warps L288 (pixelate size=1.001)

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/screen-letterbox.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T2 with letterbox size='2.39' (and size='1.0' -> default 2.39; size='1.0' variant is deliberately ambiguous, see rust_may_be_wrong) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (10.2 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/screen-scanlines.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with scanlines size='1' amount='0.8' at layer x=19 y=21 (+ default) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (24.4 dB; default 22.2)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/screen-pixelate.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with pixelate size='8' at x=19,y=21 and default (size 16); mosaic size='8' and default Expect **MATCH >= 60 dB (cell edges must coincide)** (today: pixelate 8 at 16,16 MATCH 67.4 but at 19,21 only 32.6; default 15.3; mosaic 8 at 16,16 69.2, at 19,21 24.9; default 16.0).
- Port Rust test `keyers_and_warps` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): pixelate size=1.001 on a 16x scaled 2x2 quad: each 16 px cell is one colour, (33,2) == (45,13) and (49,2) == (61,13) within 1e-3, neighbouring cells differ by > 0.1 (cells (40,8) vs (56,8) and (40,8) vs (40,24)) Python: quad.png fixture scaled 16 at x=32 on a 64x32 frame; compare frame_linear pixels; note the author must write size='1.001' to get a 1 px cell in Rust (size <= 1 is the sentinel for 'unset')
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** Pixel-grid alignment is the whole game here and relies on P3-FX-04's target rect. The sentinel size<=1 -> default means pixelate size=1 is not authorable in Rust (flagged). mosaic is listed under edgeMode in fx.rs but its shader case returns before edge handling: do not wire it.

#### P3-FX-19 — Halftone and VHS  

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: FX-22, FX-25 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02, P3-FX-04, P3-FX-05

**Goal.** halftone: cell = size >= 2 ? size : 8 (node units*px), angle = @angle if non-zero else 45, rotated grid q = R*px/cell, cell centre sampled at the cell centre (bilinear), l = encoded luma of the straight colour, dot radius = sqrt(1 - l)*0.7071 in cell units, inside = 1 - smoothstep(rad-0.05, rad+0.05, |q - cell centre|), black dots over white paper both premultiplied by the sampled alpha, result mixed with the source by amount (clamped 0..1, default 1); vhs: per-row Gaussian jitter j = D24 gaussian(seed, frame channel, row)*0.4*6*amount/width, 3 px colour bleed (R at j+bleed, G at j, B at j-bleed through the edge mode), noise gaussian*0.018*amount*g.a, scanline factor 1 - 0.15*amount*step(0.5, fract(row*0.5)), alpha max of the three samples, amount clamped 0..2. Python's CMYK/line/square halftone shapes and YIQ vhs stay behind <param name='model'>.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/stylize.py (halftone L217, vhs L169; uses scenerender/noise.py D24 gaussian)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects_full.py::test_halftone_cmyk_preserves_primary_and_screen_shape, ::test_video_damage_seed_time_and_zero (vhs); tests/test_effects.py::test_random_effects_are_independent_of_render_order (vhs)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs halftone L1807, vhs op in the warp arm L1960-1992; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl case 74 L672-690, warp case 17 L809-831

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/screen-halftone.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with halftone size='6' angle='45' amount='1' (+ default size 8) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (12.4 dB; default 8.0)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/screen-vhs.scene.xml` at t=[0.2, 0.5, 0.9]: T1 with vhs amount='1' seed='3' (+ default) and an edgeMode='1' variant on full-frame content Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB (D24 gaussian must match exactly)** (today: DIFFERENT (15.6 dB; full-frame edgeMode 16.5)).
- Port Rust test `fs_warp case 17 and fs_color case 74 (formula references)` (crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl): vhs row jitter is d24_gaussian(seed, frame, row)*2.4*amount/width px; halftone dot radius sqrt(1-l)*0.7071 Python: numpy references in tests/fxref.py; compare per-row shifts on a vertical-bar input (vhs) and a 1-px grid of constant luma l (halftone: coverage = pi*rad^2 per cell within 5 %)
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** vhs depends on the exact D24 gaussian draw index (u32(row), frame channel) and on the target's width for the noise index (u32(d.y) + y*width + x): needs P3-FX-04 alignment. halftone grids anchor to the target origin.

#### P3-FX-20 — Sharpen, unsharp-mask, emboss  

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: FX-20 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02

**Goal.** sharpen = 3x3 Laplacian: max(s + (4s - (N+S+E+W))*amount*0.25, 0) with no @radius (amount default 1); unsharp-mask = max(s + (s - blur(radius px))*amount*m, 0) where m = 0 when luma(|s - blur|) < threshold and the schema default threshold 0.7 counts as 'no threshold' (decision: flagged sentinel); emboss = luma difference of the straight colour 0.5 + (L(uv - d) - L(uv + d))*2 with d = (cos a, sin a)*max(relief,1)*px/size, treated as an encoded value (decoded to linear), times alpha, mixed with the source by amount (clamp 0..1, default 1). bevel is in P3-FX-11.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/stylize.py (sharpen L78 registers 'sharpen' and 'unsharp-mask', emboss L256); scenerender/effects/kawase.py (optional, from P3-FX-13) for the unsharp blur  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_sharpen_increases_edge_contrast (radius=1 threshold=0 for both kinds; Rust sharpen ignores radius); tests/test_effects_full.py::UPGRADED list (emboss not pinned)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs sharpen L1994, unsharp-mask L1998-2002, emboss L2003-2011; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_conv cases 0-1 L943-955, fs_combine case 6 L255-261

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/relief-sharpen.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with sharpen amount='1' (default is identical) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (26.0 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/relief-unsharp.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with unsharp-mask radius='2' amount='1' threshold='0.1' (+ default threshold 0.7 -> no gate) Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB** (today: CLOSE (34.9 dB; default 24.9)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/relief-emboss.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with emboss angle='45' relief='2' amount='1' (+ default) Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (17.6 dB; default 17.4)).
- Port Rust test `fs_conv case 0/1 and fs_combine case 6 (formula references)` (crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl): sharpen is the 4-neighbour Laplacian * 0.25 * amount; unsharp gates on luma(|diff|) < threshold Python: numpy reference on the test card compared at <= 1e-4 (sharpen, emboss) and <= 5e-3 with the Gaussian stand-in for unsharp's Kawase blur
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** unsharp-mask's blur is Dual-Kawase in Rust; a Gaussian stand-in caps the match near 40 dB on edges. The 0.7 sentinel makes threshold=0.7 impossible to author in Rust.

#### P3-FX-21 — Chromatic aberration and RGB split with edgeMode  

Size **S** · track **P3-C** · rows: FX-21, FX-25, FX-02 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02, P3-FX-04, P3-FX-05

**Goal.** chromatic-aberration: amount = pixels of shift (times px) at the input's farthest corner from its centre, l = amount/reach, red sampled at c + (x - c)/max(1-l, 0.01), blue at c + (x-c)/max(1+l, 0.01), green unshifted, alpha = max of the three; centre = node content box centre (or centerX/Y), reach = distance from the centre to the farthest corner of the content box (frame for adjustment layers), transparent beyond the input unless edgeMode is 1 or 2. rgb-split: red sampled at uv + o, blue at uv - o (so red content moves toward -o), o = node-local (offsetX, offsetY) rotated by the node's linear part (Params.offset, defaults 8,8), amount ignored, alpha = max of the three; edgeMode 1/2 as in P3-FX-05. Python's channelScale/focus/longitudinal extras stay.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/stylize.py (rgb_split L91, chromatic_aberration L101)

#### P3-FX-22 — Analytic warps: wave-warp, ripple, twirl, spherize, bulge, lens-distortion, mirror, kaleidoscope, tile  

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: FX-23, FX-25 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02, P3-FX-04, P3-FX-05

**Goal.** Inverse-mapping warps with Rust's parameters, defaults and edge modes. wave-warp: amplitude amount*px, wavelength size > 1 ? size : 100 (px), phase = time*speed*frequency*TAU, @angle direction, displacement along its normal: o = sin(dot(p,dir)/wl*2*pi + phase)*amplitude with p in target pixels; ripple: o = sin(r/wl*2*pi - time*speed*TAU)*amplitude radially from the centre, wavelength size > 1 ? size : 40, NO radius falloff; twirl: angle = @angle else amount*90 deg, radius R = r if r > 4 px else min(w,h)/2, weight w = clamp(1 - r/R, 0, 1), rotation = angle*w^2; spherize: k = mix(r, asin(r)*2/pi, amount)/r, bulge: k = r^amount / r, inside r < 1 of radius R (default half the frame, R = radius when radius > 4 px); lens-distortion: k1 = amount*0.2 on aspect-normalised uv about 0.5 (centre not configurable): uv = 0.5 + p*(1 + k1*r^2)/aspect; mirror: line through the centre at @angle, normal n = (cos a, sin a), s = dot(p,n); the side s >= 0 is kept and s < 0 reads the reflection; kaleidoscope: segments = @levels (default 8, min 1), wedge fold with rotation @angle; tile: repeats = max(amount,1), mirrored on odd cells when size > 1, else plain fract. All warps sample bilinearly with the edge mode (P3-FX-05). Python's Brown-Conrady k1/k2/p1/p2/cylindrical model, wrap-tile and exp-bulge stay behind <param name='model'>.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/distort.py (mirror L30, kaleidoscope L41, tile L52, wave_warp L160, ripple L172, twirl L182, spherize L191, bulge L202, lens_distortion L211)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_zero_displacement_and_rotational_warps (amount=0 identity: Rust bulge amount=0 collapses the disc; spherize/wave/ripple identity holds); tests/test_effects_full.py::test_distortion_coefficients_and_cylindrical; tests/test_effects.py::test_every_effect_contract and test_modifiers.py / test_core.py (bulge there is the shape modifier, unaffected)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_warp cases 2-7 and 9-11 L711-775; crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs warp arm L1865-1993 (wave-warp L1905, ripple L1914, twirl L1925, spherize/bulge L1930, lens-distortion L1936, mirror/kaleidoscope/tile L1951-1960); crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::warp_edge_modes_preserve_full_frame_colour L202

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/warp-wave-ripple.scene.xml` at t=[0.2, 0.5, 0.9]: two scenes T2: wave-warp amount='4' size='30' and ripple amount='4' size='20' (+ defaults); times 0.2/0.5/0.9 for phase Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (wave 27.9 / adjustment 31.0, ripple 16.6)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/warp-twirl-spherize-bulge.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T2: twirl (no attributes -> 90 deg, radius half frame), spherize (default), bulge amount='1.5' radius='40' Expect **MATCH >= 50 dB** (today: twirl default DIFFERENT (10.9; explicit angle='60' radius='40' already MATCH 70.6), spherize default 9.9 (explicit 68.8), bulge DIFFERENT (22.2; default 51.6)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/warp-lens-distortion.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T2 lens-distortion amount='1' (+ amount='-1') Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (23.7 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/warp-mirror-kaleidoscope-tile.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T2: mirror angle='90'; kaleidoscope levels='6' angle='0'; tile amount='3' size='2' (+ defaults) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (mirror 8.5, kaleidoscope 15.6, tile 7.2 / 5.6)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/edgemode-warps.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: full-frame content, wave-warp amount='8' size='32' and lens-distortion amount='2', each with edgeMode 1 and 2 Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (wave 21.1, lens-distortion 16.0)).
- Port Rust test `warp_edge_modes_preserve_full_frame_colour` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): wave-warp amount=16 size=32 and lens-distortion amount=4 with edgeMode 1/2 keep corners [1,1,1,1] within 0.01 on a white full-frame adjustment Python: white 4x4 layer scale 16x8 on 64x32 transparent frame + adjustment; frame_linear corner pixels
- Eval-level: Not applicable.

**Risks.** wave/ripple phase and kaleidoscope/mirror geometry are functions of target pixel coordinates (target origin matters on layers: P3-FX-04). Rust's lens-distortion sign convention comment contradicts its mapping (see rust_may_be_wrong). bulge amount=0 is degenerate in Rust.

#### P3-FX-23 — Map and noise driven warps: displacement-map, turbulent-displace, heat-haze  

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: FX-24, FX-25 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-02, P3-FX-04, P3-FX-05

**Goal.** displacement-map: o = (map.rg - 0.5)*2*amount*px*map.alpha (straight map colour) and the sample is read at uv - o (Python reads at +o: direction reversed), map = @source node rendered over the target, no displacement where the map is transparent; turbulent-displace: q = uv*d/(size > 1 ? size : 64)/px-scaled, o = (fbm(q), fbm(q + (37,17), seed+7)) - 0.5 with 6 octaves and evolution time*speed, uv += o*2*amount*px/d; heat-haze: amount*4*px, cell size > 1 ? size : 24, q2 = q - (0, time*speed) (rising cells), uv += o*amount4/d; fbm = D24 basic fractal (octave weights 0.5^i, base seed + 101 i + 7919 phase, smoothstep cross-fade); bilinear sample with the edge mode. Python's noiseType/octaves/pinning/displacement-mode/channel-selection stay under <param>.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/distort.py (displacement_map L65, _noise_warp L87, turbulent_displace L150, heat_haze L155); scenerender/effects/fields.py (fractal L16; verify equality with d24_fbm)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects_full.py::test_displacement_neutral_and_nested_plate, ::test_fractals_evolve_and_types_differ; tests/test_effects.py::test_source_node_inputs_and_offsets, ::test_recursive_source_is_guarded, ::test_zero_displacement_and_rotational_warps (turbulent/heat-haze identity at amount=0); tests/test_reference_pullback.py, tests/test_render_speed.py (turbulent-displace, heat-haze)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_warp cases 0, 1, 8 L701-710 and L749-754, d24_fbm L145-160; crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs displacement-map L1893, turbulent-displace L1900, heat-haze L1943; crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs::effect_source L724-735 (source must name a node)

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/warp-displacement-map.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 plus hidden <layer id='map' asset='tc' x='0' y='0' visible='false'/> and displacement-map source='map' amount='6' (T2 variant guards the neutral case) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (14.1 dB; direction reversed)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/warp-turbulent.scene.xml` at t=[0.2, 0.5, 0.9]: T1 with turbulent-displace amount='6' size='30' seed='3' (+ default) Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB** (today: CLOSE (37.9 dB; default 27.1)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/warp-heat-haze.scene.xml` at t=[0.2, 0.5, 0.9]: T1 with heat-haze amount='2' size='20' seed='3' (+ default) Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (22.7 dB; default 26.3)).
- Port Rust test `fs_warp case 0 (formula reference)` (crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl): a 0.5-grey opaque map gives zero displacement; red 1.0 shifts the sample position left by 2*amount px (reads uv - o) Python: extend tests/test_effects_full.py::test_displacement_neutral_and_nested_plate with the sign: a map with red=1 and amount=2 moves content by -4 px... (measure with the Rust binary on the P3-FX-01 probe and hard-code the sign)
- Eval-level: eval elements[].props returns animated effect parameters (amount, size, speed) for time-driven warps: compare Python's p.n(...) with Rust's elements props at t in {0.2,0.5,0.9}, tolerance 1e-9.

**Risks.** Needs D24 fbm bit-compatibility (check fields.fractal against d24_fbm first, same as P3-FX-14); target alignment for q = uv*d/size (P3-FX-04). Source nodes must be nodes, not assets.

#### P3-FX-24 — Generators: fill and fractal-noise  

Size **S** · track **P3-C** · rows: FX-18, FX-12 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-04

**Goal.** fill: replaces the straight colour with @color (default (1,0.5,0.2,1)) by mix(c, colour, colour.alpha), pixel alpha unchanged (Python multiplied alpha by the colour's alpha and defaulted to white); the Python paint fill (@paint) stays as an extra. fractal-noise: n = fbm(pixel/max(scale,1)*frequency, D24 seed, 6 octaves, evolution = time*speed)*intensity with scale = size > 1 ? size : 64 (node units*px), grey result (n,n,n,1) mixed over the source by clamp(amount,0,1) (default 1) on the whole-frame target; no colour, contrast/brightness, noiseType or octaves (those Python params stay behind <param>).

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/generate.py (fill L12, fractal_noise L29); scenerender/effects/fields.py (fractal, only if d24_fbm equality needs a fix; fields.py is owned by this group)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects.py::test_fill_paint_scale_and_alpha, ::test_colour_and_radius_animation (fill colour animation); tests/test_effects_full.py::test_fractals_evolve_and_types_differ; tests/test_assets.py (fractal-noise generator asset, unaffected)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs tint/tritone/color-overlay/fill arm L1635-1652 (fill amount = colour alpha), fractal-noise L1782; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl case 70 L640-644, d24_fbm L145, grade case 18 L451; crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs UNBOUNDED (fractal-noise) L84-102

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/gen-fill.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: T1 with fill color='#FF8000FF' (guard) and a no-attribute default (orange 1,0.5,0.2) Expect **MATCH >= 60 dB** (today: explicit MATCH (99.0 dB); default DIFFERENT (13.4 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/gen-fractal-noise.scene.xml` at t=[0.2, 0.5, 0.9]: T2 (whole-frame coverage like Rust) with fractal-noise size='20' intensity='1' amount='0.8' seed='3' (+ default size 64) Expect **CLOSE >= 40 dB (MATCH >= 45 when fbm is bit-identical)** (today: DIFFERENT (24.0 dB T2; 10.1 on a T1 layer)).
- Port Rust test `every_effect_type_draws` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): fill and fractal-noise draw finite pixels Python: covered by P3-FX-01; plus an fbm value test: Python fields.fractal at lattice points equals d24_fbm(p, seed, 6, 0) from a numpy port of the WGSL (tests/fxref.py)
- Eval-level: Not applicable (animated @seed/@speed values can be compared with elements[].props).

**Risks.** A fractal-noise effect on a layer node fills the whole frame in Rust (flagged), so the layer-node probe differs structurally until P3-FX-04 makes Python's target the frame. fill with @paint has no Rust counterpart; keep it.

#### P3-FX-25 — Time effects: echo, posterize-time, pixel-motion-blur  

Size **M** · track **P3-C** · rows: FX-28, FX-29 · needs schema adoption: no · depends on: P3-FX-01, P3-FX-04

**Goal.** echo: @samples copies (min(samples,16), at least 2), @amount frames between copies (default 1, delay amount/fps), @intensity decay per copy (default 1 = equal weights, clamped 0..1), weights decay^k normalised to sum 1 and accumulated ADDITIVELY (not 'over'; the newest copy contributes weight w0, so the leading edge of a moving layer is only partly opaque, as Rust), earlier frames are the node re-rendered pre-opacity at t - k*delay in the current target space; Python's interval/decay/operator/frequency model stays behind <param name='model'>. posterize-time already holds the node at floor(t*frequency + 1e-9)/frequency (guard, MATCH). pixel-motion-blur: shutter = amount*0.5 frames (default amount 1), samples (<= 64 taps, default 16), optical flow between the node's render at t - 1/fps and at t (dense, not rigid), each pixel blurred along its flow vector f (pixels per frame): mean_k S(uv + f*((k+0.5)/n - 0.5)*shutter/d); Python's rigid matrix trajectory becomes the fallback when flow cannot be computed (use scenerender/effects/fields.optical_flow(a, b, max_size=384, iterations=3, window=9)). Chain order is document order in Python (Rust hoists echo/posterize-time to the front: flagged, not copied). Python's ghost tail after the node's window (echo.ghost_active) is an extra with no Rust counterpart: keep and document.

**Python files.** scenerender/effects/stylize.py (echo L267, posterize_time L306, _echo_lookback L330, _echo_ghost_active L337); scenerender/effects/blur.py (pixel_motion_blur L178); scenerender/effects/fields.py (optical_flow L119, flow_warp L169)  
**Existing tests to migrate.** tests/test_effects_full.py::test_echo_true_earlier_positions_and_decay, ::test_posterize_holds_pixels_rotation_and_color, ::test_posterize_time_holds_child_temporal_randomness, ::test_motion_blur_rotation_and_parent_scale; tests/test_effects.py::test_temporal_approximations_use_evaluated_node_translation; tests/test_temporal_clocks.py (echo lookback/ghost, posterize clocks)

**Rust reference.** crates/sr-gpu/src/render_fx.rs run_chain posterize-time/echo L996-1057 (weights L1013-1030), pixel-motion-blur L1081-1112, posterized() L132-135, node_effects shown-times L843-870; crates/sr-gpu/src/fx.rs flow_blur L2191, accumulate L2172, pixel-motion-blur wiring; crates/sr-gpu/src/effects.wgsl fs_flow L1169-1181, crates/sr-gpu/src/flow.wgsl (DIS optical flow); crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs::time_effects_use_other_frames L516; crates/sr-gpu/tests/effect_costs.rs::echoed_layers_keep_their_effect_targets_to_the_trail L50, ::posterized_layers_keep_their_effect_targets_to_the_layer L31

**Acceptance gate.**

- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/time-echo.scene.xml` at t=[0.5, 0.8]: T1 with the layer's x animated 6->26 over 1 s and <effect type='echo' samples='4' amount='2' intensity='0.6'/> (+ default) Expect **MATCH >= 45 dB** (today: DIFFERENT (18.5 dB; default 14.1)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/time-posterize.scene.xml` at t=[0.5, 0.7]: same moving layer with posterize-time frequency='2' (guard) Expect **MATCH >= 60 dB** (today: MATCH (99.0 dB)).
- Parity scene (NEW) `tools/parity/scenes/fx/time-pixel-motion-blur.scene.xml` at t=[0.5]: moving layer with pixel-motion-blur amount='1' samples='8' (+ a deforming variant: scaleX animated) Expect **CLOSE >= 38 dB (flow algorithms differ)** (today: CLOSE (35.4 dB; Python integrates amount frames, Rust amount/2)).
- Parity scene `tools/evidence/scenes/review-title.scene.xml (existing rs corpus)` at t=[0.1, 0.5, 0.9]: existing corpus scene using posterize-time frequency=12; regression guard for the guard Expect **CLOSE >= 39 dB (no regression)** (today: CLOSE (39.1, 46.4, 46.4 dB at 0.292/1.5/2.708 s per RENDER-BASELINE)).
- Port Rust test `time_effects_use_other_frames` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effects.rs): white 4x4 layer scale 4 at (8,8), x 0->40 over 4 s on 64x32 fps 10: posterize-time frequency=2 at t=0.7 draws the square as at t=0.5 (spans 20..36: (21,16) white, alpha(38,16) < 0.05); echo samples=4 amount=2 at t=0.8: alpha at (18,16) strictly between 0.1 and 0.9; pixel-motion-blur amount=1 samples=8 draws without problems Python: frame_linear(0.7) / (0.8) / (0.5) with the same documents (keys 0->0, 4->40: speed 10 px/s)
- Port Rust test `echoed_layers_keep_their_effect_targets_to_the_trail` (crates/sr-gpu/tests/effect_costs.rs): 256x144 fps 60, white square scale 8 moving 120 px/s from x=40, echo samples=3 amount=2 at t=0.5 (now x=100, echoes at 96 and 92): alpha at (94,66) in (0.1, 0.9), analytically 1/3 Python: frame_linear(0.5)[66, 94, 3]; assert ~ 1/3 within 0.02 (equal normalised weights)
- Eval-level: eval nodes[i].local_time / source_time at t - k*amount/fps checks which times echo samples (Rust shows t, t-delay, ...); compare Python's sample times for samples=4 amount=2 fps=10: {0.8, 0.6, 0.4, 0.2}; posterize time floor(t*f+1e-9)/f; tolerance 1e-9.

**Risks.** Optical flow cannot match Rust's DIS/GPU result beyond CLOSE; the rigid fallback matches only for rigid motion. echo is the largest visible semantic gap: Rust's normalised additive weights make the newest copy only 1/count opaque (see rust_may_be_wrong; decision before implementing). Python's ghost tail will not exist in Rust.

## Deferred rows

| Row | Area | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| EVAL-01 (remainder) | EVAL | The architectural part (compile once per variant/layout into a Program of slots and bytecode, then a pure Evaluator::evaluate) is deferred: pixel parity does not need it and Python deliberately evaluates lazily so expressions can drive windows, durations and layout. What is delivered instead: the FrameGraph walker (P2-EVAL-01), the order-independence test, the static compile pass and memoisation (P2-EVAL-13). A later refactor can make render_children consume the walker's iterator. |
| EVAL-37 (geo, geoVisible only) | EVAL | Needs map assets and geoAsset/mapAsset schema types absent from the Python XSD (schema_delta lists mapAssetType, geoAssetType); belongs to the map/3D area after Bob's schema adoption. Everything else of EVAL-37 is in P2-EVAL-12. |
| EVAL-40 | EVAL | Bit-identical transcendental functions: DIVERGENT-by-design. Measured Python math vs Rust libm over 120 samples each: sin/cos/exp/pow/log/tan/atan2/cbrt differ in at most 1 ulp (max relative 2.4e-16, up to 59/120 samples for cbrt). Bit equality would need a pure-Python port of the musl libm routines (L effort, slow in the hot path) for no visible effect. Recommended contract: relative 1e-15 in eval-level gates. Revisit only if cross-machine bit determinism of Python renders becomes a requirement. |
| EVAL-41 (emitter, physics, deform, modifier seeds) | EVAL | Cross-area decision: sim.rs seeds emitters as hash_str(id) ^ project seed while Python (and its tests/test_particle_evaluation.py pins) use CRC-32 'project:id:@seed:purpose'. P2-EVAL-19 provides the primitive and node/copy seeds; the switch for simulations belongs to the SIM/FX packages. |
| EVAL-02 (--bench budget) | EVAL | The 2 ms/frame budget of crates/sr-eval/tests/perf.rs is not reachable in Python; P2-EVAL-03 only reports ms/frame and guards against regressions. |
| COMP-23 | COMP | Replacing cairo 8-bit vector rasterisation with a float rasteriser is an XL architecture change (cairo/Pango vs wgpu), not a behaviour that probes show: shapes and blends already agree (blend_grid 57.7 dB, minimal/ocean scenes 99 dB MATCH). Only pending consequence is 1-code-value quantisation in dark vector content; revisit if measured gaps appear after packages 02-17. |
| COMP-27 | COMP | Python is a superset (honours per-point x/y which the schema defines) and already agrees on regular grids (mesh probe 41.2 dB, max 19); Rust ignores x/y (paint.rs MeshGradient arm). Copying Rust would remove a schema-defined capability: coordinator decision, flagged in rust_may_be_wrong. oklch mesh in Python vs Rust enum (linear/srgb/oklab only, also the XSD) needs Bob's validation, not rendering work. |
| COMP-29 | COMP | Both dithers are deterministic and zero-mean; measured gradient with dither=true is 53.7 dB, max 1 code value, mean difference 0.00. Matching Rust's hash2 noise bit-for-bit requires the float rasteriser (COMP-23) because Python quantises in 8-bit cairo surfaces before the noise could be added. |
| COMP-40 | COMP | Architecture: headless wgpu single-command-buffer frame graph versus numpy/cairo CPU compositor with optional moderngl tiles. DIVERGENT by design (cairo/Pango vs wgpu); every observable consequence is covered by packages 02-23. gpucomp.py must simply continue to mirror the CPU semantics for the modes it accelerates (normal, behind). |
| FX-40 | FX | tracked as CLI-12/13/14 (project@quality and --quality tiers: draft half-size targets, caps of 2 motion-blur samples, 4 echo copies, 8 line samples, no grain). Once the CLI rows land, the per-effect caps hook into P3-FX-06 (motion-blur samples), P3-FX-25 (echo copies), P3-FX-17 (line samples) and the grain/noise skip; no FX package duplicates the flag work. |
| FX-R01 | FX | tracked as CLI-12/13/14 (render --changed-only fingerprinting, watch, --stats effect_pixels/textures_created); not an effect or colour semantic. |
| FX-43 (fusion of consecutive pointwise colour ops, texture pool 8 frames, cache entries 4 frames, --stats counters) | FX | performance-only with no output impact; Python is a CPU/numpy renderer with its own raster and effect-result caches and optional GL kernels. The output-affecting part of the row (effect targets sized to coverage, the 32 px rounding) is covered by P3-FX-04; --stats reporting is tracked under the CLI rows. |
| FX-42 (mechanism: ociobakelut 129^3 bake in ACEScct, ocio:// built-in configs, fallback messages) | FX | detail belongs to the asset-interop-and-colour area (the matrix says so); Python runs PyOpenColorIO in process, which is DIVERGENT-by-design and arguably better. Only the exposure/look ordering of the OCIO path is covered here (P3-FX-10). |
| FX-13 (aces2 uses the ACES fit in Rust) | FX | documented limitation of Rust (README Limitations); Python's OCIO ACES 2.0 is more accurate. Parity implementation in P3-FX-09 offers the Rust fit as the parity-profile default and keeps the exact transform as opt-in; the default is a user decision. |
